## V / 第五章

===Original===
A huge silver monoplane glided gracefully to the surface of Mines Field in Los Angeles and came to a pretty stop after a short run. A liveried footman stepped out of the forward compartment armed with a stool which he placed under the rear door. Simultaneously a high-powered foreign car swept up close to the airplane and waited. The rear door of the airplane opened, and to the apparent surprise of the nearby mechanics a tall, black, distinguished-looking Negro stepped out and down to the ground, assisted by the hand of the footman. Behind him came a pale young man and woman, evidently secretaries. The three entered the limousine which rapidly drove off.

“Who’s that coon?” asked one of the mechanics, round-eyed and respectful, like all Americans, in the presence of great wealth.

“Don’t you know who that is?” inquired another, pityingly. “Why that’s that Dr. Crookman. You know, the fellow what’s turnin’ niggers white. See that BNM on the side of his plane? That stands for Black-No-More. Gee, but I wish I had just half the jack he’s made in the last six months!”

“Why I thought from readin’ th’ papers,” protested the first speaker, “that th’ law had closed up his places and put ’im outta business.”

“Oh, that’s a lotta hockey,” said the other fellow. “Why just yesterday th’ newspapers said that Black-No-More was openin’ a place on Central Avenue. They already got one in Oakland, so a coon told me yesterday.”

===Chinese===
一架银色巨型单翼飞机姿态优雅地滑向洛杉矶迈恩斯机场的地面，短距滑行之后利落停稳。一名身着号衣的男仆从前舱走出，手里拿着一张小凳，摆到后舱门下。与此同时，一辆大马力外国轿车飞速驶近飞机，停在一旁等候。飞机后舱门打开，令附近的机械师们大感意外的是，一个身材高大、皮肤黝黑、气度不凡的黑人由男仆搀着手走下飞机。他身后跟着一男一女两个面色苍白的年轻人，显然是秘书。三人钻进豪华轿车，一溜烟开走了。

「那黑鬼是谁？」一个机械师问道，眼睛瞪得溜圆，满脸敬意——跟所有美国人一样，一见到大把的钱就这副德行。

「你不知道那是谁？」另一个可怜他的无知，「嗐，那就是克鲁克曼大夫啊。就是那个把黑鬼变白的家伙。瞧见他机身侧面那BNM字样没？那是『黑色终结』（Black-No-More）的缩写。老天，他这半年捞的钱，哪怕给我一小半儿也好啊！」

「咦，我读报纸还当，」头一个不服气，「官府早把他那些地方全封了，叫他做不成买卖了呢。」

「嗐，那全是胡扯，」另一个说，「就在昨天，报纸上还登着『黑色终结』要在中央大道开一家新店呢。他们在奥克兰已经开了一家，这是个黑鬼昨天告诉我的。」

===Original===
“’Sfunny,” ventured a third mechanic, as they wheeled the big plane into a nearby hangar, “how he don’t have nuthin’ but white folks around him. He must not like nigger help. His chauffeur’s white, his footman’s white an’ that young gal and feller what was with him are white.”

“How do you know?” challenged the first speaker. “They may be darkies that he’s turned into white folks.”

“That’s right,” the other replied. “It’s gittin’ so yuh can’t tell who’s who. I think that there Knights of Nordica ought to do something about it. I joined up with ’em two months ago but they ain’t done nuthin’ but sell me an ole uniform an’ hold a coupla meetin’s.”

They lapsed into silence. Sandol, the erstwhile Senegalese, stepped from the cockpit grinning. “Ah, zese Americains,” he muttered to himself as he went over the engine, examining everything minutely.

“Where’d yuh come from, buddy?” asked one of the mechanics.

===Chinese===
「真逗，」第三个机械师插嘴，这时他们正把大飞机推进旁边的机库，「他身边愣是一个黑人都没有，全是白人。他八成不喜欢黑鬼帮佣。他的司机是白人，男仆是白人，跟着他的那个小妞和小伙子也都是白人。」

「你怎么知道？」头一个顶了回去，「没准是些让他变白了的黑崽子呢。」

「这话在理，」那位答道，「如今这世道，谁都分不清谁是谁了。要我说，那个北欧骑士团（Knights of Nordica）就该出来管管这事。俩月前我入了他们的伙，可他们除了卖给我一身旧制服、开过两三回会，屁事没干。」

他们不再作声。昔日的塞内加尔人桑多尔（Sandol）咧着嘴从驾驶舱里走出来。「唉，这些美利坚人哪，」他一面喃喃自语，一面检查引擎，把每个部件都细细摸了一遍。

「老兄，你们打哪儿飞来的？」一个机械师问。

===Original===
“Den-vair,” Sandol replied.

“Whatcha doin’, makin’ a trip around th’ country?” queried another.

“Yes, we air, what you callem, on ze tour inspectione,” the aviator continued. They could think of no more to say and soon strolled off.

---

===Chinese===
「丹——佛，」桑多尔答道。

「你们这是干啥呢，环国旅行？」另一个问。

「是啊，我们在，你们叫什么来着，搞那个『视察之旅』呐，」这位飞行员接着说。他们想不出还有什么可问的，不多会儿便溜达开了。

---

===Original===
Around an oval table on the seventh floor of a building on Central Avenue, sat Dr. Junius Crookman, Hank Johnson, Chuck Foster, Ranford the Doctor’s secretary and four other men. At the lower end of the table Miss Bennett, Ranford’s stenographer, was taking notes. A soft-treading waiter whose Negro nature was only revealed by his mocking obsequiousness, served each with champagne.

“To our continued success!” cried the physician, lifting his glass high.

“To our continued success!” echoed the others.

They drained their glasses, and returned them to the polished surface of the table.

“Dog bite it, Doc!” blurted Johnson. “Us sho is doin’ fine. Ain’t had a bad break since we stahted, an’ heah ’tis th’ fust o’ September.”

===Chinese===
在中央大道一栋楼的七层，朱尼厄斯·克鲁克曼医生、汉克·约翰逊、查克·福斯特、医生的秘书兰福德（Ranford），外加另外四个人，正围着一张椭圆桌坐着。桌子下首，兰福德的速记员贝内特小姐在做记录。一个走路悄没声儿的侍者给每人斟上香槟——只有他那份带着嘲弄意味的谄媚劲儿，才泄露了他黑人的身份。

「为我们接连不断的成功干杯！」医生高举酒杯喊道。

「为我们接连不断的成功干杯！」众人齐声应和。

他们一饮而尽，把杯子放回光洁的桌面上。

「见鬼去吧，老克！」约翰逊脱口而出，「咱们可真是顺水顺风。打从开张起就没栽过一个跟头，这不，都到九月一号了。」

===Original===
“Don’t holler too soon,” cautioned Foster. “The opposition is growing keener every day. I had to pay seventy-five thousand dollars more for this building than it’s worth.”

“Well, yuh got it, didn’t yuh?” asked Johnson. “Just like Ah allus say: when yuh got money yuh kin git anything in this man’s country. Whenever things look tight jes pull out th’ ole check book an’ eve’ything’s all right.”

“Optimist!” grunted Foster.

“I ain’t no pess’mist,” Johnson accused.

“Now gentlemen,” Dr. Crookman interrupted, clearing his throat, “let’s get down to business. We have met here, as you know, not only for the purpose of celebrating the opening of this, our fiftieth sanitarium, but also to take stock of our situation. I have before me here a detailed report of our business affairs for the entire period of seven months and a half that we’ve been in operation.

===Chinese===
「别高兴得太早，」福斯特告诫道，「反对的势力一天比一天凶。这栋楼我多付了七万五千块钱，比它本身值的还多。」

「可房子你不还是到手了嘛，是不是？」约翰逊说，「我一贯那句话：只要手里有钱，在这个爷们儿的国度里啥都能弄到手。事情一紧，就把那本老支票簿掏出来，天下太平。」

「乐天派！」福斯特哼了一声。

「我才不是什么悲观派呢。」约翰逊抗议道。

「好了，诸位，」克鲁克曼医生清了清嗓子，打断他们，「谈正事吧。诸位都知道，咱们今天聚在这儿，不光是为了庆祝我们第五十家疗养院开张，也是要盘点一下咱们的处境。我手头有一份详尽的营业报告，涵盖我们开业七个半月以来的全部业务。

===Original===
“During that time we have put into service fifty sanitariums from Coast to Coast, or an average of one every four and one-half days, the average capacity of each sanitarium being one hundred and five patients. Each place has a staff of six physicians and twenty-four nurses, a janitor, four orderlies, two electricians, bookkeeper, cashier, stenographer and record clerk, not counting four guards.

“For the past four months we have had an equipment factory in Pittsburgh in full operation and a chemical plant in Philadelphia. In addition to this we have purchased four airplanes and a radio broadcasting station. Our expenditures for real estate, salaries and chemicals have totaled $6,255,085.10.”

“He! He!” chuckled Johnson. “Dat ten cents mus’ be fo’ one o’ them bad ceegars that Fostah smokes.”

“Our total income,” continued Dr. Crookman, frowning slightly at the interruption, “has been $18,500,300, or 370,006 patients at fifty dollars apiece. I think that vindicates my contention at the beginning that the fee should be but fifty dollars—within the reach of the rank and file of Negroes.” He laid aside his report and added:

“In the next four months we’ll double our output and by the end of the year we should cut the fee to twenty-five dollars,” he lightly twirled his waxed mustache between his long sensitive fingers and smiled with satisfaction.

===Chinese===
「这段时间里，我们从东海岸到西海岸一共开张了五十家疗养院，平均每四天半开一家，每家平均收容一百零五名病人。每处配备六名医生、二十四名护士、一名门房、四名护理员、两名电工、一名簿记员、一名出纳、一名速记员和一名档案员，这还没算四名警卫。

「过去四个月里，我们在匹兹堡的设备工厂全面开工，在费城还有一座化工厂。除此之外，我们购进了四架飞机和一座无线电广播电台。我们在房地产、薪水以及化学品上的开支共计6,255,085.10美元。」

「嘿！嘿！」约翰逊窃笑，「那十分钱，准是替福斯特抽的哪根劣质雪茄付的账。」

「我们的总收入，」克鲁克曼医生对这打岔微微皱了皱眉，接着说，「已达18,500,300美元，即370,006名病人，每人五十块。我想这证明了我当初的主张没错：收费就该只要五十块——让黑人普通大众都掏得起。」他放下报告，又补充道：

「接下来四个月，我们要把产量翻一番，到年底，收费就该降到二十五块了。」他用细长灵敏的手指轻轻捻着涂了蜡的胡子，露出心满意足的微笑。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Foster, “the sooner we get this business over with the better. We’re going to run into a whole lot more opposition from now on than we have so far encountered.”

“Why man!” growled Johnson, “we ain’t even stahted on dese darkies yet. And when we git thu wi’ dese heah, we kin work on them in th’ West Indies. Believe me, Ah doan *nevah* want dis graft tuh end.”

“Now,” continued Dr. Crookman, “I want to say that Mr. Foster deserves great praise for the industry and ingenuity he has shown in purchasing our real estate and Mr. Johnson deserves equally great praise for the efficient manner in which he has kept down the opposition of the various city officials. As you know, he has spent nearly a million dollars in such endeavors and almost as much again in molding legislative sentiment in Washington and the various state capitals. That accounts for the fact that every bill introduced in a legislature or municipal council to put us out of business has died in committee. Moreover, through his corps of secret operatives, who are mostly young women, he has placed numbers of officials and legislators in a position where they cannot openly oppose our efforts.”

A smile of appreciation went around the circle.

“We’ll have a whole lot to do from now on,” commented Foster.

===Chinese===
「不错，」福斯特说，「这桩买卖咱们干完得越快越好。从今往后，碰到的反对只会比迄今遇到的多得多。」

「我的老天！」约翰逊吼道，「这帮黑崽子咱们还没开始下手呢。等把这边这批拾掇完，还能去西印度群岛接着干。信我一句，这票捞钱的买卖，我*永远*不想让它到头。」

「现在，」克鲁克曼医生接着说，「我要说，福斯特先生在购置房地产上表现出的勤勉和巧思值得大大嘉奖；约翰逊先生压制各市政官员反对之高效，同样值得大大嘉奖。诸位知道，他在这类事情上花了近一百万美元，在华盛顿和各州首府笼络立法界舆论又花了差不多的数目。所以，每一份提交到州议会或市议会、想叫我们关门的法案，全都死在了委员会里。不仅如此，他还靠他那支主要由年轻姑娘组成的秘密特工队伍，把不少官员和议员弄到了没法公开反对我们事业的地步。」

一圈人绽出了会心的笑容。

「从今往后，咱们有得忙了。」福斯特评了一句。

===Original===
“Yeh, Big Boy,” replied the ex-gambler, “an’ whut it takes tuh do it Ah ain’t got nuthin’ else but!”

“Certainly,” said the physician, “our friend Hank has not been overburdened with scruples.”

“Ah doan know whut dat is, Chief,” grinned Johnson, “but Ah knows whut a check book’ll do. Even these crackers tone down when Ah talks bucks.”

“This afternoon,” continued Crookman, “we also have with us our three regional directors, Doctors Henry Dogan, Charles Hinckle and Fred Selden, as well as our chief chemist, Wallace Butts. I thought it would be a good idea to bring you all together for this occasion so we could get better acquainted. We’ll just have a word from each of them. They’re all good Race men, you know, even if they have, like the rest of our staff, taken the treatment.”

For the next three-quarters of an hour the three directors and the chief chemist reported on the progress of their work. At intervals the waiter brought in cold drinks, cigars and cigarettes. Overhead whirred the electric fans. Out of the wide open windows could be seen the panorama of bungalows, pavements, palm trees, trundling street cars and scooting automobiles.

“Lawd! Lawd! Lawd!” Johnson exclaimed at the conclusion of the meeting, going to the window and gazing out over the city. “Jes gimme a coupla yeahs o’ dis graft an’ Ah’ll make Henry Foahd look like a tramp.”

---

===Chinese===
「可不，老大哥，」这位昔日赌徒答道，「干这行缺的那味料，我别的没有，就光剩它了！」

「的确，」医生说，「咱们这位汉克朋友，良心上的负担可从来不重。」

「老大，您说的那个是啥，我不懂，」约翰逊咧嘴一笑，「可我懂支票簿的厉害。就连那些南方白佬，一听我谈钞票，也立马矮半截。」

「今天下午，」克鲁克曼接着说，「我们还有三位区域主管在场——亨利·多根医生、查尔斯·欣克尔医生和弗雷德·塞尔登医生——以及我们的首席化学师华莱士·巴茨。我想借此机会把大家聚到一块，彼此多熟识熟识，是个好主意。就请每位简单讲几句。你们晓得，他们都是为种族办事的好手（Race men），虽说他们跟我们其余员工一样，也都做过那个变白疗法。」

接下来的三刻钟里，三位主管和首席化学师逐一汇报了各自工作的进展。侍者不时端进冷饮、雪茄和香烟。头顶上电扇嗡嗡转着。从敞开的大窗望出去，平房、人行道、棕榈树、铛铛驶过的街车和飞驰的汽车尽收眼底。

「主啊！主啊！主啊！」散会时约翰逊感叹道，走到窗前眺望全城。「这票买卖再让我捞上两年，我就叫亨利·福特看着像个叫花子。」

---

===Original===
Meanwhile, Negro society was in turmoil and chaos. The colored folk in straining every nerve to get the Black-No-More treatment, had forgotten all loyalties, affiliations and responsibilities. No longer did they flock to the churches on Sundays or pay dues in their numerous fraternal organizations. They had stopped giving anything to the Anti-Lynching campaign. Santop Licorice, head of the once-flourishing Back-To-Africa Society, was daily raising his stentorian voice in denunciation of the race for deserting his organization.

Negro business was being no less hard hit. Few people were bothering about getting their hair straightened or skin whitened temporarily when for a couple of weeks’ pay they could get both jobs done permanently. The immediate result of this change of mind on the part of the Negro public was to almost bankrupt the firms that made the whitening and straightening chemicals. They were largely controlled by canny Hebrews, but at least a half-dozen were owned by Negroes. The rapid decline in this business greatly decreased the revenue of the Negro weekly newspapers who depended upon such advertising for their sustenance. The actual business of hair straightening that had furnished employment to thousands of colored women who would otherwise have had to go back to washing and ironing, declined to such an extent that “To Rent” signs hung in front of nine-tenths of the shops.

The Negro politicians in the various Black Belts, grown fat and sleek “protecting” vice with the aid of Negro votes which they were able to control by virtue of housing segregation, lectured in vain about black solidarity, race pride and political emancipation; but nothing stopped the exodus to the white race. Gloomily the politicians sat in their offices, wondering whether to throw up the sponge and hunt the nearest Black-No-More sanitarium or hold on a little longer in the hope that the whites might put a stop to the activities of Dr. Crookman and his associates. The latter, indeed, was their only hope because the bulk of Negroes, saving their dimes and dollars for chromatic emancipation, had stopped gambling, patronizing houses of prostitution or staging Saturday-night brawls. Thus the usual sources of graft vanished. The black politicians appealed to their white masters for succor, of course, but they found to their dismay that most of the latter had been safely bribed by the astute Hank Johnson.

Gone was the almost European atmosphere of every Negro ghetto: the music, laughter, gaiety, jesting and abandon. Instead, one noted the same excited bustle, wild looks and strained faces to be seen in a war time soldier camp, around a new oil district or before a gold rush. The happy-go-lucky Negro of song and story was gone forever and in his stead was a nervous, money-grubbing black, stuffing away coin in socks, impatiently awaiting a sufficient sum to pay Dr. Crookman’s fee.

Up from the South they came in increasing droves, besieging the Black-No-More sanitariums for treatment. There were none of these havens in the South because of the hostility of the bulk of white people but there were many all along the border between the two sections, at such places as Washington, DC, Baltimore, Cincinnati, Louisville, Evansville, Cairo, St. Louis and Denver. The various Southern communities attempted to stem this, the greatest migration of Negroes in the history of the country, but without avail. By train, boat, wagon, bicycle, automobile and foot they trekked to the promised land; a hopeful procession, filtering through the outposts of police and Knights of Nordica volunteer bands. Where there was great opposition to the Negroes’ going, there would suddenly appear large quantities of free bootleg liquor and crisp new currency which would make the most vigilant white opponent of Black-No-More turn his head the other way. Hank Johnson seemed to be able to cope with almost every situation.

---

===Chinese===
与此同时，黑人社会一片动荡混乱。有色人种拼了命地想弄到「黑色终结」疗法，把一切忠诚、归属和责任忘了个精光。礼拜天他们不再涌向教堂，也不再给林林总总的互助会交会费。反私刑运动他们一分钱也不捐了。桑托普·利科里斯（Santop Licorice）——一度兴旺的回非洲运动协会（Back-to-Africa Society）的头头——每天扯着他那洪钟般的嗓子，痛斥本族同胞抛弃了他的组织。

黑人生意挨的闷棍也不轻。既然掏上两个星期的工钱就能把拉直头发、漂白皮肤这两桩事一劳永逸地办妥，谁还费那个劲去搞什么一次性拉发、临时美白？黑人公众这一转念，直接后果就是让生产增白拉发药水的公司几乎破了产。这些公司大半把持在精明的希伯来人手里，但至少有半打是黑人自家的。这行当急速衰落，又大大削减了黑人周报的收入——它们全指望着这类广告糊口。至于拉发这门手艺活儿，原本给成千上万有色妇女提供了饭碗，免得她们回去干洗衣烫衣的营生，如今也萧条到十家铺子有九家挂出了「铺面招租」的牌子。

各黑人区（Black Belt）里那些靠住房隔离才攥得住黑人选票、靠「保护」非法勾当养得脑满肠肥的黑人政客，枉自宣讲什么黑人大团结、种族自豪感和政治解放；可什么也拦不住这场投奔白种人的大出走。政客们阴郁地坐在办公室里盘算：是认输服软、直奔最近的「黑色终结」疗养院呢，还是再撑一阵，指白人出面叫停克鲁克曼医生一伙的营生。后者确实是他们唯一的指望，因为广大黑人同胞正一个钢镚一个钢镚地攒钱，预备换取色彩解放，连赌也不赌了，窑子也不逛了，周六夜里的群架也不打了。往常那些油水来路，就此断绝。黑人政客们当然向他们的白人主子求救，却沮丧地发现：主子们大半已被精明的汉克·约翰逊妥妥帖帖地买通了。

每一处黑人聚居区那种近乎欧洲的风情——音乐、笑声、欢愉、打趣和无拘无束——一去不返。取而代之的，是战时兵营、新油田或淘金热前夜常见的那股亢奋忙乱、狂野眼神和绷紧的面孔。歌谣与故事里那个乐天知命的黑人永远地消失了，顶替他的是一个神经兮兮、见钱眼开的黑家伙，把钢镚儿往袜筒里塞，急不可耐地攒够付给克鲁克曼医生的那笔钱。

他们成群结队地从南方涌来，阵容一天比一天浩大，把「黑色终结」疗养院围了个水泄不通，只求做上手术。南方一家这样的避风港都没有，因为大多数白人敌意太深；但在南北两大区域的交界线上却星罗棋布——华盛顿特区、巴尔的摩、辛辛那提、路易斯维尔、埃文斯维尔、开罗、圣路易斯、丹佛，都是这样的地方。南方各城镇都想堵住这场美国历史上规模最大的黑人迁徙，却毫无用处。他们坐火车、搭轮船、赶马车、骑自行车、开汽车、靠两条腿，浩浩荡荡奔向应许之地；一列满怀希望的队伍，从警察和北欧骑士团志愿队的封锁哨卡间渗透而过。凡是对黑人出走阻挠得凶的地方，总会有大批免费的私酿酒和脆生生的崭新钞票凭空出现，叫最警觉的「黑色终结」白人反对派也把头扭向另一边。汉克·约翰逊似乎应付得了几乎任何局面。

---

===Original===
The national office of the militant Negro organization, the National Social Equality League, was agog. Telephone bells were ringing, mulatto clerks were hustling excitedly back and forth, messenger boys rushed in and out. Located in the Times Square district of Manhattan, it had for forty years carried on the fight for full social equality for the Negro citizens and the immediate abolition of lynching as a national sport. While this organization had to depend to a large extent upon the charity of white folk for its existence, since the blacks had always been more or less skeptical about the program for liberty and freedom, the efforts of the society were not entirely unprofitable. Vistas of immaculate offices spread in every direction from the elevator and footfalls were muffled in thick imitation-Persian rugs. While the large staff of officials was eager to end all oppression and persecution of the Negro, they were never so happy and excited as when a Negro was barred from a theater or fried to a crisp. Then they would leap for telephones, grab telegraph pads and yell for stenographers; smiling through their simulated indignation at the spectacle of another reason for their continued existence and appeals for funds.

Ever since the first sanitarium of Black-No-More, Incorporated, started turning Negroes into Caucasians, the National Social Equality League’s income had been decreasing. No dues had been collected in months and subscriptions to the national mouthpiece, *The Dilemma*, had dwindled to almost nothing. Officials, long since ensconced in palatial apartments, began to grow panic-stricken as pay days got farther apart. They began to envision the time when they would no longer be able for the sake of the Negro race to suffer the hardships of lunching on canvasback duck at the Urban Club surrounded by the white dilettante, endure the perils of first-class Transatlantic passage to stage Save-Dear-Africa Conferences or undergo the excruciating torture of rolling back and forth across the United States in drawing-rooms to hear each other lecture on the Negro problem. On meager salaries of five thousand dollars a year they had fought strenuously and tirelessly to obtain for the Negroes the constitutional rights which only a few thousand rich white folk possessed. And now they saw the work of a lifetime being rapidly destroyed.

Single-handed they felt incapable of organizing an effective opposition to Black-No-More, Incorporated, so they had called a conference of all of the outstanding Negro leaders of the country to assemble at the League’s headquarters on December 1, 1933. Getting the Negro leaders together for any purpose except boasting of each other’s accomplishments had previously been impossible. As a usual thing they fought each other with a vigor only surpassed by that of their pleas for racial solidarity and unity of action. This situation, however, was unprecedented, so almost all of the representative gentlemen of color to whom invitations had been sent agreed with alacrity to come. To a man they felt that it was time to bury the hatchet before they became too hungry to do any digging.

In a very private inner office of the N.S.E.L. suite, Dr. Shakespeare Agamemnon Beard, founder of the League and a graduate of Harvard, Yale and Copenhagen (whose haughty bearing never failed to impress both Caucasians and Negroes) sat before a glass-topped desk, rubbing now his curly gray head, and now his full spade beard. For a mere six thousand dollars a year, the learned doctor wrote scholarly and biting editorials in *The Dilemma* denouncing the Caucasians whom he secretly admired and lauding the greatness of the Negroes whom he alternately pitied and despised. In limpid prose he told of the sufferings and privations of the downtrodden black workers with whose lives he was totally and thankfully unfamiliar. Like most Negro leaders, he deified the black woman but abstained from employing aught save octoroons. He talked at white banquets about “we of the black race” and admitted in books that he was part-French, part-Russian, part-Indian and part-Negro. He bitterly denounced the Nordics for debauching Negro women while taking care to hire comely yellow stenographers with weak resistance. In a real way, he loved his people. In time of peace he was a Pink Socialist but when the clouds of war gathered he bivouacked at the feet of Mars.

Before the champion of the darker races lay a neatly typed resolution drawn up by him and his staff the day before and addressed to the Attorney General of the United States. The staff had taken this precaution because no member of it believed that the other Negro leaders possessed sufficient education to word the document effectively and grammatically. Dr. Beard reread the resolution and then placing it in the drawer of the desk, pressed one of a row of buttons. “Tell them to come in,” he directed. The mulattress turned and switched out of the room, followed by the appraising and approving eye of the aged scholar. He heaved a regretful sigh as the door closed and his thoughts dwelt on the vigor of his youth.

===Chinese===
战斗型黑人组织「全国社会平等同盟」（National Social Equality League）的全国办事处正热闹得像开了锅。电话铃此起彼伏，混血职员们兴奋地来回奔忙，送信的小厮进进出出。办事处设在曼哈顿时代广场地段，四十年来一直在为黑人公民争取完全的社会平等，并要求立即取缔私刑这项全国性的消遣运动。这个组织的存在，很大程度上得靠白人老爷的慈善施舍——黑人们对那套自由解放纲领向来半信半疑——不过协会的种种努力倒也不算白费。一尘不染的办公室从电梯口向四面八方铺展开去，厚厚的仿波斯地毯把脚步声吞得干干净净。这一大班子干事急着终结对黑人的一切压迫与迫害，这倒不假；可他们最快活、最来劲的时候，莫过于某个黑人被剧院拒之门外、或是被活活烤成焦炭的当口。那时他们便会扑向电话，抓起电报笺，扯着嗓子喊速记员；脸上挂着佯装的义愤，心里笑开了花——瞧，他们继续存在下去的理由和伸手募捐的由头，这不又来了一桩。

自从「黑色终结」公司的第一家疗养院开始把黑人变成白种人以来，全国社会平等同盟的收入便一路下滑。会费已有好几个月没收上来一分，全国喉舌刊物《困境》（*The Dilemma*）的订户也所剩无几。那些早已在宫殿般公寓里住安逸了的干事们，眼见发薪日的间隔越拉越长，开始慌了神。他们开始想象那样一天：为了黑人种族，他们将再也无从忍受这些苦难——再也无缘在城市俱乐部（Urban Club）里被白人附庸风雅之辈环绕着享用帆背鸭午餐；再也无缘冒着头等舱横渡大西洋的凶险去张罗「拯救亲爱的非洲」大会；再也无缘承受那酷刑般的折磨，坐着卧车包厢在全美国来回颠簸，听彼此就种族问题高谈阔论。他们年俸微薄，区区五千美元，却为了给黑人争得那只有几千个阔绰白人才享有的宪法权利，呕心沥血、不知疲倦地奋斗着。而如今，他们眼睁睁看着毕生的心血飞快地化为乌有。

他们觉得单凭自己一家，组织不起对「黑色终结」公司的有效抵抗，于是召集全国所有知名黑人领袖，定于 1933 年 12 月 1 日在同盟总部开会。以往要让黑人领袖们聚到一处，除非是彼此吹嘘各自的功绩，否则门儿都没有。通常他们互相斗法的那股狠劲，只有他们呼吁种族大团结、行动大一统时的嗓门能压过去。不过眼下这局面史无前例，所以接到请柬的诸位有色人种绅士代表，几乎个个欣然应允。不约而同地，他们都觉得是时候把战斧埋掉了——趁肚子还没饿得连土都刨不动。

在同盟套房一间极为私密的内室里，莎士比亚·阿伽门农·比尔德博士——同盟创始人，哈佛、耶鲁和哥本哈根的毕业生，那副倨傲的做派向来能把白人和黑人都镇住——正坐在一张玻璃台面的写字桌前，一会儿揉揉他卷曲的灰脑袋，一会儿捋捋那把黑桃形的大胡子。这位博学的博士年薪区区六千美元，替《困境》撰写学术气十足又尖酸刻薄的社论，痛斥他暗中仰慕的白种人，颂扬他时而怜悯、时而鄙夷的黑人的伟大。他用清丽流畅的文笔，倾诉着被踩在脚下的黑人工人的苦难与赤贫——对他们的生活，他谢天谢地一无所知。同大多数黑人领袖一样，他把黑人女性奉若神明，雇起人来却只肯用有八分之一黑人血统者（octoroon）。他在白人的宴席上高谈「我们黑种人」，又在书里承认自己有几分法国血统、几分俄国血统、几分印第安血统和几分黑人血统。他咬牙切齿地痛斥北欧人糟蹋黑人妇女，同时细心地专挑那些容貌姣好、意志薄弱的黄皮肤姑娘当速记员。在实实在在的意义上，他热爱自己的人民。和平时节，他是个粉色社会主义者；而一旦战云密布，他便到战神脚下扎营去了。

这位暗肤色种族的捍卫者面前，摆着一份打印得整整齐齐的决议案，由他和他手下人员头天起草，呈文致美国司法部长。手下人此举是防患于未然，因为他们谁也不相信，别的那些黑人领袖受过足够的教育，能把这份文件写得字斟句酌、文从字顺。比尔德博士把决议又读了一遍，随后把它放进写字桌抽屉，按下一排按钮中的一个。「请他们进来。」他吩咐道。那混血女文员转过身，扭着腰肢出了房间，老学者那双品鉴而赞许的目光一直跟在她身后。门关上时，他遗憾地叹了口气，思绪沉浸在自己年轻时代的雄风里。

===Original===
In three or four minutes the door opened again and several well-dressed blacks, mulattoes and white men entered the large office and took seats around the wall. They greeted each other and the President of the League with usual cordiality but for the first time in their lives they were sincere about it. If anyone could save the day it was Beard. They all admitted that, as did the Doctor himself. They pulled out fat cigars, long slender cigarettes and London briar pipes, lit them and awaited the opening of the conference.

The venerable lover of his race tapped with his knuckle for order, laid aside his six-inch cigarette and rising, said:

“It were quite unseemly for me who lives such a cloistered life and am spared the bane or benefit of many intimate contacts with those of our struggling race who by sheer courage, tenacity and merit have lifted their heads above the mired mass, to deign to take from a more capable individual the unpleasant task of reviewing the combination of unfortunate circumstances that has brought us together, man to man, within the four walls of the office.” He shot a foxy glance around the assembly and then went on suavely. “And so, my friends, I beg your august permission to confer upon my able and cultured secretary and confidant, Dr. Napoleon Wellington Jackson, the office of chairman of this temporary body. I need not introduce Dr. Jackson to you. You know of his scholarship, his high sense of duty and his deep love of the suffering black race. You have doubtless had the pleasure of singing some of the many sorrow songs he has written and popularized in the past twenty years, and you must know of his fame as a translator of Latin poets and his authoritative work on the Greek language.

“Before I gratefully yield the floor to Dr. Jackson, however, I want to tell you that our destiny lies in the stars. Ethiopia’s fate is in the balance. The Goddess of the Nile weeps bitter tears at the feet of the Great Sphinx. The lowering clouds gather over the Congo and the lightning flashes o’er Togoland. To your tents, O Israel! The hour is at hand.”

The president of the N.S.E.L. sat down and the erudite Dr. Jackson, his tall, lanky secretary got up. There was no fear of Dr. Jackson ever winning a beauty contest. He was a sooty black, very broad shouldered, with long, apelike arms, a diminutive egg-shaped head that sat on his collar like a hen’s egg on a demitasse cup and eyes that protruded so far from his head that they seemed about to fall out. He wore pince-nez that were continually slipping from his very flat and oily nose. His chief business in the organization was to write long and indignant letters to public officials and legislators whenever a Negro was mistreated, demanding justice, fair play and other legal guarantees vouchsafed no whites except bloated plutocrats fallen miraculously afoul of the law, and to speak to audiences of sex-starved matrons who yearned to help the Negro stand erect. During his leisure time, which was naturally considerable, he wrote long and learned articles, bristling with references, for the more intellectual magazines, in which he sought to prove conclusively that the plantation shouts of Southern Negro peons were superior to any of Beethoven’s symphonies and that the city of Benin was the original site of the Garden of Eden.

===Chinese===
三四分钟后，门又开了，几位衣着考究的黑人、混血儿和白人鱼贯走进大办公室，沿墙落座。他们照例热络地互相问候，也问候了同盟主席，但有生以来头一回，这份热络是真心的。要说有谁能挽回大局，那就是比尔德。大家都承认这一点，博士本人也不例外。他们掏出粗胖的雪茄、细长的香烟和伦敦石南根烟斗，点上火，静候会议开场。

这位德高望重的种族之友用指节敲敲桌子要大家安静，搁下他那支六英寸长的香烟，起身说道：

「鄙人久居书斋，深蒙上天垂怜，免于同我族那些凭勇气、韧性与德行从泥泞大众中昂起头来的人士亲密往来之苦或之福；若由鄙人屈尊越俎代庖，去承担那桩不快的差事——回顾一下把我们大家促膝相邀、聚于这四面墙壁之间的种种不幸因缘——那未免有失体统。」他朝会场狡黠地扫了一眼，接着温文尔雅地说了下去。「因此，朋友们，我恳请诸位郑重允准，将这个临时机构的主席一职，授予我的能干而有教养的秘书兼心腹——拿破仑·威灵顿·杰克逊博士。无需我向诸位介绍杰克逊博士。诸位都知道他的学问、他高度的责任感和他对受苦受难的黑色种族的深爱。过去二十年里，他创作并推广了大量哀歌（sorrow songs），诸位想必有幸唱过其中几首；他翻译拉丁诗人的名声，以及他那部论希腊语的权威著作，诸位也必然有所耳闻。

「不过，在我满怀感激地把讲台让给杰克逊博士之前，我要告诉诸位：我们的命运悬于星辰。埃塞俄比亚的命运系于一线。尼罗河女神在大斯芬克斯脚下挥洒苦泪。乌云正聚拢在刚果上空，闪电划过多哥兰。回帐去吧，以色列啊！时辰已经来临。」

全国社会平等同盟的主席坐了下来，他那博学的秘书——又高又瘦长的杰克逊博士——站起身。杰克逊博士这辈子是别想赢什么选美比赛了。他是个炭黑的黑人，肩膀极宽，胳膊长得像猿，脑袋小得像个蛋，架在衣领上，好比一颗鸡蛋搁在迷你咖啡杯口上；两只眼睛向外凸得厉害，眼看就要掉出来。他架着一副夹鼻眼镜，从他那油光光的塌鼻梁上不住地往下滑。他在组织里的主要营生，是每逢黑人受了欺负，就给官员和议员们写又长又愤慨的信，要求正义、公平以及其他种种法律保障——这些保障哪个白人也无缘享有，除非是骇人听闻地撞在法律枪口上的脑满肠肥的财阀；再就是对着一班欲火中烧、一心想帮黑人「挺直腰杆」的太太听众演讲。闲暇时分——那自然是相当可观——他便给那些更「有思想」的杂志撰写又长又学术、注释密密麻麻的文章，试图铁证如山地证明：南方黑人苦力的种植园呐喊胜过贝多芬的任何一部交响曲，贝宁城才是伊甸园的原址。

===Original===
“Hhmm! Hu‑umn! Now er‑‑ah, gentlemen,” began Dr. Jackson, rocking back on his heels, taking off his eye glasses and beginning to polish them with a silk kerchief, “as you know, the Negro race is face to face with a grave crisis. I‑‑ah‑‑presume it is er‑‑ah unnecessary for me to go into any details concerning the-ah activities of Black-No-More, Incorporated. Suffice er‑‑ah umph! ummmmh! to say-ah that it has thrown our society into rather a-ah bally turmoil. Our people are forgetting shamelessly their-ah duty to the-ah organizations that have fought valiantly for them these-ah many years and are now busily engaged chasing a bally-ah will-o-the wisp. Ahem!

“You-ah probably all fully realize that-ah a continuation of the aforementioned activities will prove disastrous to our-ah organizations. You-ah, like us, must feel-uh that something drastic must be done to preserve the integrity of Negro society. Think, gentlemen, what the future will mean to-uh all those who-uh have toiled so hard for Negro society. What-ah, may I ask, will we do when there are no longer any-ah groups to support us? Of course, Dr. Crookman and-ah his associates have a-uh perfect right to‑ah engage in any legitimate business, but-ah their present activities cannot-ah be classed under that head, considering the effect on our endeavors. Before we go any further, however, I-ah would like to introduce our research expert Mr. Walter Williams, who will-ah describe the situation in the South.”

Mr. Walter Williams, a tall, heavyset white man with pale blue eyes, wavy auburn hair and a militant, lantern jaw, rose and bowed to the assemblage and proceeded to paint a heartrending picture of the loss of pride and race solidarity among Negroes North and South. There was, he said, not a single local of the N.S.E.L. functioning, dues had dwindled to nothing, he had not been able to hold a meeting anywhere, while many of the stanchest supporters had gone over into the white race.

“Personally,” he concluded, “I am very proud to be a Negro and always have been (his great-grandfather, it seemed, had been a mulatto), and I’m willing to sacrifice for the uplift of my race. I cannot understand what has come over our people that they have so quickly forgotten the ancient glories of Ethiopia, Songhay and Dahomey, and their marvelous record of achievement since emancipation.” Mr. Williams was known to be a Negro among his friends and acquaintances, but no one else would have suspected it.

Another white man of remote Negro ancestry, Rev. Herbert Gronne of Dunbar University, followed the research expert with a long discourse in which he expressed fear for the future of his institution whose student body had been reduced to sixty-five persons and deplored the catastrophe “that has befallen us black people.”

===Chinese===
「呣！呃——姆！那么这个——啊，诸位，」杰克逊博士开了腔，脚跟一晃一晃，摘下眼镜，拿一方丝绸手帕擦拭起来，「众所周知，黑人种族正面临着一场严重危机。这个——啊，我想用不着—呃—啊——由我来详细讲述『黑色终结』公司—啊的活动了。只需—呃—啊，嗯哼！嗯——哼！—啊说一句：它已把我们的社会搅得—啊—乱作一团。我们的同胞正恬不知耻地忘却他们—啊—对这些多年来为他们英勇奋斗的组织—啊—应尽的责任，如今正忙不迭地追赶—啊—一个该死的鬼火。咳嗯！

「诸位—啊想必都十分明白，—啊上述活动再这么继续下去，对我们—啊—各组织必将酿成大祸。诸位—啊同我们一样，必能感到—呃：必须采取断然措施，保全黑人社会的完整。想一想吧，诸位，未来对—呃那些为黑人社会辛苦操劳多年的—啊人意味着什么。试问—啊，等到再没有—啊什么组织供养我们了，我们可怎么办？当然了，克鲁克曼医生—啊—和他的一伙完全有权—呃—经营任何正当买卖，可是—啊他们眼下的营生，鉴于它对我们事业的冲击，实在—啊—归不进那一类。不过，在往下进行之前，我—啊—想介绍我们的调研专家沃尔特·威廉姆斯先生，由他—啊—来讲讲南方的情况。」

沃尔特·威廉姆斯先生是个高大敦实的白人，淡蓝色眼睛，波浪式红棕头发，下巴尖长突出，带着一股好斗劲儿。他起身向全场一鞠躬，随即描绘出一幅令人心碎的图景：南北方黑人的自豪感与种族团结如何荡然无存。据他说，全国社会平等同盟已没有一个分会在运转，会费收入化为乌有，任何地方都开不成一场会，而许多最坚定的支持者已经转投白种人去了。

「就我个人而言，」他总结道，「我以身为黑人为荣，从来如此」（原来他的曾祖父据说是个混血儿），「我愿意为我种族的振兴作出牺牲。我实在不明白我们的人中了什么邪，这么快就忘掉了埃塞俄比亚、桑海和达荷美的古代荣光，忘掉了解放以来他们辉煌的成就史。」威廉姆斯先生在朋友熟人圈里是公认的黑人，可外人谁也看不出来。

另一位带点儿遥远黑人血统的白人——邓巴大学的赫伯特·格罗内牧师——接着调研专家的话头发言，滔滔不绝。他为自己那所学校的命运忧心忡忡：全校学生已锐减到六十五人；他还痛惜「降临到我们黑人头上的这场大难」。

===Original===
They all listened with respect to Dr. Gronne. He had been in turn a college professor, a social worker and a minister, had received the approval of the white folks and was thus doubly acceptable to the Negroes. Much of his popularity was due to the fact that he very cleverly knew how to make statements that sounded radical to Negroes but sufficiently conservative to satisfy the white trustees of his school. In addition he possessed the asset of looking perpetually earnest and sincere.

Following him came Colonel Mortimer Roberts, principal of the Dusky River Agricultural Institute, Supreme General of the Knights and Daughters of Kingdom Come and president of the Uncle Tom Memorial Association. Colonel Roberts was the acknowledged leader of the conservative Negroes (most of whom had nothing to conserve) who felt at all times that the white folks were in the lead and that Negroes should be careful to guide themselves accordingly.

He was a great mountain of blackness with a head shaped like an upturned bucket, pierced by two pig-like eyes and a cavernous mouth equipped with large tombstone teeth which he almost continually displayed. His speech was a cross between the woofing of a bloodhound and the explosion of an inner tube. It conveyed to most white people an impression of rugged simplicity and sincerity, which was very fortunate since Colonel Roberts maintained his school through their contributions. He spoke as usual about the cordial relations existing between the two races in his native Georgia, the effrontery of Negroes who dared whiten themselves and thus disturb the minds of white people and insinuated alliance with certain militant organizations in the South to stop this whitening business before it went too far. Having spoken his mind and received scant applause, the Colonel (some white man had once called him Colonel and the title stuck) puffing and blowing, sat down.

Mr. Claude Spelling, a scared-looking little brown man with big ears, who held the exalted office of president of the Society of Negro Merchants, added his volume of blues to the discussion. The refrain was that Negro business‑‑always anemic‑‑was about to pass out entirely through lack of patronage. Mr. Spelling had for many years been the leading advocate of the strange doctrine that an underpaid Negro worker should go out of his way to patronize a little dingy Negro store instead of going to a cheaper and cleaner chain store, all for the dubious satisfaction of helping Negro merchants grow wealthy.

The next speaker, Dr. Joseph Bonds, a little rat-faced Negro with protruding teeth stained by countless plugs of chewing tobacco and wearing horn-rimmed spectacles, who headed the Negro Data League, almost cried (which would have been terrible to observe) when he told of the difficulty his workers had encountered in their efforts to persuade retired white capitalists, whose guilty consciences persuaded them to indulge in philanthropy, to give their customary donations to the work. The philanthropists seemed to think, said Dr. Bonds, that since the Negroes were busily solving their difficulties, there was no need for social work among them or any collection of data. He almost sobbed aloud when he described how his collections had fallen from $50,000 a month to less than $1,000.

===Chinese===
大家都毕恭毕敬地听格罗内博士讲话。他先后当过大学教授、社会工作者和牧师，得到了白人们的首肯，因此在黑人那边也就加倍地吃得开。他之所以广受欢迎，多半是因为他极其乖巧，懂得如何发表这样一种声明：在黑人听来够激进，在他学校的白人校董听来又足够保守。此外，他还有一项本钱：永远一副恳切、真诚的面孔。

接着他发言的是莫蒂默·罗伯茨上校——黝黑河农业学院（Dusky River Agricultural Institute）院长、天国骑士与女儿会（Knights and Daughters of Kingdom Come）最高统帅、汤姆叔叔纪念协会会长。罗伯茨上校是保守派黑人的公认领袖（这派人大都无产可守），他们时时刻刻觉得白人领路在先，黑人理应小心翼翼、紧紧跟上。

他是一座黑黝黝的大山，脑袋像个倒扣的水桶，上面凿着两只猪眼和一张洞穴般的大嘴，嘴里一排墓碑似的大牙几乎时刻摆出来示人。他说话的动静，介于猎血猎犬的低吼和车胎爆裂之间。这声音给大多数白人留下的印象是粗犷、淳朴、诚恳——这可是天大的好事，因为罗伯茨上校的学校全靠他们的捐款维持。他照例大谈自己家乡佐治亚州两族关系如何融洽，大骂黑人竟敢把自己变白、搅得白人心神不宁是大逆不道，又旁敲侧击地暗示：不妨同南方某些战斗性组织结盟，趁这变白的勾当还没走得太远，赶紧把它刹住。把话说完，掌声寥寥，上校（某位白人当年管他叫过一声「上校」，这头衔便跟定了他）呼哧呼哧喘着粗气坐下了。

克劳德·斯佩林先生——一个一脸惊惶、耳朵奇大的棕色皮肤小个子，荣任黑人商人协会会长这一显赫职位——也为这场合唱添上了他那一节忧郁的布鲁斯。副歌是：黑人商业——向来贫血——眼看就要因为没了主顾而整个儿咽气。多年来，斯佩林先生一直是那套奇怪教义的头号鼓吹手：拿低薪的黑人工人应该特意绕远，去光顾一家又小又昏暗的黑人铺子，而不去更便宜、更干净的连锁店——这一切，只为帮黑人商人发财致富，图个说不清道不明的满足。

下一位发言人约瑟夫·邦兹博士，一个鼠脸小个黑人，龅牙被数不清的嚼烟块熏得焦黄，鼻梁上架一副牛角质眼镜，执掌黑人数据同盟（Negro Data League）。他讲到手下的干事们如何费尽唇舌，想劝动那些退了休、良心不安只好拿慈善解闷的白人资本家照常给工作捐款，讲到伤心处几乎落泪（那场面可就惨不忍睹了）。邦兹博士说，那些慈善家似乎觉得，既然黑人正忙着自解危难，也就用不着在他们中间搞什么社会工作、收集什么数据了。讲到捐款从每月 50,000 美元跌到不足 1,000 美元时，他几乎嚎啕出声。

===Original===
His feeling in the matter could easily be appreciated. He was engaged in a most vital and necessary work: i.e., collecting bales of data to prove satisfactorily to all that more money was needed to collect more data. Most of the data were highly informative, revealing the amazing fact that poor people went to jail oftener than rich ones; that most of the people were not getting enough money for their work; that strangely enough there was some connection between poverty, disease and crime. By establishing these facts with mathematical certitude and illustrating them with elaborate graphs, Dr. Bonds garnered many fat checks. For his people, he said, he wanted work, not charity; but for himself he was always glad to get the charity with as little work as possible. For many years he had succeeded in doing so without any ascertainable benefit accruing to the Negro group.

Dr. Bonds’ show of emotion almost brought the others to tears and many of them muttered “Yes, Brother” while he was talking. The conferees were getting stirred up but it took the next speaker to really get them excited.

When he rose an expectant hush fell over the assemblage. They all knew and respected the Right Reverend Bishop Ezekiel Whooper of the Ethiopian True Faith Wash Foot Methodist Church for three reasons: viz., his church was rich (though the parishioners were poor), he had a very loud voice and the white people praised him. He was sixty, corpulent and an expert at the art of making cuckolds.

“Our loyal and devoted clergy,” he boomed, “are being forced into manual labor and the Negro church is rapidly dying.” And then he launched into a violent tirade against Black-No-More and favored any means to put the corporation out of business. In his excitement he blew saliva, waved his long arms, stamped his feet, pummeled the desk, rolled his eyes, knocked down his chair, almost sat on the rug and generally reverted to the antics of Negro bush preachers.

This exhibition proved contagious. Rev. Herbert Gronne, face flushed and shouting amens, marched from one end of the room to the other; Colonel Roberts, looking like an inebriated black-faced comedian, rocked back and forth clapping his hands; the others began to groan and moan. Dr. Napoleon Wellington Jackson, sensing his opportunity, began to sing a spiritual in his rich soprano voice. The others immediately joined him. The very air seemed charged with emotion.

===Chinese===
他这份心情不难体会。他从事的是一项极其重大而必要的工作，即：成捆成捆地收集数据，好让所有人都满意地相信——要收集更多数据，就需要更多的钱。这些数据大部分极有教益，揭示出令人惊叹的事实：穷人比富人更常进监狱；大多数人干活拿的钱不够糊口；说来也怪，贫穷、疾病和犯罪之间似乎还有点瓜葛。邦兹博士以数学般的精确确立了这些事实，再配上精工细作的图表，于是收进了一张张肥厚的支票。他声称，为他的人民，他要的是工作，不是施舍；至于他自己，他向来乐于少干工作、多领施舍。多年来他在这方面无往不利，而黑人族群没有从其中得到任何看得见的好处。

邦兹博士这番动情的表演几乎催下了旁人的眼泪，他讲话时，好些人不住地低声附和：「是啊，弟兄。」与会者渐渐被煽动起来了，可要把他们的情绪真正点着，还得靠下一位发言人。

他一起身，全场便肃然静候。人人都认识并敬重埃塞俄比亚真信洗脚卫理公会教会（Ethiopian True Faith Wash Foot Methodist Church）的最可敬主教以西结·胡珀（Ezekiel Whooper），理由有三：其一，他的教会阔绰（虽然教众穷得很）；其二，他的嗓门奇响；其三，白人们夸他。他年届六十，大腹便便，还是给别人制造绿帽子这门艺术的一代宗师。

「我们忠贞奉献的神职人员，」他声若洪钟，「正被逼去干体力活，黑人教会正在迅速消亡。」接着他对「黑色终结」发起了一场暴风骤雨般的痛骂，声称任何能让这家公司关门的手段都值得赞成。他激动起来，唾沫横飞，长臂乱舞，跺脚捶桌，眼珠乱翻，把自己的椅子都带倒了，险些一屁股坐到地毯上，总而言之，把黑人乡野布道牧师的看家把式全使唤了出来。

这场表演证明极富感染力。赫伯特·格罗内牧师满面通红，高喊着「阿门」，从屋子这头正步走到那头；罗伯茨上校活像个醉醺醺的涂黑脸滑稽戏子，前摇后晃拍着巴掌；其余的人也开始哼哼唧唧、长吁短叹。拿破仑·威灵顿·杰克逊博士一见机会来了，便用他那浑厚的女高音唱起一支灵歌。众人立刻跟着唱了起来。连空气都仿佛充满了激情。

## V / 第五章

===Original===
Bishop Whooper was about to start up again, when Dr. Beard, who had sat cold and disdainful through this outbreak of revivalism, toying with his gold-rimmed fountain pen and gazing at the exhibition through half-closed eyelids, interrupted in sharp metallic tones.

“Let’s get down to earth now,” he commanded. “We’ve had enough of this nonsense. We have a resolution here addressed to the Attorney General of the United States demanding that Dr. Crookman and his associates be arrested and their activities stopped at once for the good of both races. All those in favor of this resolution say aye. Contrary? … Very well, the ayes have it. … Miss Hilton please send off this telegram at once!”

They looked at Dr. Beard and each other in amazement. Several started to meekly protest.

“You gentlemen are all twenty-one, aren’t you?” sneered Beard. “Well, then be men enough to stand by your decision.”

===Chinese===
胡珀主教正要重新开腔，比尔德博士却用尖利如金属的嗓音打断了话头。在这场奋兴会式的大发作里，他自始至终冷冷坐着，一脸不屑，一边把玩着那支金圈自来水笔，一边眼皮半阖地睨着这出表演。

「现在言归正传，」他下令道，「这些废话听够了。我们这里有一份致美国司法部长的决议案，要求逮捕克鲁克曼医生及其同伙，立即停止他们的活动——这是为了两个种族的共同利益。赞成本决议的请说『赞成』。有反对的吗？……很好，多数赞成，通过。……希尔顿小姐（Hilton），请立刻把这封电报发出去！」

他们惊愕地望着比尔德博士，又面面相觑。好几位温顺地张口想抗议。

「诸位先生都满二十一岁了吧，啊？」比尔德讥笑道，「那就拿出点男子汉气概来，对自己通过的决定负责到底。」

===Original===
“But Doctor Beard,” objected Rev. Gronne, “isn’t this a rather unusual procedure?”

“Rev. Gronne,” the great man replied, “it’s not near as unusual as Black-No-More. I have probably ruffled your dignity but that’s nothing to what Dr. Crookman will do.”

“I guess you’re right, Beard,” the college president agreed.

“I know it,” snapped the other.

---

===Chinese===
「可是比尔德博士，」格罗内牧师提出异议，「这么办是不是有点不合常规？」

「格罗内牧师，」这位大人物答道，「它可远没有『黑色终结』那么不合常规。我这一手兴许冒犯了诸位的尊严，可比起克鲁克曼医生将要干出的事，这算不了什么。」

「我想你说得对，比尔德。」这位大学校长表示同意。

「我知道我说得对。」对方抢白道。

---

===Original===
The Honorable Walter Brybe, who had won his exalted position as Attorney General of the United States because of his long and faithful service helping large corporations to circumvent the federal laws, sat at his desk in Washington, DC. Before him lay the wired resolution from the conference of Negro leaders. He pursed his lips and reached for his private telephone.

“Gorman?” he inquired softly into the receiver. “Is that you?”

“Nossuh,” came the reply, “this heah is Mistah Gay’s valet.”

“Well, call Mister Gay to the telephone at once.”

“Yassuh.”

===Chinese===
尊敬的沃尔特·布莱布（Walter Brybe）阁下，靠长年忠心耿耿地帮大公司钻联邦法律的空子，赢得了美国司法部长这一显赫职位。此刻他正坐在华盛顿特区的办公桌前，面前摊着黑人领袖会议电告而来的那份决议。他抿了抿嘴，伸手去拿私人电话。

「戈尔曼（Gorman）？」他对着话筒柔声问，「是你吗？」

「不是，老爷，」话筒里答道，「这厢是盖——伊先生（Mistah Gay）的贴身男仆。」

「那就请盖伊先生立刻来听电话。」

「是，老爷。」

===Original===
“That you, Gorman,” asked the chief legal officer of the nation addressing the National Chairman of his party.

“Yeh, what’s up?”

“You heard ’bout this resolution from them niggers in New York, aint you? It’s been in all of the papers.”

“Yes I read it.”

“Well, whaddya think we oughtta do about it?”

===Chinese===
「是你吧，戈尔曼，」全国首席法律官员问道——接电话的正是他所在政党的全国主席。

「是我，啥事儿？」

「纽约那帮黑鬼发来一份决议，你听说啦？所有的报纸都登了。」

「嗯，我看了。」

「那你说，这事儿咱们该怎么办？」

===Original===
“Take it easy, Walter. Give ’em the old run around. You know. They ain’t got a thin dime; it’s this other crowd that’s holding the heavy jack. And ’course you know we gotta clean up our deficit. Just lemme work with that Black-No-More crowd. I can talk business with that Johnson fellow.”

“All right, Gorman, I think you’re right, but you don’t want to forget that there’s a whole lot of white sentiment against them coons.”

“Needn’t worry ’bout that,” scoffed Gorman. “There’s no money behind it much and besides it’s in states we can’t carry anyhow. Go ahead; stall them New York niggers off. You’re a lawyer, you can always find a reason.”

===Chinese===
「别急，沃尔特。老一套伺候——跟他们兜圈子，你懂的。他们连一毛薄钱都掏不出来；攥着大把票子的是另一伙人。再说你心里有数，咱们的亏空总得填平。就让我来跟『黑色终结』那伙人打交道吧。约翰逊那家伙，我跟他谈得成买卖。」

「好吧，戈尔曼，我想你是对的。可你别忘了，白人舆论对那帮黑鬼的反感可大着呢。」

「这个用不着操心，」戈尔曼不以为然地说，「那股情绪背后没多少钱撑腰，再说都集中在咱们反正也赢不下来的州里。放手去办；把纽约那帮黑鬼先拖住。你是律师嘛，找条理由还不是张口就来。」

===Original===
“Thanks for the compliment, Gorman,” said the Attorney General, hanging up the receiver.

He pressed a button on his desk and a young girl, armed with pencil and pad, came in.

“Take this letter,” he ordered: “To Doctor Shakespeare Agamemnon Beard (what a hell of a name!), Chairman of the Committee for the Preservation of Negro Racial Integrity, 1400 Broadway, New York City.”

===Chinese===
「多谢夸奖，戈尔曼。」司法部长说着挂上了听筒。

他按了按桌上的按钮，一个手持铅笔和便笺簿的年轻姑娘走了进来。

「记这封信，」他吩咐道：「致莎士比亚·阿伽门农·比尔德博士（这他妈算个什么名字！），黑人种族完整性维护委员会（Committee for the Preservation of Negro Racial Integrity）主席，纽约市百老汇大街 1400 号。」

===Original===
> “My dear Dr. Beard:
> “The Attorney General has received the resolution signed by yourself and others and given it careful consideration.
> “Regardless of personal views in the matter (I don’t give a damn whether they turn white or not, myself) it is not possible for the Department of Justice to interfere with a legitimate business enterprise so long as its methods are within the law. The corporation in question has violated no federal statute and hence there is not the slightest ground for interfering with its activities.
> “Very truly yours,

**Walter Brybe**.

“Get that off at once. Give out copies to the press. That’s all.”

---

===Chinese===
> 亲爱的比尔德博士：
> 司法部长已收到您及诸位联署的决议，并已审慎加以考虑。
> 无论个人对此事持何种看法（就我本人而言，他们变不变白，我才他妈不在乎），只要其经营手段在法律范围之内，司法部便不能干预一家正当经营的企业。该公司并未触犯任何联邦法令，因此干预其活动毫无根据。
> 您忠实的，

**沃尔特·布莱布**

「马上把信发出去。给报界送几份副本。就这样。」

---

===Original===
Santop Licorice, founder and leader of the Back-to-Africa Society, read the reply of the Attorney General to the Negro leaders with much malicious satisfaction. He laid aside his morning paper, pulled a fat cigar from a box near by, lit it and blew clouds of smoke above his woolly head. He was always delighted when Dr. Beard met with any sort of rebuff or embarrassment. He was doubly pleased in this instance because he had been overlooked in the sending out of invitations to Negro leaders to join the Committee for the Preservation of Negro Racial Integrity. It was outrageous, after all the talking he had done in favor of Negro racial integrity.

Mr. Licorice for some fifteen years had been very profitably advocating the emigration of all the American Negroes to Africa. He had not, of course, gone there himself and had not the slightest intention of going so far from the fleshpots, but he told the other Negroes to go. Naturally the first step in their going was to join his society by paying five dollars a year for membership, ten dollars for a gold, green and purple robe and silver-colored helmet that together cost two dollars and a half, contributing five dollars to the Santop Licorice Defense Fund (there was a perpetual defense fund because Licorice was perpetually in the courts for fraud of some kind), and buying shares at five dollars each in the Royal Black Steamship Company, for obviously one could not get to Africa without a ship and Negroes ought to travel on Negro-owned and operated ships. The ships were Santop’s especial pride. True, they had never been to Africa, had never had but one cargo and that, being gin, was half consumed by the unpaid and thirsty crew before the vessel was saved by the Coast Guard, but they had cost more than anything else the Back-To-Africa Society had purchased even though they were worthless except as scrap iron. Mr. Licorice, who was known by his followers as Provisional President of Africa, Admiral of the African Navy, Field Marshal of the African Army and Knight Commander of the Nile, had a genius for being stuck with junk by crafty salesmen. White men only needed to tell him that he was shrewder than white men and he would immediately reach for a check book.

But there was little reaching for check books in his office nowadays. He had been as hard hit as the other Negroes. Why should anybody in the Negro race want to go back to Africa at a cost of five hundred dollars for passage when they could stay in America and get white for fifty dollars? Mr. Licorice saw the point but instead of scuttling back to Demerara from whence he had come to save his race from oppression, he had hung on in the hope that the activities of Black-No-More, Incorporated, would be stopped. In the meantime, he had continued to attempt to save the Negroes by vigorously attacking all of the other Negro organizations and at the same time preaching racial solidarity and cooperation in his weekly newspaper, The African Abroad, which was printed by white folks and had until a year ago been full of skin-whitening and hair-straightening advertisements.

===Chinese===
回非洲运动协会的创始人兼首领桑托普·利科里斯，怀着满腔恶意的快意，读罢了司法部长给黑人领袖们的复函。他放下晨报，从近旁一只盒子里抽出一支粗雪茄点上，在他那羊毛般的脑袋顶上喷出一团团烟云。比尔德博士但凡碰个钉子、出个丑，他总是乐不可支；这一回他更是加倍地来劲，因为向黑人领袖们发出请柬、邀他们加入黑人种族完整性维护委员会时，单单把他漏掉了。太岂有此理了——他为鼓吹黑人种族完整性做过那么多演讲啊。

十五年来，利科里斯先生一直有利可图地鼓吹全体美国黑人移居非洲。他自己当然没去过，也压根没打算离开这片酒肉温柔乡跑到那么远，可他叫别的黑人去。他们要动身，头一步自然就是加入他的协会：每年缴五块钱会费；掏十块钱置办一件金、绿、紫三色的长袍和一顶银色头盔——这两样的成本总共两块五；再向「桑托普·利科里斯辩护基金」（Santop Licorice Defense Fund）捐上五块（这笔辩护基金之所以永续长存，是因为利科里斯没完没了地为这类那种欺诈官司出庭）；还得按每股五块买「皇家黑色轮船公司」（Royal Black Steamship Company）的股票——道理明摆着：没有船就到不了非洲，黑人出远门就该坐黑人自有自营的船。那几条船是桑托普的得意至宝。诚然，它们从没去过非洲，也只运过一趟货，还是杜松子酒——没领到工钱、渴得发慌的船员在海岸警卫队把船救下来之前，已经把它灌掉了半船；诚然，除了论斤当废铁，它们一文不值，可它们的价钱，却是回非洲运动协会历次置办的家当里最贵的。利科里斯先生被追随者们尊称为非洲临时总统、非洲海军上将、非洲陆军元帅兼尼罗河骑士统帅，他自有一样天才：总能叫狡猾的推销员把破烂塞到他手里。白人只消对他说一句「您比白人还精明」，他立刻就会伸手去摸支票簿。

不过如今，他办公室里可没什么摸支票簿的机会了。他跟别的黑人一样挨了重创。既然待在美国花五十块就能变白，本族里还有谁肯花五百块船票钱回非洲去？这个理儿利科里斯先生不是不明白；可他并没有仓皇逃回德梅拉拉（Demerara）——他当年正是从那儿赶来，要把本族从压迫下拯救出去的——而是死撑不走，指望着「黑色终结」公司的活动早晚会被叫停。在此期间，他仍在继续设法拯救黑人：一边起劲地攻击其余所有黑人组织，一边在他那份周报《海外非洲人报》（*The African Abroad*）上宣讲种族团结与合作——那报纸是白人承印的，而且直到一年前，满纸登的还是皮肤增白、头发拉直的广告。

===Original===
“How is our treasury?” he yelled back through the dingy suite of offices to his bookkeeper, a pretty mulatto.

“What treasury?” she asked in mock surprise.

“Why, I thought we had seventy-five dollars,” he blurted.

“We did, but the Sheriff got most of it yesterday or we wouldn’t be in here today.”

“Huumn! Well, that’s bad. And tomorrow’s pay day, isn’t it?”

“Why bring that up?” she sneered. “I’d forgotten all about it.”

===Chinese===
「咱们金库怎么样了？」他隔着一套昏暗的办公室朝里间他的簿记员喊道——那是个标致的混血姑娘。

「什么金库？」她假意吃惊地问。

「咦，我记得咱们还有七十五块钱呢。」他脱口而出。

「本来是有，可昨天让治安官把大半查扣走了，不这样的话，咱们今天就进不了这间办公室喽。」

「嗯哼！唉，这可不妙。明天是发薪日，对吧？」

「提这个干吗？」她讥讽道，「我倒把这事忘得一干二净了。」

===Original===
“Haven’t we got enough for me to get to Atlanta?” Licorice inquired, anxiously.

“There is if you’re gonna hitchhike.”

“Well, of course, I couldn’t do that,” he smiled deprecatingly.

“I should say not,” she retorted surveying his 250-pound, five-feet-six-inches of black blubber.

“Call Western Union,” he commanded.

“What with?”

===Chinese===
「咱们难道凑不出让我上亚特兰大的盘缠？」利科里斯焦急地问。

「你要是打算一路搭顺风车，那还够。」

「这个嘛，当然，我可干不来那种事，」他自谦地赔笑道。

「我看也是干不来，」她顶了回去，上下打量着他那 250 磅重、五英尺六英寸高的一身黑肥膘。

「给西联电报公司（Western Union）挂电话。」他命令道。

「拿什么挂？」

===Original===
“Over the telephone, of course, Miss Hall,” he explained.

“If you can get anything over that telephone you’re a better man than I am, Gunga Din.”

“Has the service been discontinued, young lady?”

“Try and get a number,” she chirped. He gazed ruefully at the telephone.

===Chinese===
「当然是拿电话挂啦，霍尔小姐（Hall）。」他解释道。

「你要是能从那部电话里要通一件事，那你可比我强多了，冈加·丁（Gunga Din）。」

「电话业务停办了吗，年轻的小姐？」

「您倒是拨个号试试呗。」她脆生生地说。他哭丧着脸望着电话机。

===Original===
“Is there anything we can sell?” asked the bewildered Licorice.

“Yeah, if you can get the Sheriff to take off his attachments.”

“That’s right, I had forgotten.”

“You would.”

===Chinese===
「那咱们还有什么能卖的吗？」利科里斯一筹莫展地问。

「有啊——只要你能求动治安官，把他的查封令给撤了。」

「可不是，这个我倒忘了。」

「你自然是会忘的。」

===Original===
“Please be more respectful, Miss Hall,” he snapped. “Somebody might overhear you and tell my wife.”

“Which one?” she mocked.

“Shut up,” he blurted, touched in a tender spot, “and try to figure out some way for us to get hold of some money.”

“You must think I’m Einstein,” she said, coming up and perching herself on the edge of his desk.

“Well, if we don’t get some operating expenses I won’t be able to obtain money to pay your salary,” he warned.

===Chinese===
「请放尊重些，霍尔小姐，」他厉声道，「叫人听见了，跑去告诉我老婆怎么办。」

「告诉哪一位呀？」她揶揄道。

「闭嘴，」他冲口而出——这话正戳到痛处——「想法子给咱们弄点钱来才是正经。」

「你准拿我当爱因斯坦了，」她说着走过来，一屁股坐在他桌沿上。

「哼，要是咱们筹不到办公经费，我可就没钱给你发薪水了。」他警告道。

===Original===
“The old songs are the best songs,” she wisecracked.

“Oh, come now, Violet,” he remonstrated, pawing her buttock, “let’s be serious.”

“After all these years!” she declared, switching away.

In desperation, he eased his bulk out of the creaking swivel chair, reached for his hat and overcoat and shuffled out of the office. He walked to the curb to hail a taxicab but reconsidered when he recalled that a worn half-dollar was the extent of his funds. Sighing heavily, he trudged the two blocks to the telegraph office and sent a long day letter to Henry Givens, Imperial Grand Wizard of the Knights of Nordica—collect.

===Chinese===
「老歌才是最好听的歌嘛。」她俏皮地顶了一句。

「哦，好了好了，维奥莱特（Violet），」他劝道，一只手在她屁股上摸了一把，「咱们严肃点。」

「这么多年了，还是这一套！」她撂下一句，扭着腰肢走开了。

走投无路之下，他从那把吱呀作响的转椅里挪出庞大的身躯，抓起帽子和大衣，拖着步子出了办公室。他走到路边想叫辆出租汽车，转念一想，全部家当不过一枚磨旧了的半美元，便打消了念头。他长叹一声，拖着沉重的步子走过两个街区来到电报局，给北欧骑士团「帝国大巫师」（Imperial Grand Wizard）亨利·吉文斯（Henry Givens）发去一封长长的昼间电报——由收报人付费。

===Original===
“Well, have you figured it out?” asked Violet when he barged into his office again.

“Yes, I just sent a wire to Givens,” he replied.

“But he’s a nigger-hater, isn’t he?” was her surprised comment.

“You want your salary, don’t you?” he inquired archly.

“I have for the past month.”

“Well, then, don’t ask foolish questions,” he snapped.

===Chinese===
「怎么样，想出法子了吗？」维奥莱特见他再冲进办公室，问道。

「想出了，我刚给吉文斯拍了封电报。」他答道。

「可他不是个恨黑鬼入骨的家伙吗？」她吃惊地评了一句。

「你不是想要你的薪水吗？」他狡黠地反问。

「想要了一个月了。」

「那就好，就别问傻问题啦。」他没好气地说。

## VI / 六

===Original===
Two important events took place on Easter Sunday, 1934. The first was a huge mass meeting in the brand new reinforced concrete auditorium of the Knights of Nordica for the double purpose of celebrating the first anniversary of the militant secret society and the winning of the millionth member. The second event was the wedding of Helen Givens and Matthew Fisher, Grand Exalted Giraw of the Knights of Nordica.

Rev. Givens, the Imperial Grand Wizard of the order, had never regretted that he had taken Fisher into the order and made him his right-hand man. The membership had grown by leaps and bounds, the treasury was bursting with money in spite of the Wizard's constant misappropriation of funds, the regalia factory was running night and day and the influence of the order was becoming so great that Rev. Givens was beginning to dream of a berth in the White House or near by.

For over six months the order had been publishing *The Warning*, an eight-page newspaper carrying lurid red headlines and poorly-drawn quarter-page cartoons, and edited by Matthew. The noble Southern working people purchased it eagerly, devouring and believing every word in it. Matthew, in 14-point, one-syllable word editorials painted terrifying pictures of the menace confronting white supremacy and the utter necessity of crushing it. Very cleverly he linked up the Pope, the Yellow Peril, the Alien Invasion and Foreign Entanglements with Black-No-More as devices of the Devil. He wrote with such blunt sincerity that sometimes he almost persuaded himself that it was all true.

As the money flowed in, Matthew's fame as a great organizer spread throughout the Southland, and he suddenly became the most desirable catch in the section. Beautiful women literally threw themselves at his feet, and, as a former Negro and thus well versed in the technique of amour, he availed himself of all offerings that caught his fancy.
===Chinese===
1934 年复活节，两件大事相继发生。头一件，是在北欧骑士团崭新的钢筋混凝土大礼堂里举行的盛大群众集会，为的是双重庆祝：一是这个好斗的秘密社团成立一周年，二是第一百万名会员的诞生。第二件大事，则是海伦·吉文斯与马修·费舍尔——北欧骑士团的至尊吉罗（Grand Exalted Giraw）——的婚礼。

吉文斯牧师——社团的帝国大巫师（Imperial Grand Wizard）——从不后悔把费舍尔拉进社团、让他当上自己的左膀右臂。会员人数突飞猛进；尽管大巫师时常挪用公款，金库里的钱还是胀得快要爆开；礼服工场日夜开工；社团的声势越来越浩大，以致吉文斯牧师开始做起美梦，想在白宫里谋个位子，退一步也要在白宫旁边安个差事。

六个多月以来，社团一直在出版《警告报》——一份八个版面的小报，通栏是触目惊心的血红标题，配着画得拙劣的四分之一版漫画，主编正是马修。南方可敬的劳动人民踊跃抢购，一个字一个字地囫囵吞下，句句信以为真。马修用 14 磅大字、清一色的单音节词写社论，把白人至上面临的重重威胁、以及把它碾个稀烂的绝对必要，描绘得毛骨悚然。他手段高明，把教皇、黄祸、外侨入侵、外国纠缠统统同"黑色终结"串成一气，说成是魔鬼的惯技。他写得那么直截了当、义形于色，有时候连他自己都差点信以为真。

随着金钱滚滚而来，马修作为一大组织奇才的名声传遍南方全境，转眼成了这一带人人争抢的头号金龟婿。漂亮女人当真一个个扑倒在他脚下；他呢，身为前黑人，于谈情说爱的门道无所不精，凡入眼的奉献一概照单全收。

===Original===
At the same time he was a frequent visitor to the Givens home, especially when Mrs. Givens, whom he heartily detested, was away. From the very first Helen had been impressed by Matthew. She had always longed for the companionship of an educated man, a scientist, a man of literary ability. Matthew to her mind embodied all of these. She only hesitated to accept his first offer of marriage two days after they met because she saw no indication that he had much, if any, money. She softened toward him as the Knights of Nordica treasury grew; and when he was able to boast of a million-dollar bank account, she agreed to marriage and accepted his ardent embraces in the meantime.

And so, before the yelling multitude of night-gowned Knights, they were united in holy wedlock on the stage of the new auditorium. Both, being newlyweds, were happy. Helen had secured the kind of husband she wanted, except that she regretted his association with what she called lowbrows; while Matthew had won the girl of his dreams and was thoroughly satisfied, except for a slight regret that her grotesque mother wasn't dead and some disappointment that his spouse was so much more ignorant than she was beautiful.

As soon as Matthew had helped to get the Knights of Nordica well under way with enough money flowing in to satisfy the avaricious Rev. Givens, he had begun to study ways and means of making some money on the side. He had power, influence and prestige and he intended to make good use of them. So he had obtained audiences individually with several of the leading business men of the Georgia capital.

He always prefaced his proposition by pointing out that the working people were never so contented, profits never so high and the erection of new factories in the city never so intensive; that the continued prosperity of Atlanta and of the entire South depended upon keeping labor free from Bolshevism, Socialism, Communism, Anarchism, trade unionism and other subversive movements. Such un-American philosophies, he insisted had ruined European countries and from their outposts in New York and other Northern cities were sending emissaries to seek a foothold in the South and plant the germ of discontent. When this happened, he warned gloomily, then farewell to high profits and contented labor. He showed copies of books and pamphlets which he had ordered from radical book stores in New York but which he asserted were being distributed to the prospect's employees.
===Chinese===
与此同时，他成了吉文斯家的常客，尤其是在他打心眼里厌恶的吉文斯太太出门的时候。打一开始，海伦就对马修另眼相看。她一向渴望身边有个受过教育的男人，一个科学家，一个有文采的男人。在她眼里，马修三者兼备。相识才两天，马修就头一回求婚；她之所以踌躇，只因看不出他究竟有没有几个钱。北欧骑士团的金库越鼓，她对马修越软；等到他能夸口银行里存着一百万美元，她便点头应下了婚事，并在此期间接受了他热烈的拥抱。

于是，在一群身穿长袍的骑士震天的呐喊声里，两人在新礼堂的舞台上结成了神圣姻缘。新婚燕尔，两人都很快活。海伦称心如意地嫁了个如意的丈夫，只遗憾他老跟她所谓的下里巴人厮混；马修则抱得了梦中佳人，十二分满足，只是微微遗憾她那位奇形怪状的老妈还没死，再有几失望——太太愚笨的程度，实在远远超过了她标致的程度。

马修帮着把北欧骑士团的摊子一支起来、滚滚的进项足以喂饱贪得无厌的吉文斯牧师，便着手琢磨另辟财路的门道。他手中有权，有影响，有名声，打算物尽其用。于是他逐一求得接见，会晤了佐治亚州首府的几位工商界巨头。

每回开讲，他总要先指明：劳动大众从来没有这样心满意足，利润从来没有这样高，城里新工厂的兴建从来没有这样热火朝天；亚特兰大和整个南方的持续繁荣，全赖使劳工远离布尔什维主义、社会主义、共产主义、无政府主义、工团主义以及其他种种颠覆运动。他一口咬定，这班非美学说早已毁掉了欧洲各国，如今正从盘踞纽约和北方其他城市的据点派出密使，想在南方觅一处立足之地，播下不满的祸种。他阴沉沉地警告：一旦让他们得逞，高利润、顺民劳工，统统再见。说罢，他捧出几本书和小册子请对方过目——那是他从纽约的激进书店订购来的，他却咬定它们正被人散发给这位老板手下的雇工。

===Original===
He then explained the difference between the defunct Ku Klux Klan and the Knights of Nordica. While both were interested in public morals, racial integrity and the threatened invasion of America by the Pope, his organization glimpsed its larger duty, the perpetuation of Southern prosperity by the stabilization of industrial relations. The Knights of Nordica, favored by increasing membership, was in a position to keep down all radicalism, he said, and then boldly asserted that Black-No-More was subsidized by the Russian Bolsheviks. Would the gentlemen help the work of the Nordicists along with a small contribution? They would and did. Whenever there was a slump in the flow of cash from this source, Matthew merely had his print shop run off a bale of Communistic tracts which his secret operatives distributed around in the mills and factories. Contributions would immediately increase.

Matthew had started this lucrative side enterprise none too soon. There was much unemployment in the city, wages were being cut and work speeded up. There was dissatisfaction and grumbling among the workers and a small percentage of them was in a mood to give ear to the half-dozen timid organizers of the conservative unions who were being paid to unionize the city but had as yet made no headway. A union might not be so bad after all.

The great mass of white workers, however, was afraid to organize and fight for more pay because of a deepset fear that the Negroes would take their jobs. They had heard of black labor taking the work of white labor under the guns of white militia, and they were afraid to risk it. They had first read of the activities of Black-No-More, Incorporated, with a secret feeling akin to relief but after the orators of the Knights of Nordica and the editorials of *The Warning* began to portray the menace confronting them, they forgot about their economic ills and began to yell for the blood of Dr. Crookman and his associates. Why, they began to argue, one couldn't tell who was who! Herein lay the fundamental cause of all their ills. Times were hard, they reasoned, because there were so many white Negroes in their midst taking their jobs and undermining their American standard of living. None of them had ever attained an American standard of living to be sure, but that fact never occurred to any of them. So they flocked to the meetings of the Knights of Nordica and night after night sat spellbound while Rev. Givens, who had finished the eighth grade in a one-room country school, explained the laws of heredity and spoke eloquently of the growing danger of black babies.

Despite his increasing wealth (the money came in so fast he could scarcely keep track of it), Matthew maintained close contact with the merchants and manufacturers. He sent out private letters periodically to prominent men in the Southern business world in which he told of the marked psychological change that had come over the working classes of the South since the birth of the K. of N. He told how they had been discontented and on the brink of revolution when his organization rushed in and saved the South. Unionism and such destructive nostrums had been forgotten, he averred, when *The Warning* had revealed the latest danger to the white race. Of course, he always added, such work required large sums of money and contributions from conservative, substantial and public-spirited citizens were ever acceptable. At the end of each letter there appeared a suggestive paragraph pointing out the extent to which the prosperity of the New South was due to its "peculiar institutions" that made the worker race conscious instead of class conscious, and that with the passing of these "peculiar institutions" would also pass prosperity. This reasoning proved very effective, financially speaking.
===Chinese===
接着，他阐明已经寿终正寝的三K党同北欧骑士团的分别。两者固然都热心公共道德、种族纯正，都防备着教皇对美国的入侵威胁；可他这个社团的目光更高远，看清了自己更大的天职：靠稳定劳资关系来永葆南方繁荣。他说，北欧骑士团会员日增，完全压得住一切激进主义；随后又放胆断言，"黑色终结"受着俄国布尔什维克的津贴。诸位先生可肯捐出一小笔款项，助北欧主义者一臂之力？他们肯，而且果真捐了。每逢这条财源的现款一告疲软，马修只消吩咐自己的印刷铺赶印一捆共产主义小册子，派密探们到纱厂工厂四处一撒，捐款立时便见回涨。

马修这门有利可图的副业，开张得正是时候。城里失业的一抓一大把，工资一削再削，活计越赶越紧。工人中间怨声载道，其中一小撮已经竖起耳朵，愿意听听那半打胆小如鼠的保守工会组织者的说项了——这班人拿着薪水，本说来要把全城组织成工会，至今却寸步未进。有个工会，到头来兴许也不算太坏。

可是，广大白人工人不敢组织起来为加工资而斗，只因心里扎了根似的害怕黑人抢走他们的差事。他们听说过黑人工人在白人民团的枪口底下夺走白人的活计，不敢去冒那个险。起初读到"黑色终结"公司的种种活动，他们心底还暗暗松了口气，颇近于宽慰；可等北欧骑士团的演说家们和《警告报》的社论开始描绘起大祸临头，他们就把自己的经济疾苦忘了个精光，扯着嗓子喊叫，非要克鲁克曼大夫一伙的性命不可。可不是嘛，他们开始论证：如今谁是谁都认不出来啦！这正是万祸之源。他们的道理是：世道艰难，就因为身边挤满了白皮黑人，抢他们的饭碗，糟蹋他们美国式的生活水准。诚然，他们哪一个也不曾过上过什么美国式的生活水准，可这一层从没在他们任何人脑子里转过。于是他们蜂拥去北欧骑士团的集会，一夜又一夜听得魂儿都出了窍：只在乡下一间屋里的小学堂念完八年级的吉文斯牧师，给他们大讲遗传定律，又口若悬河，痛陈黑娃娃日益迫近的危险。

尽管家业越滚越大（钱来得太快，他简直来不及记），马修仍同商人厂主们过从甚密。他定期向南方工商界的头面人物发出私信，告知自北欧骑士团（K. of N.）问世以来，南方劳动阶级的心理发生了怎样显著的变化。他说，他们本来如何满腹怨气、革命已到边沿，幸得他的组织火速杀到，救下了南方。他一口保证，《警告报》揭出白人种族面临的最新危险之后，工团主义以及诸如此类的虎狼偏方便被抛到了九霄云外。当然，他照例补上一句：这般事业需款甚巨，保守、殷实、热心公益的公民解囊相助，无任欢迎。每封信的末尾，总缀着一段意味深长的文字，点明新南方的繁荣在多大程度上拜它的"特殊制度"所赐——正是这些制度叫工人有了种族觉悟，而没有阶级觉悟；这些"特殊制度"一旦消亡，繁荣也就随之东流。就进账而论，这套道理实在灵验得很。

===Original===
Matthew's great success as an organizer and his increasing popularity was not viewed by Rev. Givens with equanimity. The former evangelist knew that everybody of intelligence in the upper circles of the order realized that the growth and prosperity of the Knights of Nordica was largely due to the industry, efficiency and intelligence of Matthew. He had been told that many people were saying that Fisher ought to be Imperial Grand Wizard instead of Grand Exalted Giraw.

Givens had the ignorant man's fear and suspicion of anybody who was supposedly more learned than he. His position, he felt, was threatened, and he was decidedly uneasy. He neither said nor did anything about it, but he fretted a great deal to his wife, much to her annoyance. He was consequently overjoyed when Matthew asked him for Helen's hand, and gave his consent with alacrity. When the marriage was consummated, he saw his cup filled to overflowing and no clouds on the horizon. The Knights of Nordica was safe in the family.

One morning a week or two after his wedding, Matthew was sitting in his private office, when his secretary announced a caller, one B. Brown. After the usual delay staged for the purpose of impressing all visitors, Matthew ordered him in. A short, plump, well-dressed, soft-spoken man entered and greeted him respectfully. The Grand Exalted Giraw waved to a chair and the stranger sat down. Suddenly, leaning over close to Matthew, he whispered, "Don't recognize me, do you Max?"
===Chinese===
马修当组织家的莫大成功和扶摇直上的声望，吉文斯牧师瞧在眼里，心里可不大是滋味。这位昔日的福音布道者心里明白，社团上层凡有几分头脑的，都看得出北欧骑士团的兴旺发达，大半靠的是马修的勤勉、干练和才智。有人向他通风报信：外头好些人都在讲，该当帝国大巫师的是费舍尔，而不是什么至尊吉罗。

吉文斯怀着无知之辈对一切"比自己有学问的人"通有的畏惧和猜疑。他觉得自己那把交椅受了威胁，着实忐忑。他嘴上不提，手上不动，私下却对着老婆长吁短叹、唠叨个没完，烦得她不胜其苦。因此，马修上门求娶海伦时，他真是喜从天降，痛痛快快一口应承。等到婚事落定，他只觉福杯满溢，天边万里无云。北欧骑士团，总归是肥水不流外人田了。

婚后一两个礼拜的一个上午，马修正坐在私家办公室里，秘书通报有客来访，来人名叫B·布朗。照例摆足了让访客干等的架子、好叫人人对他肃然起敬之后，马修吩咐请进。进来的是个矮墩墩的胖子，衣冠楚楚，说话轻声细气，恭恭敬敬向他问安。至尊吉罗朝椅子一挥手，来客便坐下了。冷不防，那人俯身凑近马修，悄悄地说："认不出我了吧，马克斯？"

===Original===
The Grand Giraw paled and started. "Who are you?" he whispered hoarsely. How in the devil did this man know him? He peered at him sharply.

The newcomer grinned. "Why it's me, Bunny Brown, you big sap!"

"Well, cut my throat!" Matthew exclaimed in amazement. "Boy, is it really you?" Bunny's black face had miraculously bleached. He seemed now more chubby and cherubic than ever.

"It aint my brother," said Bunny with his familiar beam.
===Chinese===
大吉罗脸色一白，浑身一震。"你是什么人？"他声音嘶哑地悄声问。这家伙到底是打哪儿认得他的？他狠狠地逼视对方。

来客咧嘴一笑。"哎呀是我呀，邦尼·布朗，你这大傻瓜！"

"好家伙，一刀宰了我吧！"马修惊叫起来。"乖乖，真是你？"邦尼的黑脸奇迹般地漂白了，如今瞧着比先前越发白胖，越发像个圆脸蛋的胖天使。

"如假包换，不是我还能是谁。"邦尼照例咧开那张熟悉的笑脸。

===Original===
"Bunny, where've you been all this time? Why didn't you come on down here when I wrote you? You must've been in jail."

"Mind reader! That's just where I've been," declared the former bank clerk.

"What for? Gambling?"

"No: Rambling."
===Chinese===
"邦尼，这么久你上哪儿去了？我写信叫你下来，你怎么没来？你准是蹲了班房。"

"你真是会读心术！我正是在那儿来着。"这位昔日的银行职员宣称。

"犯了什么事？赌博？"

"不是赌博，是'赌摸'。"

===Original===
"What do you mean: Rambling?" asked the puzzled Matthew.

"Just what I said, Big Boy. Got to rambling around with a married woman. Old story: husband came in unexpectedly and I had to crown him. The fire escape was slippery and I slipped. Couldn't run after I hit the ground and the flatfoot nabbed me. Got a lucky break in court or I wouldn't be here."

"Was it a white woman?" asked the Grand Exalted Giraw.

"She wasn't black," said Bunny.
===Chinese===
"你说的'赌摸'是什么意思？"马修给闹糊涂了。

"就是我说的意思呗，大个子。跟一个有夫之妇到处'摸'来'摸'去。老掉牙的故事：丈夫冷不防回家撞见，我只好敲了他一家伙。防火梯太滑，我一出溜滑了下去。着了地就跑不动，让警察一把薅住。庭上走了运，不然今天也到不了这儿。"

"那女人是白人吗？"至尊吉罗问。

"反正她不是黑人。"邦尼说。

===Original===
"It's a good thing you weren't black, too!"

"Our minds always ran in the same channels," Bunny commented.

"Got any jack?" asked Matthew.

"Is it likely?"
===Chinese===
"幸好你自己也不是黑的了！"

"咱俩的心思向来走在一条道上。"邦尼评道。

"身上带票子没有？"马修问。

"你看像有吗？"

===Original===
"Do you want a job?"

"No, I prefer a position."

"Well, I think I can fix you up here for about five grand to begin with," said Matthew.

"Santa Claus! What do I have to do: assassinate the President?"
===Chinese===
"想找份活儿干吗？"

"不，我想要的是一份职位。"

"呃，我想我这儿能给你安顿一个，起薪约莫五千块。"马修说。

"我的圣诞老人呐！那我得干什么——去刺杀总统么？"

===Original===
"No, kidder; just be my right-hand man. You know, follow me through thick and thin."

"All right, Max; but when things get too thick, I'm gonna thin out."

"For Christ's sake don't call me Max," cautioned Matthew.

"That's your name, aint it?"
===Chinese===
"不，小滑头；只消当我左膀右臂。你知道的，有福同享，有难同当。"

"行啊，马克斯；不过'难'要是当得太狠，我的'福'可就要先享了。"

"看在基督面上，别叫我马克斯。"马修告诫道。

"那不是你的名字吗？"

===Original===
"No, simp. Them days has gone forever. It's Matthew Fisher now. You go pulling that Max stuff and I'll have to answer more questions than a traffic officer."

"Just think," mused Bunny. "I been reading about you right along in the papers but until I recognized your picture in last Sunday's paper I didn't know who you were. Just how long have you been in on this graft?"

"Ever since it started."

"Say not so! You must have a wad of cash salted away by this time."
===Chinese===
"不，蠢货。那段日子一去不复返啦。本人如今叫马修·费舍尔。你再'马克斯'长'马克斯'短的，我得应对的盘问能比交通警察还多。"

"想想看，"邦尼沉吟道，"我在报上一直读着你的新闻，可直到上个礼拜天才从照片上认出你来。这门肥缺你干了多久了？"

"打一开始就在里头。"

"不至于吧！到这会儿你私藏的现款总得有一大捆了。"

===Original===
"Well, I'm not appealing for charity," Matthew smiled sardonically.

"How many squaws you got now?"

"Only one, Bunny—regular."

"What's matter, did you get too old?" chided his friend.
===Chinese===
"哼，我又犯不上求人施舍。"马修皮笑肉不笑。

"你如今养了几个娘们？"

"就一个，邦尼——正经八百的。"

"怎么回事，你老得不中用了？"朋友揶揄道。

===Original===
"No, I got married."

"Well, that's the same thing. Who's the unfortunate woman?"

"Old man Givens' girl."

"Judas Priest! You got in on the ground floor didn't you?"
===Chinese===
"不，我结婚了。"

"嗐，那还不是一码事。那位不幸的女人是谁？"

"老吉文斯的千金。"

"我的老天爷！你可真是抢了头排、抄了底儿啦！"

===Original===
"I didn't miss. Bunny, old scout, she's the same girl that turned me down that night in the Honky Tonk," Matthew told him with satisfaction.

"Well, hush my mouth! This sounds like a novel," Bunny chuckled.

"Believe it or not, papa, it's what God loves," Matthew grinned.

"Well, you lucky hound! Getting white didn't hurt you none."
===Chinese===
"我这回没打空。邦尼，老伙计，她就是那晚在欢乐调俱乐部把我一口回绝的那位姑娘。"马修满心得意地对他说。

"哎哟喂，快捂上我的嘴！这简直跟小说似的。"邦尼抿嘴暗笑。

"信不信由你，老爹，真事才合上帝的口味。"马修咧嘴一笑。

"嘿，你这条走运的猎狗！变白可一点没亏待你。"

===Original===
"Now listen, Bunny," said Matthew, dropping to a more serious tone, "from now on you're private secretary to the Grand Exalted Giraw; that's me."

"What's a Giraw?"

"I can't tell you; I don't know myself. Ask Givens sometime. He invented it but if he can explain it I'll give you a grand."

"When do I start to work? Or rather, when do I start drawing money?"
===Chinese===
"听着，邦尼，"马修收起玩笑，换上正经些的口气，"从今往后，你就是至尊吉罗的私人秘书了；也就是我的秘书。"

"吉罗是个什么官儿？"

"这我说不上来；我自己也不明白。你哪天去问吉文斯。这词是他造的，他要是能解释清楚，我输你一千块。"

"那我什么时候上工？或者说得更确切些，什么时候开领钞票？"

===Original===
"Right now, Old Timer. Here's a century to get you fixed up. You eat dinner with me tonight and report to me in the morning."

"Fathers above!" said Bunny. "Dixie must be heaven."

"It'll be hell for you if these babies find you out; so keep your nose clean."

"Watch me, Mr. Giraw."
===Chinese===
"现在就上，老爷子。给，一张一百的，拿去把自己打点利索。今晚陪我吃饭，明早来报到。"

"天上的列祖列宗呐！"邦尼说，"迪克西（南方）准是天堂。"

"这帮家伙要是查出你的老底，这儿对你就成了地狱；所以放规矩点，别惹是非。"

"瞧我的吧，吉罗大人。"

===Original===
"Now listen, Bunny. You know Santop Licorice, don't you?"

"Who doesn't know that hippo?"

"Well, we've had him on our payroll since December. He's fighting Beard, Whooper, Spelling and that crowd. He was on the bricks and we helped him out. Got his paper to appearing regularly, and all that sort of thing."

"So the old crook sold out the race, did he?" cried the amazed Bunny.
===Chinese===
"再听着，邦尼。你认得桑托普·利科里斯（Santop Licorice）吧？"

"谁不认识那头河马？"

"呃，打十二月起，他就在咱们的工资单上挂着。他在跟比尔德、胡珀、斯佩林那帮人斗。他当时穷途潦倒，是咱们拉扯了他一把，让他的报纸得以照常出版，诸如此类。"

"这么说，那老骗子把种族给卖啦？"邦尼大吃一惊，嚷了起来。

===Original===
"Hold that race stuff, you're not a shine anymore. Are you surprised that he sold out? You're actually becoming innocent," said Matthew.

"Well, what about the African admiral?" Bunny asked.

"This: In a couple of days I want you to run up to New York and look around and see if his retention on the payroll is justified. I got a hunch that nobody is bothering about his paper or what he says, and if that's true we might as well can him; I can use the jack to better advantage."

"Listen here, Boy, this thing is running me nuts. Here you are fighting this Black-No-More, and so is Beard, Whooper, Gronne, Spelling and the rest of the Negro leaders, yet you have Licorice on the payroll to fight the same people that are fighting your enemy. This thing is more complicated than a flapper's past."
===Chinese===
"少跟我提种族不种族的，你已经不是黑炭头了。他出卖种族，你还吃惊？你倒真是越长越天真了。"马修说。

"那么，那位'非洲海军上将'呢？"邦尼问。

"是这么回事：过一两天，我要你上纽约跑一趟，四下里瞧瞧，看他挂在工资单上究竟还值不值。我有股预感：压根没人在乎他的报纸，也没人在乎他说什么；果真如此，索性把他炒掉，那笔钱我还能派更大的用场。"

"我说老兄，这套把戏把我脑子搅成一锅粥了。你在这儿跟'黑色终结'斗，比尔德、胡珀、格罗恩、斯佩林和其余黑人领袖也在斗；可你偏把利科里斯养在工资单上，让他去斗那些正跟你仇敌作对的人。这里头的门道，比摩登少女的情史还复杂。"

===Original===
"Simple, Bunny, simple. Reason why you can't understand it is because you don't know anything about high strategy."

"High what?" asked Bunny.

"Never mind, look it up at your leisure. Now you can savvy the fact that the sooner these spades are whitened the sooner this graft will fall through, can't you?"

"Righto," said his friend.
===Chinese===
"简单，邦尼，简单得很。你所以闹不明白，就因为你对'高阶战略'一窍不通。"

"高阶什么？"邦尼问。

"别管，得空自己查去。那你如今总该想通这个理儿了吧：这帮黑崽子变白变得越快，这门肥缺就垮得越快，对不对？"

"对喽。"他的朋友说。

===Original===
"Well, the longer we can make the process, the longer we continue to drag down the jack. Is that clear?"

"As a Spring day."

"You're getting brighter by the minute, old man," jeered Matthew.

"Coming from you, that's no compliment."
===Chinese===
"呃，咱们能把这道工序拖多长，咱们就能把钱往腰包里捞多久。明白了吗？"

"明白得跟春天的天色一样。"

"你这一分钟比一分钟开窍啊，老伙计。"马修嘲弄道。

"这话打你嘴里出来，可算不得恭维。"

===Original===
"As I was saying, the longer it takes, the longer we last. It's my business to see that it lasts a long time but neither do I want it to stop because that also would be disastrous."

Bunny nodded: "You're a wise egg!"

"Thanks, that makes it unanimous. Well, I don't want my side to get such an upper hand that it will put the other side out of business, or vice versa. What we want is a status quo."

"Gee, you've got educated since you've been down here with these crackers."
===Chinese===
"我方才说的是，拖得越久，咱们撑得越久。让这事长长久久是我的本分；可我也不愿它戛然而止——那同样是灭顶之灾。"

邦尼点点头："你可真是颗聪明蛋！"

"多谢，那咱俩可就想到一块儿去了。呃，我不愿我这一边占尽上风，把那一边挤得关张，反过来也一样。咱们要的是维持现状。"

"嚯，跟这帮南方白佬混了这些日子，你还真有学问了。"

===Original===
"You flatter them, Bunny; run along now. I'll have my car come by your hotel to bring you to dinner."

"Thanks for the compliment, old man, but I'm staying at the Y.M.C.A. It's cheaper," laughed Bunny.

"But is it safer?" kidded Matthew, as his friend withdrew.
===Chinese===
"你太抬举他们了，邦尼；去忙你的吧。我派车去你旅馆接你来吃晚饭。"

"多谢抬举，老伙计，可我住的是基督教青年会招待所，便宜。"邦尼笑道。

"可那儿太平吗？"马修打趣道，目送朋友退出房去。

===Original===
Two days later Bunny Brown left for New York on a secret mission. Not only was he to spy on Santop Licorice and see how effective his work was, but he was also to approach Dr. Shakespeare Agamemnon Beard, Dr. Napoleon Wellington Jackson, Rev. Herbert Gronne, Col. Mortimer Roberts, Prof. Charles Spelling, and the other Negro leaders with a view to getting them to speak to white audiences for the benefit of the Knights of Nordica. Matthew already knew that they were in a precarious economic situation since they now had no means of income, both the black masses and the philanthropic whites having deserted them. Their white friends, mostly Northern plutocrats, felt that the race problem was being satisfactorily solved by Black-No-More, Incorporated, and so did the Negroes. Bunny's job was to convince them that it was better to lecture for the K. of N. and grow fat than to fail to get chances to lecture to Negroes who weren't interested in what they said anyhow. The Grand Exalted Giraw had a personal interest in these Negro leaders. He realized that they were too old or too incompetent to make a living except by preaching and writing about the race problem, and since they had lost their influence with the black masses, they might be a novelty to introduce to the K. of N. audiences. He felt that their racial integrity talks would click with the crackers. They knew more about it too than any of his regular speakers, he realized.

As the train bearing Bunny pulled into the station in Charlotte, he bought an evening paper. The headline almost knocked him down:

> **Wealthy White Girl Has Negro Baby**

He whistled softly and muttered to himself, "Business picks up from now on." He thought of Matthew's marriage and whistled again.

---
===Chinese===
两天后，邦尼·布朗身负秘密使命动身去了纽约。他不但要暗中盯住桑托普·利科里斯，看看他的营生究竟有多少成效，还要去接洽莎士比亚·阿伽门农·比尔德博士、拿破仑·威灵顿·杰克逊博士、赫伯特·格罗恩牧师、莫蒂默·罗伯茨上校、查尔斯·斯佩林教授以及其他黑人领袖，设法请他们面向白人听众发表演说，为北欧骑士团捞取好处。马修早已知道，这班人眼下处境岌岌可危：黑人群众和乐善好施的白人两头都弃他们而去，收入来源断了个干净。他们的白人朋友大都是北方的豪阀，觉得种族问题正由"黑色终结"公司解决得妥妥帖帖；黑人们也作如是想。邦尼的差事，就是要说服他们：与其捞不着机会去向那些压根不听他们废话的黑人演讲，倒不如替北欧骑士团讲讲，养得脑满肠肥。这位至尊吉罗对这班黑人领袖，另有几分切身的盘算。他清楚，这班人除却拿种族问题布道、著文，年岁太大，本事太小，别无糊口之途；而他们既已失了号令黑人群众的号召力，正好当作一件新鲜玩意儿，引荐给北欧骑士团的听众。他寻思，他们那一套"种族纯正"的高论，必能唱进南方白佬的心坎；再说他也明白，论这门学问，他们比他手底下任何一位固定演说员都懂得多。

载着邦尼的列车驶进夏洛特车站时，他买了一份晚报。版面上的大标题险些把他震倒在地：

> **豪门白人小姐产下黑婴**

他低低打了个呼哨，喃喃自语："这买卖从今往后要红火喽。"他想到马修的婚事，又打了个呼哨。

---

===Original===
From that time on there were frequent reports in the daily press of white women giving birth to black babies. In some cases, of course, the white women had recently become white but the blame for the tar-brushed offspring in the public mind always rested on the shoulders of the father, or rather, of the husband. The number of cases continued to increase. All walks of life were represented. For the first time the prevalence of sexual promiscuity was brought home to the thinking people of America. Hospital authorities and physicians had known about it in a general way but it had been unknown to the public.

The entire nation became alarmed. Hundreds of thousands of people, North and South, flocked into the Knights of Nordica. The real white people were panic-stricken, especially in Dixie. There was no way, apparently, of telling a real Caucasian from an imitation one. Every stranger was viewed with suspicion, which had a very salutory effect on the standard of sex morality in the United States. For the first time since 1905, chastity became a virtue. The number of petting parties, greatly augmented by the development of aviation, fell off amazingly. One must play safe, the girls argued.

The holidays of traveling salesmen, business men and fraternal delegates were made less pleasant than of yore. The old orgiastic days in the big cities seemed past for all time. It also suddenly began to dawn upon some men that the pretty young thing they had met at the seashore and wanted to rush to the altar might possibly be a whitened Negress; and young women were almost as suspicious. Rapid-fire courtships and gin marriages declined. Matrimony at last began to be approached with caution. Nothing like this situation had been known since the administration of Grover Cleveland.
===Chinese===
从那时起，日报上频频登出白人妇女生下黑婴的报道。当然，其中有些白人妇女本身就是才变白不久的，可在公众的心目里，这些"刷了黑漆"的小生命，罪责照例一概扣在父亲头上——说得确切些，是扣在丈夫头上。这类案子有增无已，三教九流、各行各业，无一不有。性放纵之泛滥，头一回真真切切地敲打在美国有识之士的心上。医院当局和大夫们对此早有个大谱，只是公众一直蒙在鼓里。

整个国家慌了神。成千上万的民众，不分南北，一齐涌进北欧骑士团。如假包换的白人吓破了胆，在迪克西尤其如此。明摆着，真高加索人和假高加索人，谁也没法分辨。个个陌生人都叫人疑神疑鬼，这对美国的性道德水准，倒产生了极见疗效的作用。自 1905 年以来，贞操头一回重新成了美德。自从航空事业发达以来激增的搂搂抱抱派对，数量骤减得惊人。姑娘们的道理是：稳妥第一。

旅行推销员、生意人和兄弟会代表们的"寻欢假期"，不似往昔那般受用。大城市里纵欲狂欢的好时光，看来一去永不复返。有些男人也蓦然省悟：自己在海滨结识、恨不得立刻拽上圣坛的那位小可人儿，保不齐是个变白的黑人姑娘；年轻女子们的疑心也几无二致。急管繁弦式的求爱和杜松子酒里泡成的婚姻，双双见少。婚姻大事，终于开始有人小心翼翼地对待了。自格罗弗·克利夫兰当政以来，还不曾见过这等光景。

===Original===
Black-No-More, Incorporated, was not slow to seize upon this opportunity to drum up more business. With one hundred sanitariums going full blast from Coast to Coast, it now announced in full page advertisements in the daily press that it was establishing lying-in hospitals in the principal cities where all prospective mothers could come to have their babies, and that whenever a baby was born black or mulatto, it would immediately be given the 24-hour treatment that permanently turned black infants white. The country breathed easier, particularly the four million Negroes who had become free because white.

In a fortnight Bunny Brown returned. Over a quart of passable rye, the two friends discussed his mission.

"What about Licorice?" asked Matthew.
===Chinese===
