"黑色终结"公司手脚飞快，立刻抓住这个机会大吹大擂地招揽生意。旗下一百家疗养院从东海岸到西海岸开足马力，如今又在日报上以整版广告宣告：公司正在各主要城市设立产科医院，凡准妈妈皆可前来分娩；婴儿不论落地时是黑是混血，一律立即施以 24 小时疗法，教黑肤婴儿永久变白。全国上下呼吸都松快了，尤其是那四百万因变白而获得自由的黑人。

两星期后，邦尼·布朗回来了。两人就着一夸脱还过得去的裸麦威士忌，合计起他此行的收获。

"利科里斯怎么样？"马修问。

===Original===
"Useless. You ought to give him the gate. He's taking your jack but he isn't doing a thing but getting your checks and eating regularly. His followers are scarcer than Jews in the Vatican."

"Well, were you able to talk business with any of the Negro leaders?"

"Couldn't find any of them. Their offices are all closed and they've moved away from the places where they used to live. Broke, I suppose."

"Did you inquire for them around Harlem?"
===Chinese===
"废物一个。你该请他卷铺盖。他吃着你的粮，却屁事不干，光顾着收你的支票、按顿吃饭。他的追随者比梵蒂冈里的犹太人还稀罕。"

"那么，黑人领袖里头，有谁让你谈上了正事？"

"一个也没找着。他们的事务所全都大门紧闭，人也从原先住的老地方搬走了。想必是破产了。"

"你在哈莱姆一带打听过他们没有？"

===Original===
"What was the use? All of the Negroes around Harlem nowadays are folks that have just come there to get white; the rest of them left the race a long time ago. Why, Boy, darkies are as hard to find on Lenox Avenue now as they used to be in Tudor City."

"What about the Negro newspapers? Are any of them running still?"

"Nope, they're a thing of the past. Shines are too busy getting white to bother reading about lynching, crime and peonage," said Bunny.

"Well," said Matthew, "it looks as if old Santop Licorice is the only one of the old gang left."
===Chinese===
"打听有什么用？眼下哈莱姆一带的黑人，全是刚跑来变白的；其余那些人，早就脱离种族啦。哎呀老大，如今黑崽子在莱诺克斯大道上可难找了，就跟从前在都铎城一样难找。"

"黑人报纸呢？还有哪家在出？"

"没啦，都成了明日黄花。黑炭头们忙着变白还忙不过来，哪有闲工夫去读什么私刑、犯罪、债役的消息。"邦尼说。

"这么着，"马修说，"老家伙们中间，就剩下老桑托普·利科里斯一个了。"

===Original===
"Yeah, and he won't be black long, now that you're cutting him off the payroll."

"I think he could make more money staying black."

"How do you figure that out?" asked Bunny.

"Well, the dime museums haven't closed down, you know," said Matthew.
===Chinese===
"可不，如今你把他从工资单上划掉，他这身黑皮也长不了啦。"

"依我看，他留着黑皮肤反倒更能来钱。"

"这账你是怎么算的？"邦尼问。

"呃，你知道，十美分博物馆（dime museum，猎奇珍品馆）可还没关门呢。"马修说。

## VII / 七

===Original===
One June morning in 1934, Grand Exalted Giraw Fisher received a report from one of his secret operatives in the town of Paradise, South Carolina, saying:

> “The working people here are talking about going on strike next week unless Blickdoff and Hortzenboff, the owners of the Paradise Mill increase pay and shorten hours. The average wage is around fifteen dollars a week, the work day eleven hours. In the past week the company has speeded up the work so much that the help say they cannot stand the pace.
> “The owners are two Germans who came to this country after the war. They employ one thousand hands, own all of the houses in Paradise and operate all of the stores. Most of the hands belong to the Knights of Nordica and they want the organization to help them unionize. Am awaiting instructions.”

===Chinese===
1934 年 6 月的一个早晨，至尊大吉罗费舍尔（Grand Exalted Giraw Fisher）收到他安插在南卡罗来纳州天堂镇（Paradise）的一名密探呈上的报告，上面写道：

> “本地的劳工正在酝酿下周罢工，除非天堂棉纺厂（Paradise Mill）的厂主布利克多夫（Blickdoff）和霍岑博夫（Hortzenboff）给涨工资、缩短工时。这里平均周薪十五块上下，一个工日要干十一个钟头。过去一星期，厂里把活儿催得一天紧过一天，工人们都说这个节奏实在扛不住了。
> “厂主是两个战后才来本国的德国人。他们雇着一千名工人，天堂镇的房子全归他们所有，各家铺子也都由他们经营。大多数工人都加入了北欧骑士团（Knights of Nordica），想让组织帮他们组建工会。静候指示。”

===Original===
Matthew turned to Bunny and grinned. “Here’s more money,” he boasted, shaking the letter in his assistant’s face.

“What can you do about it?” that worthy inquired.

“What can I do? Well, Brother, you just watch my smoke. Tell Ruggles to get the plane ready,” he ordered. “We’ll fly over there at once.”

Two hours later Matthew’s plane sat down on the broad, close-clipped lawn in front of the Blickdoff-Hortzenboff cotton mill. Bunny and the Grand Giraw entered the building and walked to the office.

===Chinese===
马修（Matthew）转向邦尼，咧嘴一笑。“又来进账啦。”他好不得意，把那封信一直杵到助手脸跟前晃。

“这碍着你什么事了？”那位宝贝问道。

“碍着我什么？嘿，好兄弟，你就瞧我的吧。叫拉格尔斯（Ruggles）把飞机准备好，”他下令道，“咱们马上飞过去。”

两个钟头后，马修的飞机降落在布利克多夫—霍岑博夫棉纺厂前那片修剪得平平整整的宽阔草坪上。邦尼和大吉罗走进厂房，径直朝办公室走去。

===Original===
“Whom do you wish to see?” asked a clerk.

“Mr. Blickdoff, Mr. Hortzenboff or both; preferably both,” Matthew replied.

“And who’s calling?”

“The Grand Exalted Giraw of the Ancient and Honorable Order of the Knights of Nordica and his secretary,” boomed that gentleman. The awed young lady retired into an inner sanctum.

===Chinese===
“二位要见谁？”一个职员问。

“布利克多夫先生、霍岑博夫先生，或者两位一起见；最好两位一起。”马修答道。

“怎么通报二位？”

“古老尊贵的北欧骑士团至尊大吉罗，和他的秘书。”那位先生声如洪钟。小姑娘满脸敬畏，退进了里间密室。

===Original===
“That sure is some title,” commented Bunny in an undertone.

“Yes, Givens knows his stuff when it comes to that. The longer and sillier a title, the better the yaps like it.”

The young lady returned and announced that the two owners would be glad to receive the eminent Atlantan. Bunny and Matthew entered the office marked “Private.”

Hands were shaken, greetings exchanged and then Matthew got right down to business. He had received contributions from these two mill owners so to a certain extent they understood one another.

===Chinese===
“这头衔可真够唬人的。”邦尼低声嘀咕。

“可不是，论这个，吉文斯（Givens）是行家。头衔越长越蠢，乡巴佬们越是吃这一套。”

小姐回来说，两位厂主很高兴接见这位亚特兰大来的显要。邦尼和马修走进了那间标着“私人重地”的办公室。

握手，寒暄，随后马修直奔正题。这两位厂主都给他捐过款，所以在某种程度上，彼此是心照不宣的。

===Original===
“Gentlemen,” he queried, “is it true that your employees are planning to strike next week?”

“So ve haff heardt,” puffed the corpulent, undersized Blickdoff.

“Well, what are you going to do about it?”

“De uszual t’ing, uff coarse,” replied Hortzenboff, who resembled a beer barrel on stilts.

“You can’t do the usual thing,” warned Matthew. “Most of these people are members of the Knights of Nordica. They are looking to us for protection and we mean to give it to them.”

===Chinese===
“二位先生，”他开口问道，“听说你们的雇工打算下周罢工，可有此事？”

“我们确实有所耳闻。”肥胖矮小的布利克多夫呼哧呼哧地说。

“那你们打算拿它怎么办？”

“照老一套办，自然喽。”霍岑博夫答道。这位老兄的模样，活像一只踩着高跷的啤酒桶。

“老一套可使不得了。”马修提出警告，“这些人多半是北欧骑士团的会员。他们指望着我们出来保护，我们也决意要保护他们。”

===Original===
“Vy ve t’ought you vas favorable,” exclaimed Blickdoff.

“Und villing to be reasonable,” added Hortzenboff.

“That’s true,” Matthew agreed, “but you’re squeezing these people too hard.”

“But ve can’t pay dem any more,” protested the squat partner. “Vot ve gonna do?”

“Oh, you fellows can’t kid me, I know you’re coining the jack; but if you think its worth ten grand to you, I think I can adjust matters,” the Grand Giraw stated.

“Ten t’ousand dollars?” the two mill men gasped.

===Chinese===
“我们原当您是向着我们这边的呀！”布利克多夫嚷起来。

“还当您愿意通情达理呢。”霍岑博夫补了一句。

“这倒不假。”马修表示同意，“可你们把这帮人榨得太狠了。”

“可我们实在没法再多付给他们啦。”矮胖的合伙人抗议道，“那叫我们怎么办嘛？”

“哦，二位可糊弄不了我，我知道你们正大把大把地捞钱；不过，要是你们觉得这事值一万块，我想我就能把事情摆平。”大吉罗说。

“一万块？！”两位厂主倒抽一口凉气。

===Original===
“You’ve got good ears,” Matthew assured them. “And if you don’t come across I’ll put the whole power of my organization behind your hands. Then it’ll cost you a hundred grand to get back to normal.”

The Germans looked at each other incredulously.

“Are you t’reatening us, Meester Fisher?” whined Blickdoff.

“You’ve got a good head at figuring out things, Blickdoff,” Matthew retorted, sarcastically.

“Suppose ve refuse?” queried the heavier Teuton.

===Chinese===
“二位耳朵尖得很哪。”马修给他们吃定心丸，“要是你们不肯掏钱，我就把我会里的全部力量调去给你们那些工人撑腰。到那时候，想恢复原样，就得花上十万块。”

两个德国人难以置信地面面相觑。

“你这是在威胁我们吗，费舍尔先生？”布利克多夫哀哀地哼唧。

“布利克多夫，你琢磨事儿的脑筋开窍得很嘛。”马修不阴不阳地回敬。

“那要是我们拒绝呢？”块头更大的那位条顿人问道。

===Original===
“Yeah, suppose you do. Can’t you imagine what’ll happen when I pull these people off the job?”

“Ve’ll call oudt de militia,” warned Blickdoff.

“Don’t make me laugh,” Matthew commented. “Half the militiamen are members of my outfit.”

The Germans shrugged their shoulders hopelessly while Matthew and Bunny enjoyed their confusion.

===Chinese===
“好啊，那你们就拒绝试试。我把这些人从活儿上通通撤下来，会有什么后果，二位想象得出来吗？”

“我们就把民兵调来！”布利克多夫放话威胁。

“别逗我笑了。”马修说，“民兵有一半都是我手下会里的人。”

两个德国人无可奈何地耸耸肩膀，马修和邦尼则满心受用地欣赏着他们的狼狈相。

===Original===
“How mutch you say you wandt?” asked Hortzenboff.

“Fifteen grand,” replied the Grand Giraw, winking at Bunny.

“Budt you joost said ten t’ousand a minute ago,” screamed Blickdoff, gesticulating.

“Well, it’s fifteen now,” said Matthew, “and it’ll be twenty grand if you babies don’t hurry up and make up your mind.”

Hortzenboff reached hastily for the big check book and commenced writing. In a moment he handed Matthew the check.

===Chinese===
“你刚才是说，要多少来着？”霍岑博夫问。

“一万五。”大吉罗答道，朝邦尼递了个眼色。

“可你一分钟前还说的是一万呀！”布利克多夫挥舞着双手尖叫起来。

“哦，现在是一万五了。”马修说，“要是你们小宝贝儿还不赶紧拿定主意，那就要涨到两万喽。”

霍岑博夫慌忙抓过那本大支票簿，唰唰写了起来。不一会儿，就把支票递给了马修。

===Original===
“Take this back to Atlanta in the plane,” ordered Matthew, handing the check to Bunny, “and deposit it. Safety first.” Bunny went out.

“You don’t act like you trust us,” Blickdoff accused.

“Why should I?” the ex-Negro retorted. “I’ll just stick around for a while and keep you two company. You fellows might change your mind and stop payment on that check.”

“Ve are honest men, Meester Fisher,” cried Hortzenboff.

“Now I’ll tell one,” sneered the Grand Giraw, seating himself and taking a handful of cigars out of a box on the desk.

===Chinese===
“把这个搭飞机带回亚特兰大去，”马修把支票塞给邦尼，吩咐道，“存进银行。安全第一。”邦尼出去了。

“你这做派，可不大像信任我们的样子。”布利克多夫控诉说。

“我凭什么信你们？”这位前黑人（ex-Negro）回敬道，“我就在这儿多盘桓几天，陪陪你们二位。保不齐你们会改了主意，把那张支票止付了呢。”

“我们是老实人呀，费舍尔先生！”霍岑博夫喊道。

“哟，这话可真新鲜。”大吉罗冷笑一声，一屁股坐下，从桌上的盒子里抓出一把雪茄。

===Original===
The following evening the drab, skinny, hollow-eyed mill folk trudged to the mass meeting called by Matthew in the only building in Paradise not owned by the company—the Knights of Nordica Hall. They poured into the ramshackle building, seated themselves on the wooden benches and waited for the speaking to begin.

They were a sorry lot, undernourished, bony, vacant-looking, and yet they had seen a dim light. Without suggestion or agitation from the outside world, from which they were almost as completely cut off as if they had been in Siberia, they had talked among themselves and concluded that there was no hope for them except in organization. What they all felt they needed was wise leadership, and they looked to the Knights of Nordica for it, since they were all members of it and there was no other agency at hand. They waited now expectantly for the words of wisdom and encouragement which they expected to hear fall from the lips of their beloved Matthew Fisher, who now looked down upon them from the platform with cynical humor mingled with disgust.

They had not long to wait. A tall, gaunt, mountaineer, who acted as chairman, after beseeching the mill hands to stand together like men and women, introduced the Grand Exalted Giraw.

===Chinese===
第二天傍晚，天堂镇那些衣着灰暗、瘦骨嶙峋、眼窝深陷的纺织工人，拖着沉重的步子赶来参加马修召集的群众大会。会场设在镇上唯一一栋不归公司所有的建筑——北欧骑士团会堂。他们涌进那座摇摇欲坠的屋子，在木板长凳上坐定，等着演讲开场。

他们是一副可怜相：营养不良、瘦得挂相、目光空茫茫的，然而他们毕竟望见了一线微光。外面的世界与他们隔绝得几乎如同置身西伯利亚，没有谁从外头递主意、搞鼓动，他们全靠自己互相合计，便断定了除了组织起来，再没有别的活路。人人都觉得，缺的是一位贤明的领路人，于是指望起北欧骑士团来，一来他们个个都是会员，二来眼前也没有别的门路。此刻他们满怀期待，只等敬爱的马修·费舍尔开口吐出智慧与鼓励的金玉良言；而此刻的马修，正站在讲台上俯视着他们，一脸冷嘲，混着厌恶。

没等多久。主席是个瘦高嶙峋的山里人，他先恳求纱厂工人们像顶天立地的男人女人一样团结起来，随后就把至尊大吉罗介绍上场。

===Original===
Matthew spoke forcefully and to the point. He reminded them that they were men and women; that they were free, white and twenty-one; that they were citizens of the United States; that America was their country as well as Rockefeller’s; that they must stand firm in the defense of their rights as working people; that the worker was worthy of his hire; that nothing should be dearer to them than the maintenance of white supremacy. He insinuated that even in their midst there probably were some Negroes who had been turned white by Black-No-More. Such individuals, he insisted made poor union material because they always showed their Negro characteristics and ran away in a crisis. Ending with a fervent plea for liberty, justice and a square deal, he sat down amid tumultuous applause. Eager to take advantage of their enthusiasm, the chairman began to call for members. Happily the people crowded around the little table in front of the platform to give their names and pay dues.

Swanson, the chairman and acknowledged leader of the militant element, was tickled with the results of the meeting. He slapped his thighs mountaineer fashion, shifted his chew of tobacco from the right cheek to the left, his pale blue eyes twinkling, and “allowed” to Matthew that the union would soon bring the Paradise Mill owners to terms. The Grand Exalted Giraw agreed.

Two days later, back in Atlanta, Matthew held a conference with a half-dozen of his secret operatives in his office. “Go to Paradise and do your stuff,” he commanded, “and do it right.”

The next day the six men stepped from the train in the little South Carolina town, engaged rooms at the local hotel and got busy. They let it be known that they were officials of the Knights of Nordica sent from Atlanta by the Grand Exalted Giraw to see that the mill workers got a square deal. They busied themselves visiting the three-room cottages of the workers, all of which looked alike, and talking very confidentially.

===Chinese===
马修讲得慷慨激昂，句句切中要害。他提醒大家：他们是有血有肉的男人女人；他们自由之身、白皮肤、年满二十一岁；他们是合众国的公民；美国既是洛克菲勒的美国，也是他们的美国；身为劳工，他们必须站稳脚跟，捍卫自己的权利；工人得工价是应当的；天底下没有什么比维护白人至上更要紧的了。他还旁敲侧击地说，说不定就在他们中间，就混着几个让「黑色终结」（Black-No-More）变白了的黑人。他一口咬定，这种人当工会会员是块废料，因为他们总免不了露出黑人的本性，一到节骨眼上就开溜。末了，他热切呼吁自由、正义和公道待遇，在雷鸣般的掌声中落了座。主席急于趁热打铁，当即开始征募会员。人们欢天喜地地围拢到讲台前的小桌子旁，报名的报名，交会费的交会费。

主席斯旺森（Swanson）是公认的好斗派头目，对大会的成果乐得合不拢嘴。他按山里人的做派拍着大腿，把嚼着的烟草从右腮挪到左腮，一双淡蓝的眼睛闪闪发亮，“放话”对马修说，这工会要不了多久就能治得天堂棉纺厂的厂主们低头认账。大吉罗深表赞同。

两天后，回到亚特兰大的马修在办公室里召见了他那五六名密探。“去天堂镇，施展你们的本事，”他发话道，“给我干得干净利落。”

第二天，这六个人在南卡罗来纳那座小镇下了火车，在当地旅馆订好房间，随即忙活起来。他们放出风声，说自己是北欧骑士团的干事，奉大吉罗之命专程从亚特兰大赶来，保障纱厂工人得到公道待遇。他们挨家走访工人们那些长得一个模样的三间小屋，神神秘秘地咬耳朵。

===Original===
In a day or so it began to be noised about that Swanson, leader of the radical element, was really a former Negro from Columbia. It happened that a couple of years previously he had lived in that city. Consequently he readily admitted that he had lived there when asked innocently by one of the strangers in the presence of a group of workers. When Swanson wasn’t looking, the questioner glanced significantly at those in the group.

That was enough. To the simple-minded workers Swanson’s admission was conclusive evidence that the charge of being a Negro was true. When he called another strike meeting, no one came except a few of Fisher’s men. The big fellow was almost ready to cry because of the unexplained falling away of his followers. When one of the secret operatives told him the trouble he was furious.

“Ah haint no damn nigger a-tall,” he shouted. “Ah’m a white man an’ kin prove hit!”

Unfortunately he could not prove to the satisfaction of his fellow workers that he was not a Negro. They were adamant. On the streets they passed him without speaking and they complained to the foremen at the mill that they didn’t want to work with a nigger. Broken and disheartened after a week of vain effort, Swanson was glad to accept carfare out of the vicinity from one of Matthew’s men who pretended to be sympathetic.

===Chinese===
不出一两天，风言风语便传开了：激进派的头头斯旺森，其实是个从哥伦比亚（Columbia）来的前黑人。偏巧前几年他真在那座城里住过。于是，当其中一个陌生人当着一群工人的面装傻发问时，他爽爽快快地承认自己在那儿住过。趁斯旺森没留神，发问的人朝在场众人意味深长地递眼色。

这就够了。在头脑简单的工人们看来，斯旺森既然招认了，“他是黑人”的指控便铁证如山。他再召集罢工大会时，除了费舍尔的几个眼线，谁也没来。这条大汉子眼见追随的人不明不白地作鸟兽散，急得几乎要哭出来。等一名密探点破其中原委，他登时火冒三丈。

“俺可不是什么该死的黑鬼，压根儿不是！”他吼道，“俺是白人，俺能证明！”

可惜的是，他没法把这件事证明到让工友们心服口服的地步。他们铁了心。在街上迎面撞见，谁也不跟他搭腔；他们还到厂里向工头告状，说不愿跟一个黑鬼一起干活。白忙活了一个星期，斯旺森心灰意冷，巴不得收下马修手下一人假装同情塞给他的车马费，离开这是非之地。

===Original===
With the departure of Swanson, the cause of the mill workers was dealt a heavy blow, but the three remaining ringleaders sought to carry on. The secret operatives of the Grand Exalted Giraw got busy again. One of the agitators was asked if it was true that his grandfather was a nigger. He strenuously denied the charge but being ignorant of the identity of his father he could not very well be certain about his grandfather. He was doomed. Within a week the other two were similarly discredited. Rumor was wafted abroad that the whole idea of a strike was a trick of smart niggers in the North who were in the pay of the Pope.

The erstwhile class conscious workers became terror-stricken by the specter of black blood. You couldn’t, they said, be sure of anybody any more, and it was better to leave things as they were than to take a chance of being led by some nigger. If the colored gentry couldn’t sit in the movies and ride in the trains with white folks, it wasn’t right for them to be organizing and leading white folks.

The radicals and laborites in New York City had been closely watching developments in Paradise ever since the news of the big mass meeting addressed by Matthew was broadcast by the Knights of Nordica news service. When it seemed that the mill workers were, for some mysterious reason, going to abandon the idea of striking, liberal and radical labor organizers were sent down to the town to see what could be done toward whipping up the spirit of revolt.

===Chinese===
斯旺森一走，纱厂工人的事业挨了当头一棒，可剩下三名头目还想接着干。大吉罗的密探们又忙碌起来。有人去问其中一个鼓动家：你爷爷是个黑鬼，可有此事？此人拼死拼活地喊冤否认，可他连亲爹是谁都闹不清，爷爷是谁自然更拿不准。他完蛋了。不出一个星期，另外两个也落得同样身败名裂。流言四处飘散，说罢工这整个主意，全是北方那帮精明黑鬼设下的圈套，而他们，是拿了教皇的钱办事的。

先前那些满脑子阶级觉悟的工人，如今被黑人血统的幽灵吓得魂飞魄散。他们说，如今谁都靠不住了，与其冒险让哪个黑鬼牵着鼻子走，还不如一切照旧。既然有色绅士们不能跟白人老爷们同坐电影院、同乘火车，那让他们来组织白人、领导白人，就更没有这个道理。

纽约的激进派和工运人士，自打北欧骑士团的新闻社把马修演讲那场大会的消息播发出去之后，就一直密切注视着天堂镇的动向。眼见纱厂工人不知中了什么邪，眼看要放弃罢工的念头，自由派和激进派的工运组织者便被派到镇上来，看能不能把反抗的精神重新煽动起来。

===Original===
The representative of the liberal labor organization arrived first and immediately announced a meeting in the Knights of Nordica Hall, the only obtainable place. Nobody came. The man couldn’t understand it. He walked out into the town square, approached a little knot of men and asked what was the trouble.

“Y’re from that there Harlem in N’Yawk, haint ye?” asked one of the villagers.

“Why yes, I live in Harlem. What about it?”

“Well, we haint a gonna have no damn nigger leadin’ us, an’ if ye know whut’s healthy fer yuh yo’ll git on away f’um here,” stated the speaker.

===Chinese===
自由派工运组织的代表先到了，随即宣布在北欧骑士团会堂——唯一能弄到手的场地——召开大会。结果一个人影都没有。这人百思不得其解。他踱到镇中心的广场上，凑近一小撮闲汉，问是怎么回事。

“你们是从纽约那个啥哈莱姆来的吧？”镇上一个人问。

“是啊，我就住在哈莱姆。怎么啦？”

“哼，俺们可不要哪个该死的黑鬼来领头；你要是识相，晓得啥对自个儿有好处，就趁早离开这地方。”那汉子发话了。

===Original===
“Where do you get that nigger stuff?” inquired the amazed and insulted organizer. “I’m a white man.”

“Yo ain’t th’ first white nigger whut’s bin aroun’ these parts,” was the reply.

The organizer, puzzled but helpless, stayed around town for a week and then departed. Somebody had told the simple folk that Harlem was the Negro district in New York, after ascertaining that the organizer lived in that district. To them Harlem and Negro became synonymous and the laborite was doomed.

The radical labor organizer, refused permission to use the Knights of Nordica Hall because he was a Jew, was prevented from holding a street meeting when someone started a rumor that he believed in dividing up property, nationalizing women, and was in addition an atheist. He freely admitted the first, laughed at the second and proudly proclaimed the third. That was sufficient to inflame the mill hands, although God had been strangely deaf to their prayers, they owned no property to divide and most of their women were so ugly that they need have had no fears that any outsiders would want to nationalize them. The disciple of Lenin and Trotsky vanished down the road with a crowd of emaciated workers at his heels.

===Chinese===
“你凭什么拿黑鬼这套血口喷人？”组织者又惊又怒地问道，“我可是白人。”

“在这一带晃悠过的白皮黑鬼，你可不是头一个。”对方回敬道。

这位组织者一头雾水又无计可施，在镇上耗了一个星期，只好走人。原来有人先打探清楚这组织者就住在哈莱姆，然后告诉这些头脑简单的乡民：哈莱姆就是纽约的黑人区。在他们眼里，哈莱姆和黑人从此画上了等号，这位工运人士的命数便注定了。

那位激进派工运组织者因为是犹太人，被拒绝使用北欧骑士团会堂；想开街头大会，又有人放出谣言作梗，说他主张平分财产、妇女公有，外加是个无神论者。头一条他坦然认账，第二条他付之一笑，第三条他更是自豪地当众宣告。这就足以点燃纱厂工人的怒火了——尽管上帝对他们的祷告一直出奇地充耳不闻，尽管他们根本没有财产可供平分，尽管他们村里的女人大多丑得令人放心，绝不必担心哪个外乡人想把她们公有。这位列宁和托洛茨基的信徒顺着大路一溜烟没了影，身后跟着一大群瘦骨嶙峋的工人。

===Original===
Soon all was quiet and orderly again in Paradise, SC. On the advice of a conciliator from the United States Labor Department, Blickdoff and Hortzenboff took immediate steps to make their workers more satisfied with their pay, their jobs and their little home town. They built a swimming pool, a tennis court, shower baths and a playground for their employees but neglected to shorten their work time so these improvements could be enjoyed. They announced that they would give each worker a bonus of a whole day’s pay at Christmas time, hereafter, and a week’s vacation each year to every employee who had been with them more than ten years. There were no such employees, of course, but the mill hands were overjoyed with their victory.

The local Baptist preacher, who was very thoughtfully paid by the company with the understanding that he would take a practical view of conditions in the community, told his flock their employers were to be commended for adopting a real Christian and American way of settling the difficulties between them and their workers. He suggested it was quite likely that Jesus, placed in the same position, would have done likewise.

“Be thankful for the little things,” he mooed. “God works in mysterious ways his wonders to perform. Ye shall know the truth and the truth shall set ye free. The basis of all things is truth. Let us not be led astray by the poison from vipers’ tongues. This is America and not Russia. Patrick Henry said ‘Give me liberty or give me death’ and the true, red-blooded, one hundred percent American citizen says the same thing today. But there are right ways and wrong ways to get liberty. Your employers have gone about it the right way. For what, after all is liberty except the enjoyment of life; and have they not placed within your reach those things that bring happiness and recreation?

===Chinese===
不久，南卡罗来纳的天堂镇又归于安宁有序。听了美国劳工部一位调解员的指点，布利克多夫和霍岑博夫立刻采取措施，好让工人们对薪水、活计和小家乡更加称心满意。他们给雇工修了游泳池、网球场、淋浴室和游乐场，却忘了缩短工时，好让人有工夫享用这些设施。他们还宣布，从今往后每年圣诞节给每个工人发一整天工资的奖金；凡是工龄满十年的员工，每年另享一周假期。这样的员工自然一个也没有，可纱厂工人们还是为自己的胜利乐开了花。

本地那位浸信会牧师，一向受公司“体贴”供养，条件是要他对镇上的事态持一种讲实际的态度。他对自己的羊群说，雇主们找到了一条地地道道基督徒式的、美国式的路子来化解他们同工人之间的纠纷，值得大大夸赞。他还暗示，耶稣若处在同样的位置，多半也会照此办理。

“要为小事知恩，”他像老牛哞叫似的说道。“上帝行事奥妙莫测，自有奇工要成全。你们必晓得真理，真理必叫你们得以自由。万事的根基是真理。莫让毒蛇之舌吐出的毒液把咱们引入歧途。这里是美利坚，不是俄罗斯。帕特里克·亨利说过‘不自由，毋宁死’，今天，真正热血的百分之百美国公民说的还是这句话。不过，争取自由有正道，也有邪道。你们的雇主走的正是正道。说到底，自由不就是享受生活吗？他们不是已经把带来幸福和消遣的东西都放到你们手边了吗？

===Original===
“Your employers are interested, just as all true Americans are interested, in the welfare of their fellow citizens, their fellow townsmen. Their hearts beat for you. They are always thinking of you. They are always planning ways to make conditions better for you. They are sincerely doing all in their power. They have very heavy responsibilities.

“So you must be patient. Rome wasn’t built in a day. All things turn out well in time. Christ knows what he is doing and he will not permit his children to suffer.

“O, ye of little faith! Let not your hearts store up jealousy, hatred and animosity. Let not your minds be wooed by misunderstanding. Let us try to act and think as God would wish us to, and above all, let us, like those two kindly men yonder, practice Christian tolerance.”

Despite this inspiring message, it was apparent to everyone that Paradise would never be the same again. Rumors continued to fill the air. People were always asking each other embarrassing questions about birth and blood. Fights became more frequent. Large numbers of the workers, being of Southern birth, were unable to disprove charges of possessing Negro ancestry, and so were forced to leave the vicinity. The mill hands kept so busy talking about Negro blood that no one thought of discussing wages and hours of labor.

===Chinese===
> “你们的雇主关心自己同胞、自己同镇乡亲的福祉，跟所有真正的美国人一个样。他们的心为你们而跳动。他们时时刻刻想着你们。他们一直在筹划法子，要把你们的境况变得更好。他们真心实意，竭尽所能。他们肩上的担子重得很哪。

“所以你们得有耐心。罗马不是一天建成的。日子到了，一切自会好转。基督知道自己做的是什么，他决不容让自己的儿女受苦。

“哦，你们这小信的人哪！别叫心里积存起嫉妒、仇恨和怨毒。别叫头脑让误解勾引了去。让我们照着上帝的意愿去行事、去思想；而最要紧的是，让我们像那边那两位仁厚的好人一样，践行基督徒的宽容。”

尽管有这篇鼓舞人心的训诫，人人都看得出，天堂镇再也回不到从前了。流言继续满天飞。人们见面总要互相盘问出身血统，问得人下不来台。打架斗殴越来越勤。大批工人本是南方土生土长的，洗不清身上带有黑人血统的指控，只好被迫离开这一带。纱厂工人们忙着谈论黑人血统还忙不过来，工资和工时早没人提起了。

===Original===
In August, Messrs. Blickdoff and Hortzenboff, being in Atlanta on business, stopped by Matthew’s office.

“Well, how’s the strike?” asked the Grand Giraw.

“Dot strike!” echoed Blickdoff. “Ach Gott! Dot strike neffer come off. Vat you do, you razscal?”

“That’s my secret,” replied Matthew, a little proudly. “Every man to his trade, you know.”

It had indeed become Matthew’s trade and he was quite adept at it. What had happened at Paradise had also happened elsewhere. There were no more rumors of strikes. The working people were far more interested in what they considered, or were told was, the larger issue of race. It did not matter that they had to send their children into the mills to augment the family wage; that they were always sickly and that their death rate was high. What mattered such little things when the very foundation of civilization, white supremacy, was threatened?

===Chinese===
八月间，布利克多夫和霍岑博夫两位先生到亚特兰大办事，顺路拐进了马修的办公室。

“怎么样，那场罢工啊？”大吉罗问。

“那场罢工！”布利克多夫跟着念叨。“哎呀我的老天爷！那罢工压根儿没闹起来。你到底使了什么手段，你这个坏蛋？”

“这是我的秘密。”马修答道，不无得意。“人各有各的行当嘛，你知道的。”

这确实已经成了马修的行当，而且他干这一行相当在行。天堂镇上演的戏码，别处也在上演。罢工的传闻从此绝迹。劳工们如今更上心的，是他们自认为——或者是别人告诉他们——更为重大的种族问题。孩子们不得不进厂做工，贴补一家人的工资，这不要紧；他们自己常年病歪歪、死亡率居高不下，也不要紧。文明的根基——白人至上——眼看受到了威胁，这等小事又算得了什么呢？

## VIII / 第八章

===Original===
For over two years now had Black-No-More, Incorporated, been carrying on its self-appointed task of turning Negroes into Caucasians. The job was almost complete, except for the black folk in prisons, orphan asylums, insane asylums, homes for the aged, houses of correction and similar institutions. Those who had always maintained that it was impossible to get Negroes together for anything but a revival, a funeral or a frolic, now had to admit that they had cooperated well in getting white. The poor had been helped by the well-to-do, brothers had helped sisters, children had assisted parents. There had been revived some of the same spirit of adventure prevalent in the days of the Underground Railroad. As a result, even in Mississippi, Negroes were quite rare. In the North the only Negroes to be seen were mulatto babies whose mothers, charmed by the beautiful color of their offspring, had defied convention and not turned them white. As there had never been more than two million Negroes in the North, the whitening process had been viewed indifferently by the masses because those who controlled the channels of opinion felt that the country was getting rid of a very vexatious problem at absolutely no cost; but not so in the South.

===Chinese===
两年多来，「黑色终结」公司（Black-No-More, Inc.）一直干着它自领的差事：把黑人变成白人。这项事业眼看大功告成，只剩监狱、孤儿院、精神病院、养老院、感化院之类机构里的黑人还没有动。从前总有人说，黑人除了布道会、葬礼和游乐，干什么都聚不到一块儿，如今也不得不承认，这回为了变白，他们配合得着实不坏。有钱的帮了穷的，兄姊帮了弟妹，儿女帮了爹娘。当年「地下铁路」（南北战争前协助黑奴北逃的秘密网络）盛行时的那股冒险精神，一时间竟重现于世。结果呢，连密西西比都难得见到几个黑人了。在北方，能见到的黑人只剩混血娃娃，他们的母亲被小家伙漂亮的肤色迷住，硬是不顾流俗，没把他们送去变白。北方从来没有超过两百万黑人，对于这场变白风潮，大众本就反应淡漠，因为把持舆论渠道的人觉得，国家这是分文不花就甩掉了一个老大难问题；南方却全然不是这么回事。

===Original===
When one-third of the population of the erstwhile Confederacy had consisted of the much-maligned Sons of Ham, the blacks had really been of economic, social and psychological value to the section. Not only had they done the dirty work and laid the foundation of its wealth, but they had served as a convenient red herring for the upper classes when the white proletariat grew restive under exploitation. The presence of the Negro as an under class had also made of Dixie a unique part of the United States. There, despite the trend to industrialization, life was a little different, a little pleasanter, a little softer. There was contrast and variety, which was rare in a nation where standardization had progressed to such an extent that a traveler didn’t know what town he was in until someone informed him. The South had always been identified with the Negro, and vice versa, and its most pleasant memories treasured in song and story, were built around this pariah class.

===Chinese===
从前，昔年南部邦联的人口有三分之一是那帮饱受诋毁的「含的子孙」，那时候，黑人在这片地方确有经济、社会和心理上的价值。脏活累活是他们干的，此地财富的根基是他们垫下的；而且每逢白人无产阶级在剥削下骚动不平，他们又是上层阶级顺手拈来的现成幌子，把众人的视线引开。黑人作为一个底层阶级的存在，还使得南方成了合众国里独一无二的一角。在这里，尽管工业化大潮涌动，日子总还有点不一样，有点更舒心，有点更绵软。这里有色调的对照，有花样经，这在一个标准化登峰造极的国度里可是稀罕物——在那样的国度，旅人不问人，根本不知道自己身在何城。南方从来同黑人互为表里，那些珍藏于歌谣与传说中的最美好的回忆，也都是围着这个贱民阶级搭建起来的。

===Original===
The deep concern of the Southern Caucasians with chivalry, the protection of white womanhood, the exaggerated development of race pride and the studied arrogance of even the poorest half-starved white peon, were all due to the presence of the black man. Booted and starved by their industrial and agricultural feudal lords, the white masses derived their only consolation and happiness from the fact that they were the same color as their oppressors and consequently better than the mudsill blacks.

===Chinese===
南方白人对骑士精神那般上心，对保护白人妇女那般紧张，种族自豪感膨胀到那个地步，连最穷最饿的白人雇农也摆出一副煞有介事的傲慢——凡此种种，全都是因为有黑人在。被他们工农两界的封建主老爷又踢又饿的白人大众，唯一的安慰和快乐，就来自于这么一个事实：他们跟骑在自己头上的主子同一种肤色，因此总比垫在泥底的黑人高出一头。

===Original===
The economic loss to the South by the ethnic migration was considerable. Hundreds of wooden railroad coaches, long since condemned as death traps in all other parts of the country, had to be scrapped by the railroads when there were no longer any Negroes to Jim Crow. Thousands of railroad waiting rooms remained unused because, having been set aside for the use of Negroes, they were generally too dingy and unattractive for white folk or were no longer necessary. Thousands of miles of streets located in the former Black Belts, and thus without sewers or pavement, were having to be improved at the insistent behest of the rapidly increased white population, real and imitation. Real estate owners who had never dreamed of making repairs on their tumbledown property when it was occupied by the docile Negroes, were having to tear down, rebuild and alter to suit white tenants. Shacks and drygoods boxes that had once sufficed as schools for Negro children, had now to be condemned and abandoned as unsuitable for occupation by white youth. Whereas thousands of school teachers had received thirty and forty dollars a month because of their Negro ancestry, the various cities and counties of the Southland were now forced to pay the standard salaries prevailing elsewhere.

===Chinese===
这场族群大迁徙给南方造成的经济损失着实可观。几百节木制客车车厢——早在国内其他地方就被判定是死亡陷阱——如今再没有黑人可供他们施行吉姆·克劳（种族隔离），铁路公司只好把她们报废了事。几千间火车站候车室闲置下来：这些屋子本是划给黑人用的，一般又脏又寒碜，白人不屑一顾，要不然就是根本用不着了。位于原黑人区（Black Belt）的成千上万英里街道，向来没有下水道、没有路面铺装，如今白人人口——货真价实的和冒名顶替的都算上——迅速膨胀，逼得当局不得不动手整修。那些收着顺从的黑人房客时做梦也不想着维修破烂房产的业主，如今只好拆的拆、盖的盖、改的改，好合白人房客的心意。从前给黑人孩子当学堂都嫌将就的窝棚和杂货木箱，如今也得判定不堪人居、弃置不用了——总不能叫白人少年委屈在那里面。从前成千上万的小学教师因为有黑人血统，月薪只有三十块、四十块；如今南方各地的城镇县治，都被迫按别处通行的标准发薪水了。

===Original===
Naturally taxes increased. Chambers of Commerce were now unable to send out attractive advertising to Northern business firms offering no or very low taxation as an inducement to them to move South nor were they able to offer as many cheap building sites. Only through the efforts of the Grand Exalted Giraw of the Knights of Nordica were they still able to point to their large reserves of docile, contented, Anglo-Saxon labor, and who knew how long that condition would last?

Consequently, the upper classes faced the future with some misgivings. As if being deprived of the pleasure of black mistresses were not enough, there was a feeling that there would shortly be widespread revolt against the existing medieval industrial conditions and resultant reduction of profits and dividends. The mill barons viewed with distaste the prospect of having to do away with child labor. Rearing back in their padded swivel chairs, they leaned fat jowls on well-manicured hands and mourned the passing of the halcyon days of yore.

===Chinese===
税收自然见涨。商会再也没法子向北方企业寄出诱人的广告，吹嘘此地免税或税负极低，招他们南迁；廉价的建房地皮也拿不出那么多块了。多亏北欧骑士团的大吉罗（Grand Exalted Giraw）卖力，他们还能夸口本地蓄有大批温驯知足的盎格鲁-撒克逊劳工储备——只是这样的好光景还能撑多久，天晓得。

于是，上层阶级面对前程，心里直打鼓。失了黑人情妇的乐趣犹在其次，更让他们不安的是一种预感：用不了多久，就会有人群起反抗现下这种中世纪式的工业条件，利润和股息势必跟着缩水。纱厂大王们一想到早晚非取缔童工不可，就满心的不痛快。他们朝后靠在软垫转椅里，肥腮帮搁在修剪齐整的手上，哀悼昔日太平岁月的一去不返。

===Original===
If the South had lost its Negroes, however, it had certainly not lost its vote, and the political oligarchy that ruled the section was losing its old assurance and complacency. The Republicans had made inroads here and there in the 1934 Congressional elections. The situation politically was changing and if drastic steps were not immediately taken, the Republicans might carry the erstwhile Solid South, thus practically destroying the Democratic Party. Another Presidential election was less than two years off. There would have to be fast work to ward off disaster. Farsighted people, North and South, even foresaw the laboring people soon forsaking both of the old parties and going Socialist. Politicians and business men shuddered at the thought of such a tragedy and saw horrible visions of old-age pensions, eight-hour laws, unemployment insurance, workingmen’s compensation, minimum-wage legislation, abolition of child labor, dissemination of birth-control information, monthly vacations for female workers, two-month vacations for prospective mothers, both with pay, and the probable killing of individual initiative and incentive by taking the ownership of national capital out of the hands of two million people and putting it into the hands of one hundred and twenty million.

===Chinese===
话虽如此，南方虽然失去了黑人，选票可是一票没少，而统治这片地方的政治寡头集团，却渐渐失去了往日的自信与自得。1934年的国会选举里，共和党已经在这儿那儿攻下了几处滩头。政治风向变了，倘若不立刻拿出断然手段，共和党人没准就会席卷昔日铁板一块的南方，民主党实际上也就玩完了。距离下届总统大选，已不足两年。要躲过这场大祸，手脚必须快。南北两地的有识之士甚至预言，劳工大众用不了多久就会抛开两大老党，改投社会主义。政客们和生意人一想这幅惨象就浑身发冷，眼前尽是可怖的幻影：养老金啦，八小时工作制啦，失业保险啦，工伤赔偿啦，最低工资立法啦，取缔童工啦，节育知识的推广啦，女工每月带薪假期、准妈妈两个月带薪假期啦——更可怕的是，国家资本的所有权一旦从两百万人手里挪到一亿两千万人手里，个人的进取心和干劲八成也就给扼杀了。

===Original===
Which explains why Senator Rufus Kretin of Georgia, one of the old Democratic war horses, an incomparable Negro-baiter, a faithful servitor of the dominant economic interests of his state and the lusty father of several black families since whitened, walked into the office of Imperial Grand Wizard Givens one day in March, 1935.

===Chinese===
这就解释了，1935年3月的某一天，佐治亚州的拉夫斯·克雷廷（Rufus Kretin）参议员——民主党一员老资历的战将，猎逐黑人的旷世高手，本州经济权势集团的忠实仆役，并且是好几个黑人家庭（如今都已变白）的生龙活虎的生身父亲——为什么会踱进帝国大巫师吉文斯的办公室。

===Original===
“Boys,” he began, as closeted with Rev. Givens, Matthew and Bunny in the new modernistic Knights of Nordica palace, they quaffed cool and illegal beverages, “we gotta do sumpin and do it quick. These heah damn Yankees ah makin’ inroads on ouah preserves, suh. Th’ Republican vote is a-growin’. No tellin’ what’s li’ble tuh happen in this heah nex’ ’lection.”

“What can we do, Senator?” asked the Imperial Grand Wizard. “How can we serve the cause?”

“That’s just it. That’s just it, suh; jus’ what Ah came heah fo’,” replied the Senator. “Naow sum o’ us was thinkin’ that maybe yo’all might be able to he’p us keep these damn hicks in line. Yo’all are intelligent gent’men; you know what Ah’m gettin’ at?”

“Well, that’s a pretty big order, Colonel,” said Givens.

===Chinese===
「孩子们，」他开口道。此时，他和吉文斯牧师、马修和邦尼在新落成的摩登北欧骑士团大厦里密室对坐，正大口灌着冰镇的非法饮料。「俺们得想个法子，还得快。这帮该死的北方佬正在俺们的老地盘上蚕食鲸吞呢，先生。共和党的选票见天往上蹿。下回选举要出啥乱子，谁也保不齐。」

「那咱们能干点啥呢，参议员？」帝国大巫师问道。「怎样为这番事业效劳？」

「正是这话。正是这话，先生；俺就是为这个来的，」参议员答道。「方才俺们几个人正琢磨，兴许诸位能帮俺们把这帮该死的乡巴佬管束住。诸位都是有见识的绅士，懂俺的意思吧？」

「这个嘛，这单差事可不小啊，上校。」吉文斯说。

===Original===
“Yes,” Matthew added. “It’ll be a hard proposition. Conditions are no longer what they used to be.”

“An’,” said Givens, “we can’t do much with that nigger business, like we used to do when th’ old Klan was runnin’.”

“What about one o’ them theah Red scares,” asked the Senator, hopefully.

“Humph!” the clergyman snorted. “Better leave that there Red business alone. Times ain’t like they was, you know. Anyhow, them damn Reds’ll be down here soon enough ’thout us encouragin’ ’em none.”

===Chinese===
「是啊。」马修补了一句。「这差事不好办。眼下的形势不比当年喽。」

「再说，」吉文斯道，「黑人那一套咱们也耍不大起来了，不像老三K党当令那阵子。」

「那来一场赤色恐慌怎么样？」参议员满怀希望地问。

「哼！」这位牧师从鼻孔里喷出一声。「赤色那档子事还是别沾为妙。世道不一样了，你晓得。再说了，不用咱们去招惹，那帮该死的赤色分子早晚自己就会摸下来。」

===Original===
“Guess that’s right, Gen’ral,” mused the statesman. Then brightening: “Lookaheah, Givens. This fellah Fisher’s gotta good head. Why not let him work out sumpin?”

“Yeah, he sure has,” agreed the Wizard, glad to escape any work except minding the treasury of his order. “If he can’t do it, ain’t nobody can. Him and Bunny here is as shrewd as some o’ them old time darkies. He! He! He!” He beamed patronizingly upon his brilliant son-in-law and his plump secretary.

“Well, theah’s money in it. We got plenty o’ cash; what we want now is votes,” the Senator explained. “C’ose yuh caint preach that white supremacy stuff ve’y effectively when they haint no niggahs.”

“Leave it to me. I’ll work out something,” said Matthew. Here was a chance to get more power, more money. Busy as he was, it would not do to let the opportunity slip by.

===Chinese===
「倒也是，将军。」这位政界元老沉吟道。随即眼睛一亮：「听我说，吉文斯。费舍尔这小子脑瓜好使。何不让他想个法子出来？」

「是啊，那脑瓜是好使得很。」大巫师附和道——只要能躲开一切正经活计、光是看管团里的金库，他就快活似神仙。「他要办不成，就没人办得成。他跟这位邦尼，精明得跟旧年头的黑崽子一个样。嘿！嘿！嘿！」他居高临下地朝那才气过人的女婿和胖乎乎的秘书绽开满面笑容。

「咳，这里头有钱可图。现钱咱们有的是；如今缺的是选票，」参议员解释道。「当然了，连个黑鬼都不剩，你还怎么宣讲白人至上那套玩意儿？效力总要大打折扣。」

「交给我吧，我会琢磨出个章程来。」马修说。这可是攫取更多权力、更多钱财的机会。他再忙，也不能叫这机会从指缝里溜走。

===Original===
“Yuh caint lose no time,” warned the Senator.

“We won’t,” crowed Givens.

A few minutes later they took a final drink together, shook hands and the Senator, bobbing his white head to the young ladies in the outer office, departed.

Matthew and Bunny retired to the private office of the Grand Exalted Giraw.

“What you thinkin’ about pullin’?” asked Bunny.

“Plenty. We’ll try the old sure fire Negro problem stuff.”

===Chinese===
「这事可一刻也耽搁不得。」参议员提醒道。

「耽搁不了。」吉文斯欢声应道。

几分钟后，众人干了最后一杯，握手作别。参议员朝外间办公室的几位小姐频点白头，起身去了。

马修和邦尼退进大吉罗的私人办公室。

「你琢磨着干什么买卖？」邦尼问。

「大有可干。咱们就把那套老掉牙却百发百中的种族问题把戏再搬出来。」

===Original===
“But that’s ancient history, Brother,” protested Bunny. “These ducks won’t fall for that any more.”

“Bunny, I’ve learned something on this job, and that is that hatred and prejudice always go over big. These people have been raised on the Negro problem, they’re used to it, they’re trained to react to it. Why should I rack my brain to hunt up something else when I can use a dodge that’s always delivered the goods?”

“It may go over at that.”

“I know it will. Just leave it to me,” said Matthew confidently. “That’s not worrying me at all. What’s got my goat is my wife being in the family way.” Matthew stopped bantering a moment, a sincere look of pain erasing his usual ironic expression.

===Chinese===
「可那都是老皇历了，老兄。」邦尼不以为然。「这帮冤大头不会再上这个当了。」

「邦尼，这行当干到现在，我悟出一条道理：仇恨和偏见，永远吃香。这帮人从小是拿种族问题喂大的，习惯了，训练有素，一碰就有反应。既然有个百试百灵的老法子摆在手边，我何苦绞尽脑汁另寻新招？」

「这么一说，倒兴许真成。」

「我知道准成。全交给我。」马修满有把握地说。「这个我一点都不发愁。真正让我堵心的，是我老婆有喜了。」马修一时收起打趣的腔调，惯常挂在脸上的讥诮褪了下去，换上一副真切的苦相。

===Original===
“Congratulations!” burbled Bunny.

“Don’t rub it in,” Matthew replied. “You know how the kid will look.”

“That’s right,” agreed his pal. “You know, sometimes I forget who we are.”

“Well, I don’t. I know I’m a darky and I’m always on the alert.”

“What do you intend to do?”

“I don’t know, Big Boy, I don’t know. I would ordinarily send her to one of those Lying-in Hospitals but she’d be suspicious. Yet, if the kid is born it’ll sure be black.”

===Chinese===
「恭喜恭喜！」邦尼咕咕呱呱地道喜。

「你就别往伤口上撒盐了。」马修回道。「你知道那孩子生下来会是什么模样。」

「这话不假。」他的伙伴应道。「说真的，有时候我自己都忘了咱们是什么人。」

「我可忘不了。我知道自己是个黑崽子，所以时刻警醒着。」

「那你打算怎么办？」

「不知道啊，老大哥，真不知道。平常我会送她去哪家产科医院，可她准起疑心。可这孩子要是真生下来，保准是黑的。」

===Original===
“It won’t be white,” Bunny agreed. “Why not tell her the whole thing and since she’s so crazy about you, I don’t think she’d hesitate to go.”

“Man, you must be losing your mind, or else you’ve lost it!” Matthew exploded. “She’s a worse nigger-hater than her father. She’d holler for a divorce before you could say Jack Robinson.”

“You’ve got too much money for that.”

“You’re assuming that she has plenty of intelligence.”

“Hasn’t she?”

“Let’s not discuss a painful subject,” pleaded Matthew. “Suggest a remedy.”

===Chinese===
「反正是白不了的。」邦尼附和。「何不把实情原原本本抖给她？她对你痴成那副样子，我看她不会推三阻四不肯去的。」

「伙计，你八成是疯了，不，是早就疯透了！」马修炸了。「她恨黑鬼比她爹还狠。你话音还没落，她就会嚷着要离婚。」

「就凭你如今这份家财，她未必舍得。」

「你这是高估了她的脑子。」

「难道不高？」

「这个伤心话题就别提了。」马修央道。「说说不怎么治吧。」

===Original===
“She don’t have to know that she’s going to one of Crookman’s places, does she?”

“No, but I can’t get her to leave home to have the baby.”

“Why?”

“Oh, a lot of damn sentiment about having her baby in the old home, and her damned old mother supports her. So what can I do?”

“Then, the dear old homestead is the only thing that’s holding up the play?”

===Chinese===
「她又不必知道去的是克鲁克曼开的院所，对吧？」

「是不必，可我没法说动她离家去生孩子。」

「为什么？」

「唉，什么『孩子得生在老宅』，一大堆酸溜溜的讲究，她那该死的老娘又死命撑她的腰。你说我能有什么法子？」

「这么说来，挡住这出戏的，就是那座亲爱的老家宅子喽？」

===Original===
“You’re a smart boy, Bunny.”

“Don’t stress the obvious. Seriously, though, I think everything can be fixed okeh.”

“How?” cried Matthew, eagerly.

“Is it worth five grand?” countered Bunny.

===Chinese===
「邦尼，你小子真机灵。」

「明摆着的事就别夸我了。不过说正经的，我看这事儿全能摆平。」

「怎么摆平？」马修急切地问。

「这事值五千块吗？」邦尼反问。

===Original===
“Money’s no object, you know, but explain your proposition.”

“I will not. You get me fifty century notes and I’ll explain later.”

“It’s a deal, old friend.”

===Chinese===
「你知道，钱不成问题。说来听听你的章程。」

「不说。你先给我弄五十张百元大钞来，回头我再细讲。」

「成交，老朋友。」

===Original===
Bunny Brown was a man of action. That evening he entered the popular Niggerhead Café, rendezvous of the questionable classes, and sat down at a table. The place was crowded with drinkers downing their “white mule” and contorting to the strains issuing from a radio loudspeaker. A current popular dance piece, “The Black Man Blues,” was filling the room. The songwriters had been making a fortune recently writing sentimental songs about the passing of the Negro. The plaintive voice of a blues singer rushed out of the loudspeaker:

> “I wonder where my big, black man has gone;
>
> Oh, I wonder where my big, black man has gone.
>
> Has he done got faded an’ left me all alone?”

===Chinese===
邦尼·布朗是个说干就干的人。当天晚上，他走进了那家生意红火的「黑鬼头」咖啡馆——各路可疑人物的聚会据点——拣了张桌子坐下。店里挤满酒客，一个个灌着「白骡子」私酿，随着收音机喇叭里淌出的曲调扭动身腰。眼下正走红的一支舞曲《黑人蓝调》满屋回荡。写歌的近来光靠谱写哀叹黑人消逝的感伤歌曲就发了大财。一位蓝调歌手哀哀切切的嗓音从喇叭里冲出来：

> 「不知道我家那黑大个儿上哪儿去了；
>
> 哦，不知道我家那黑大个儿上哪儿去了。
>
> 他是不是褪了色，撇下我孤零零一个人？」

===Original===
When the music ceased and the dancers returned to their tables, Bunny began to look around. In a far corner he saw a waiter whose face seemed familiar. He waited until the fellow came close when he hailed him. As the waiter bent over to get his order, he studied him closely. He had seen this fellow somewhere before. Who could he be? Suddenly with a start he remembered. It was Dr. Joseph Bonds, former head of the Negro Data League in New York. What had brought him here and to this condition? The last time he had seen Bonds, the fellow was a power in the Negro world, with a country place in Westchester County and a swell apartment in town. It saddened Bunny to think that catastrophe had overtaken such a man. Even getting white, it seemed, hadn’t helped him much. He recalled that Bonds in his heyday had collected from the white philanthropists with the slogan: “Work, Not Charity,” and he smiled as he thought that Bonds would be mighty glad now to get a little charity and not so much work.

===Chinese===
曲子一停，跳舞的人各回各桌，邦尼便开始四下打量。在远远一个角落，他看见一个侍者，面相好生眼熟。他一直捱到那家伙走近才招呼他。侍者俯身听他点酒时，他细细端详。这人他从前准在哪儿见过。会是谁呢？猛地他心里一激灵，想起来了。是约瑟夫·邦兹（Joseph Bonds）博士，纽约黑人资料同盟（Negro Data League）的前任头目。是什么把他弄到这儿来，落到这步田地？上回见邦兹的时候，这家伙在黑人世界里还是响当当的人物，在韦斯特切斯特县有座乡间别墅，城里有一套气派的公寓。想到这样一号人物也遭了大难，邦尼心里发沉。看来，就连变白也没帮上他多少忙。他记起邦兹当年走红时，向白人慈善家募捐的口号是「要工作，不要施舍」，如今一想，邦兹只怕巴不得少来点工作、多来点施舍，不由得笑了。

===Original===
“Would a century note look good to you right now?” he asked the former Negro leader when he returned with his drink.

“Just show it to me, Mister,” said the waiter, licking his lips. “What you want me to do?”

“What will you do for a hundred berries?” pursued Bunny.

“I’d hate to tell you,” replied Bonds, grinning and revealing his familiar tobacco-stained teeth.

“Have you got a friend you can trust?”

“Sure, a fellow named Licorice that washes pots in back.”

===Chinese===
待这位昔日的黑人领袖端着酒回来，他问：「这会儿要是一张百元大钞摆在你眼前，好看不好看？」

「先生，先亮出来给我瞧瞧。」侍者说着舔了舔嘴唇。「你要我干什么？」

「一百块大洋到手，你肯干什么？」邦尼追问。

「那话我可不好意思说出口。」邦兹答道，咧嘴一笑，露出那口熟悉的、烟渍斑斑的黄牙。

「你有没有信得过的朋友？」

「有哇，一个叫利科里斯的家伙，在后头洗碗刷锅。」

===Original===
“You don’t mean Santop Licorice, do you?”

“Ssh! They don’t know who he is here. He’s white now, you know.”

“Do they know who you are?”

“What do you mean?” gasped the surprised waiter.

===Chinese===
「你说的该不会是桑托普·利科里斯（Santop Licorice）吧？」

「嘘！这儿没人知道他的底细。他如今可是白人了，你晓得。」

「那这儿的人知道你是谁吗？」

「你这话什么意思？」侍者大惊失色，倒抽一口冷气。

===Original===
“Oh, I won’t say anything but I know you’re Bonds of New York.”

“Who told you?”

“Oh, a little fairy.”

“How could that be? I never associate with them.”

“It wasn’t that kind of a fairy,” Bunny reassured him, laughing. “Well, you get Licorice and come to my hotel when this place closes up.”

“Where is that?” asked Bonds. Bunny wrote his name and room number down on a piece of paper and handed it to him.

Three hours later Bunny was awakened by a knocking at his door. He admitted Bonds and Licorice, the latter smelling strongly of steam and food.

“Here,” said Bunny, holding up a hundred dollar bill, “is a century note. If you boys can lay aside your scruples for a few hours you can have five of them apiece.”

===Chinese===
「哦，我什么也不会说出去，不过我知道，你是纽约来的邦兹。」

「谁告诉你的？」

「哦，一位小仙女。」

「这怎么可能？我从来不跟他们那种人来往。」

「不是那种小仙女。」邦尼笑着安抚他。「这样吧，你把利科里斯找上，等这家店打了烊，到我旅馆来一趟。」

「旅馆在哪儿？」邦兹问。邦尼把姓名和房间号写在一张纸条上，递给了他。

三个钟头后，邦尼被一阵敲门声惊醒。他放邦兹和利科里斯进了屋，后者一身蒸汽和饭菜的浓重气味。

「喏，」邦尼举起一张一百美元的钞票，「这就是百元大钞。二位小哥要是肯把良心搁到一边几个小时，一人就能挣上五张。」

===Original===
“Well,” said Bonds, “neither Santop nor I have been overburdened with them.”

“That’s what I thought,” Bunny murmured. He proceeded to outline the work he wanted them to do.

“But that would be a criminal offense,” objected Licorice.

“You too, Brutus?” sneered Bonds.

“Well, we can’t afford to take chances unless we’re protected,” the former President of Africa argued rather weakly. He was money-hungry and was longing for a stake to get back to Demerara where, since there was a large Negro population, a white man, by virtue of his complexion, amounted to something. Yet, he had had enough experience behind the bars to make him wary.

“We run this town and this state, too,” Bunny assured him. “We could get a couple of our men to pull this stunt but it wouldn’t be good policy.”

===Chinese===
「这么说吧，」邦兹道，「我和桑托普两个，向来就没让那玩意儿压垮过。」

「我就知道。」邦尼低声说。接着，他把要两人干的活计大略交代了一遍。

「可那可是犯法的呀。」利科里斯提出异议。

「你也来这一手，布鲁图斯？」邦兹讥笑道。

「这个嘛，没人给咱们撑腰，咱们可冒不起这个险。」这位前「非洲总统」争辩得有些底气不足。他见钱眼开，一心巴望攒下一笔本钱回德梅拉拉去——那地方黑人众多，白人单凭一张脸皮就高人一等。只是他蹲班房的教训受够了，不由得不提防三分。

「这座城归咱们管，这个州也归咱们管。」邦尼给他吃定心丸。「这把戏咱们手底下也有一两个人能办，可那不是什么高明的做法。」

===Original===
“How about a thousand bucks apiece?” asked Bonds, his eyes glittering as he viewed the crisp banknotes in Bunny’s hand.

“Here,” said Bunny. “Take this century note between you, get your material and pull the job. When you’ve finished I’ll give you nineteen more like it between you.”

The two cronies looked at each other and nodded.

“It’s a go,” said Bonds.

They departed and Bunny went back to sleep.

===Chinese===
「一人一千块怎么样？」邦兹问，眼睛盯着邦尼手里那张挺括的钞票，直放光。

「喏，」邦尼说。「这张百元钞你们俩先合拿着，去置办你们的料，把事情办了。办完之后，我再给你们十九张，还是你们俩分。」

两个老搭档对望一眼，点头应允。

「干了。」邦兹说。

两人走后，邦尼倒头接着睡。

===Original===
The next night about eleven-thirty the bells began to toll and the mournful sirens of the fire engines awakened the entire neighborhood in the vicinity of Rev. Givens’s home. That stately edifice, built by Ku Klux Klan dollars was in flames. Firemen played a score of streams onto the blaze but the house appeared to be doomed.

On a lawn across the street, in the midst of a consoling crowd, stood Rev. and Mrs. Givens, Helen and Matthew. The old couple were taking the catastrophe fatalistically, Matthew was puzzled and suspicious, but Helen was in hysterics. She presented a bedraggled and woebegone appearance with a blanket around her night dress. She wept afresh every time she looked across at the blazing building where she had spent her happy childhood.

===Chinese===
第二天夜里十一点半光景，钟声开始哀鸣，救火车凄厉的警笛把吉文斯牧师宅邸左近的街坊全都惊醒了。那座用三K党的钞票垒起来的巍峨大宅起火了。消防队员把二十来股水柱浇向火海，可那房子看来是保不住了。

街对面一片草坪上，围着一圈劝慰的人，吉文斯牧师夫妇、海伦和马修站在当中。老两口听天由命地受着这场横祸；马修满腹狐疑；海伦却已歇斯底里。她睡衣外胡乱裹着条毯子，一副落汤鸡似的苦相。每回抬眼望望那座火光冲天的房子——她在那儿度过了快乐的童年——都要再哭上一场。

===Original===
“Matthew,” she sobbed, “will you build me another one just like it?”

“Why certainly, Honey,” he agreed, “but it will take quite a while.”

“Oh, I know; I know, but I want it.”

“Well, you’ll get it, darling,” he soothed, “but I think it would be a good idea for you to go away for a while to rest your nerves. We’ve got to think of the little one that’s coming, you know.”

“I don’t wanna go nowhere,” she screamed.

===Chinese===
「马修，」她抽噎着说，「你会给我再盖一座一模一样的，对吗？」

「那还用说，宝贝儿。」他满口应承，「不过得花上好一阵子。」

「哦，我知道；我知道，可我就要嘛。」

「行，一定给你盖，亲爱的。」他柔声哄着。「不过依我看，你不妨出去走动些日子，养养神经。要知道，咱们得为快降生的小家伙着想啊。」

「我哪儿也不去！」她尖声嚷道。

===Original===
“But you’ve got to go somewhere,” he reasoned. “Don’t you think so, Mother?” Old Mrs. Givens agreed it would be a good idea but suggested that she go along. To this Rev. Givens would not listen at first but he finally yielded.

“Guess it’s a good idea after all,” he remarked. “Women folks is always in th’ way when buildin’s goin’ on.”

Matthew was tickled at the turn of affairs. On the way down to the hotel, he sat beside Helen, alternately comforting her and wondering as to the origin of the fire.

===Chinese===
「可你总得上个什么地方去呀。」他好说歹说。「你说是不是，妈？」老吉文斯太太也道这主意不赖，只是提出要跟着一道去。吉文斯牧师起初说什么也不依，末了还是让了步。

「我看这主意横竖不坏。」他说。「盖房子的当口，女人家总是碍手碍脚的。」

事态这样转折，马修心里乐开了花。上旅馆的一路，他挨着海伦坐，一边哄她，一边暗自琢磨这把火是打哪儿烧起来的。

===Original===
Next morning, bright and early, Bunny, grinning broadly walked into the office, threw his hat on a hook and sat down before his desk after the customary salutation.

“Bunny,” called Matthew, looking at him hard. “Get me told!”

“What do you mean?” asked Bunny innocently.

“Just as I thought,” chuckled Matthew. “You’re a nervy guy.”

“Why, I don’t get you,” said Bunny, continuing the pose.

“Come clean, Big Boy. How much did that fire cost?”

===Chinese===
第二天一早，天刚放亮，邦尼咧着大嘴走进办公室，帽子往衣钩上一甩，照例请过安，便在办公桌前落了座。

「邦尼，」马修直勾勾盯着他，叫了一声。「快从实招来！」

「你说什么哪？」邦尼一脸无辜。

「跟我想的一个样。」马修暗自得意地笑。「你小子胆子真肥。」

「哎，我听不懂你在讲什么。」邦尼接着装蒜。

「老实交代吧，老大哥。那把火花掉了多少钱？」

===Original===
“You gave me five grand, didn’t you?”

“Just like a nigger: a person can never get a direct answer from you.”

“Are you satisfied?”

“I’m not crying my eyes out.”

“Is Helen going North for her confinement?”

“Nothing different.”

===Chinese===
「你给了我五千块，不是吗？」

「真跟黑鬼一个样：跟你要句痛快话，比登天还难。」

「那你满意了吧？」

「我还没哭肿眼睛呢。」

「海伦是要去北方待产吗？」

「一切照旧。」

===Original===
“Well, then, why do you want to know the why and wherefore of that blaze?”

“Just curiosity, Nero, old chap,” grinned Matthew.

“Remember,” warned Bunny, mischievously, “curiosity killed the cat.”

The ringing of the telephone bell interrupted their conversation.

===Chinese===
「那又何必呢？那场火的前因后果，你刨根问底地打听做什么？」

「好奇罢了，尼禄老兄。」马修咧嘴一笑。

「记住喽，」邦尼促狭地提醒，「好奇害死猫。」

电话铃响，打断了他们的谈话。

===Original===
“What’s that?” yelled Matthew into the mouthpiece. “The hell you say! All right, I’ll be right up.” He hung up the receiver, jumped up excitedly and grabbed his hat.

“What’s the matter?” shouted Bunny. “Somebody dead?”

“No,” answered the agitated Matthew, “Helen’s had a miscarriage,” and he dashed out of the room.

“Somebody dead right on,” murmured Bunny, half aloud.

===Chinese===
「什么？」马修冲着话筒嚷道。「你这不是胡说吧！好，我马上上来。」他撂下听筒，兴奋地跳起来，一把抓过帽子。

「出什么事了？」邦尼喊道。「死人了？」

「不是，」马修激动地答道，「海伦流产了。」说罢冲出了房间。

「也真算是死了口人，一点不差。」邦尼半出声地喃喃。

===Original===
Joseph Bonds and Santop Licorice, clean shaven and immaculate, followed the Irish red cap into their drawing room on the New York Express.

“It sure feels good to get out of the barrel once more,” sighed Bonds, dropping down on the soft cushion and pulling out a huge cigar.

“Ain’t it the truth?” agreed the former Admiral of the Royal African Navy.

===Chinese===
约瑟夫·邦兹和桑托普·利科里斯胡子刮得干干净净，浑身上下簇新笔挺，跟着一名爱尔兰裔的红帽搬运工，走进了「纽约特快」列车上他们那间包厢客厅。

「总算又能从桶里爬出来见见天日了，真美气。」邦兹叹道，一屁股陷进软垫，掏出一支老粗的雪茄。

「可不是嘛！」这位前「皇家非洲海军上将」深表同感。

## X / 十

===Original===
Hank Johnson, Chuck Foster, Dr. Crookman and Gorman Gay, National Chairman of the Republican National Committee, sat in the physician’s hotel suite conversing in low tones.

“We’re having a tough time getting ready for the Fall campaign,” said Gay. “Unfortunately our friends are not contributing with their accustomed liberality.”

“Can’t complain about us, can you?” asked Foster.

“No, no,” the politician denied quickly. “You have been most liberal in the past two years, but then we have done many favors for you, too.”

“Yuh sho right, Gay,” Hank remarked. “Dem crackahs mighta put us outa business efen it hadn’ bin fo’ th’ admin’strations suppo’t.”
===Chinese===
汉克·约翰逊、查克·福斯特、克鲁克曼大夫，再加上共和党全国委员会主席戈曼·盖伊（Gorman Gay），正坐在大夫下榻旅馆的套房里，低声交谈。

「今年秋天这场选战，筹备起来着实头疼，」盖伊说，「可惜呀，我们那些朋友掏起钱来，不像往常那么慷慨了。」

「总不能抱怨我们吧，啊？」福斯特问。

「不不，」这位政客连忙否认。「过去两年，二位是顶慷慨的。不过话说回来，我们也替你们办过不少事嘛。」

「说得一点不差，盖伊，」汉克搭腔。「要不是政府撑腰，那些南方白佬早叫咱们关张大吉了。」

===Original===
“I’m quite sure we deeply appreciate the many favors we’ve received from the present administration,” added Dr. Crookman.

“We won’t need it much longer, though,” said Chuck Foster.

“How’s that?” asked Gay, opening his half-closed eyes.

“Well, we’ve done about all the business we can do in this country. Practically all of the Negroes are white except a couple of thousand diehards and those in institutions,” Chuck informed him.

“Dat’s right,” said Hank. “An’ it sho makes dis heah country lonesome. Ah ain’t seen a brown-skin ooman in so long Ah doan know whut Ah’d do if Ah seen one.”
===Chinese===
「本届政府给过我们许许多多的关照，这一点我们感激不尽，」克鲁克曼大夫补了一句。

「不过，这层关照我们也用不了多久啦，」查克·福斯特说。

「此话怎讲？」盖伊问，半阖的眼睛睁了开来。

「是这样，这个国家里能做的生意，我们差不多做绝了。眼下的黑人几乎全成了白人，只剩千把个死硬分子，还有收在各色机构里的那一些。」查克告诉他。

「一点儿没错，」汉克说。「这国家如今冷清得真够呛。棕皮姑娘我可太久没见着了，冷不丁撞上一个，我都不知道该咋办好。」

===Original===
“That’s right, Gay,” added Dr. Crookman. “We’ve about cleaned up the Negro problem in this country. Next week we’re closing all except five of our sanitariums.”

“Well, what about your Lying-In hospitals?” asked Gay.

“Of course we’ll have to continue operating them,” Crookman replied. “The women would be in an awful fix if we didn’t.”

“Now look here,” proposed Gay, drawing closer to them and lowering his voice. “This coming campaign is going to be one of the bitterest in the history of this country. I fear there will be rioting, shooting and killing. Those hospitals cannot be closed without tremendous mental suffering to the womanhood of the country. We want to avoid that and you want to avoid it, too. Yet, these hospitals will constantly be in danger. It ought to be worth something to you to have them especially protected by the forces of the government.”

“You would do that anyway, wouldn’t you Gay?” asked Crookman.
===Chinese===
「正是如此，盖伊，」克鲁克曼大夫又补了一句。「这个国家的黑人问题，我们已经办得差不离了。下个礼拜起，我们的疗养院除了五家，其余统统关门。」

「那么，你们那些产科医院呢？」盖伊问。

「产院当然还得接着开，」克鲁克曼答道。「一关门，全国的太太们可就掉进天大的麻烦里了。」

「听我说，」盖伊凑近二人，压低了嗓门。「眼下一仗，会是这个国有史以来打得最凶的一仗。我担心会出暴乱、动枪、死人。那些产院一关门，全国的妇女同胞精神上就要受天大的罪。我们要避免这个，你们也想避免这个。可这些医院时时刻刻都处在危险之中。让政府的武力特别保护着它们，这对二位总该值几个钱吧。」

「这个你们反正也会做的，是不是，盖伊？」克鲁克曼问。

===Original===
“Well, it’s going to cost us millions of votes to do it, and the members of the National Executive Committee seem to feel that you ought to make a very liberal donation to the campaign fund to make up for the votes we’ll lose.”

“What would you call a liberal donation?” Crookman inquired.

“A successful campaign cannot be fought this year,” Gay replied, “under twenty millions.”

“Man,” shouted Hank, “yuh ain’t talkin’ ’bout dollahs, is yuh?”

“You got it right, Hank,” answered the National Chairman. “It’ll cost that much and maybe more.”
===Chinese===
「这个嘛，做这件事要搭上我们几百万张选票，全国执行委员会的委员们似乎觉得，二位该向竞选基金献上一笔非常慷慨的捐款，好贴补我们要丢的那些票。」

「多大一笔才算慷慨呢？」克鲁克曼问。

「今年这一仗要打赢，」盖伊答道，「两千万打不住。」

「我的爷，」汉克嚷起来，「你们说的不是大洋吧？」

「你没猜错，汉克，」全国主席答道。「就得这个数，兴许还要多。」

===Original===
“Where do you expect to get all of that money?” queried Foster.

“That’s just what’s worrying us,” Gay replied, “and that’s why I’m here. You fellows are rolling in wealth and we need your help. In the past two years you’ve collected around ninety million dollars from the Negro public. Why not give us a good break? You won’t miss five million, and it ought to be worth it to you fellows to defeat the Democrats.”

“Five millions! Great Day,” Hank exploded. “Man, is you los’ yo’ min’?”

“Not at all,” Gay denied. “Might as well own up that if we don’t get a contribution of about that size from you we’re liable to lose this election. … Come on, fellows, don’t be so tight. Of course, you’re setting pretty and all you’ve got to do is change your residence to Europe or some other place if things don’t run smoothly in America, but you want to think of those poor women with their black babies. What will they do if you fellows leave the country or if the Democrats win and you have to close all of your places?”

“That’s right, Chief,” Foster observed. “You can’t let the women down.”
===Chinese===
「这笔钱你们指望上哪儿弄去？」福斯特问道。

「这正是我们发愁的地方，」盖伊答道，「也是我到这儿来的缘由。各位老兄财源滚滚，我们需要你们搭把手。过去两年，你们从黑人公众身上拢共收了九千万上下。行行好，让我们也捞个好机会，何妨？五百万，你们不会心疼的；再说，把民主党打下去，这个价钱对各位总归是值的。」

「五百万！老天爷！」汉克炸了。「伙计，你脑子丢啦？」

「哪里的话，」盖伊否认。「不妨明说：你们要是不肯掏一笔这个数目的捐款，这场选举我们多半要输。……得啦，各位，别这么抠门。当然，你们眼下日子舒坦，美国的事一旦不顺，把宅子搬到欧洲或别的什么地方就是了；可你们也该想想那些抱着黑娃娃的可怜女人。你们各位要是拍拍屁股出国，或是民主党赢了、你们不得不把所有场子统统关掉，她们可怎么办呢？」

「正是这话，头儿，」福斯特说。「可不能叫娘们儿寒了心。」

===Original===
“Yeah,” said Johnson. “Give ’im th’ jack.”

“Well, suppose we do?” concluded Crookman, smiling.

The National Chairman was delighted. “When can we collect?” he asked, “and how?”

“Tomorrow, if yuh really wants it then,” Johnson observed.

“Now remember,” warned Gay. “We cannot afford to let it be known that we are getting such a large sum from any one person or corporation.”

“That’s your lookout,” said the physician, indifferently. “You know *we* won’t say anything.”

Mr. Gay, shortly afterward, departed to carry the happy news to the National Executive Committee, then in session right there in New York City.

---
===Chinese===
「就是，」约翰逊说。「把钞票给他就是了。」

「那好，就当我们掏了，如何？」克鲁克曼笑眯眯地一锤定音。

全国主席喜出望外。「什么时候可以来取？」他问，「怎么个取法？」

「明儿就成，只要你们真急等着要，」约翰逊说。

「可记住了，」盖伊提醒。「这么一大笔款子若是叫人知道出自某一个人或某一家公司手里，我们可吃罪不起。」

「那是你们自家的事，」大夫漠不关心地说。「你知道，『我们』是什么也不会说的。」

不多时，盖伊先生便起身告辞，把这桩喜讯送去给全国执行委员会——委员会当时恰巧就在纽约市开会。

---

===Original===
The Republicans certainly needed plenty of money to reelect President Goosie. The frequent radio addresses of Rev. Givens, the growing numbers of the Knights of Nordica, the inexplicable affluence of the Democratic Party and the vitriolic articles in *The Warning*, had not failed to rouse much Democratic sentiment. People were not exactly for the Democrats but they were against the Republicans. As early as May it did not seem possible for the Republicans to carry a single Southern state and many of the Northern and Eastern strongholds were in doubt. The Democrats seemed to have everything their way. Indeed, they were so confident of success that they were already counting the spoils.

When the Democratic Convention met in Jackson, Mississippi, on July 1, 1936, political wiseacres claimed that for the first time in history the whole program was cut and dried and would be run off smoothly and swiftly. Such, however, was not the case. The unusually hot sun, coupled with the enormous quantities of liquor vended, besides the many conflicting interests present, soon brought dissension.

Shortly after the keynote speech had been delivered by Senator Kretin, the Anglo-Saxon crowd let it be known that they wanted some distinguished Southerner like Arthur Snobbcraft nominated for the Presidency. The Knights of Nordica were intent on nominating Imperial Grand Wizard Givens. The Northern faction of the party, now reduced to a small minority in party councils, was holding out for former Governor Grogan of Massachusetts who as head of the League of Catholic Voters had a great following.
===Chinese===
要让古西（Goosie）总统连任，共和党人确实少不了大把大把的钞票。吉文斯牧师隔三差五的广播演说，北欧骑士团日益膨胀的会员，民主党来路莫测的阔绰，还有《警告报》上那些毒汁四溅的文章，把一股不小的民主党风煽动了起来。老百姓倒不见得拥护民主党，只是恨共和党罢了。早在五月，共和党就眼看连南方一个州也拿不下来，北方和东部的好些地盘也悬得很。民主党凡事顺风顺水。真的，他们胜券在握，已经在分肥了。

1936 年 7 月 1 日，民主党代表大会在密西西比州杰克逊市开幕。政界那些自作聪明的先生们夸口说，有史以来头一遭，全套程序早已板板六十四地定好，必定顺顺当当、麻麻利利地走完。可惜事与愿违。毒得出奇的日头，加上成山成海兜售的酒水，再搭上在场各路人马利益相左，没多久就吵成了一锅粥。

克雷廷参议员（Kretin）致完基调演说没多久，盎格鲁-撒克逊那帮人就放出了话风：总统候选人，他们要推一位像阿瑟·斯诺布克拉夫特（Snobbcraft）那样声名赫赫的南方人。北欧骑士团则一门心思要提名他们的帝国大巫师吉文斯。党内北方派在党内的决策圈里如今只剩一小撮，还在力挺马萨诸塞州前州长格罗根（Grogan）——此人身为天主教选民联盟的首领，门徒甚众。

===Original===
Through twenty ballots the voting proceeded, and it remained deadlocked. No faction would yield. Leaders saw that there had to be a compromise. They retired to a suite on the top floor of the Judge Lynch Hotel. There, in their shirt sleeves, with collars open, mint juleps on the table and electric fans stirring up the hot air, they got down to business. Twelve hours later they were still there.

Matthew, wilted, worn but determined, fought for his chief. Simeon Dump of the Anglo-Saxon Association swore he would not withdraw the name of Arthur Snobbcraft. Rev. John Whiffle, a power in the party, gulped drink after drink, kept dabbing a damp handkerchief at the shining surface of his skull, and held out for one Bishop Belch. Moses Lejewski of New York argued obstinately for the nomination of Governor Grogan.

In the meantime the delegates, having left the oven-like convention hall, either lay panting and drinking in their rooms, sat in the hotel lobbies discussing the deadlock or cruised the streets in automobiles confidently seeking the dens of iniquity which they had been told were eager to lure them into sin.

When the clock struck three, Matthew rose and suggested that since the Knights of Nordica and the Anglo-Saxon Association were the two most powerful organizations in the party, Givens should get the presidential nomination, Snobbcraft the vice-presidential and the other candidates be assured of cabinet positions. This suggested compromise appealed to no one except Matthew.
===Chinese===
投票一连投了二十轮，还是僵着，哪一派也不肯相让。各派领袖看出非妥协不可，便退到林奇法官旅馆（Judge Lynch Hotel）顶楼的一间套房里。在那里，一个个只穿衬衫、敞着领口，桌上摆着薄荷朱利酒，电扇搅着热风，他们办起正事来。十二个钟头之后，他们还在那儿。

马修蔫了、累了，却毫不松口，为他的主子力撑到底。盎格鲁-撒克逊协会的西米恩·邓普（Simeon Dump）赌咒发誓，绝不撤回阿瑟·斯诺布克拉夫特的名字。党内一位有头有脸的人物约翰·惠弗尔牧师（Whiffle）一杯接一杯地灌酒，不住地拿湿手绢揩他那颗油光锃亮的秃脑袋，咬牙力挺一位贝尔奇主教（Bishop Belch）。纽约来的摩西·列耶夫斯基（Lejewski）则犟头犟脑，非要提名格罗根州长不可。

这边厢，代表们逃出那座烤炉般的会堂，有的躺在房里喘气灌酒，有的坐在旅馆大堂里议论僵局，有的开着汽车满街转悠，满有把握地四处寻觅那些据说急不可耐要引诱他们堕入罪孽的销金窟。

钟敲三点，马修站起身来提议：北欧骑士团和盎格鲁-撒克逊协会既是党内最拿分量的两大团体，总统提名就该归吉文斯，副总统归斯诺布克拉夫特，其余各位候选人一律保证入阁。这道折中方案，除了马修自己，谁也不感冒。

===Original===
“You people forget,” said Simeon Dump, “that the Anglo-Saxon Association is putting up half the money to finance this campaign.”

“And you forget,” declared Moses Lejewski, “that we’re supporting your crazy scheme to disfranchise anybody possessing Negro ancestry when we get into office. That’s going to cost us millions of votes in the North. You fellows can’t expect to hog everything.”

“Why not?” challenged Dump. “How could you win without money?”

“And how,” added Matthew, “can you get anywhere without the Knights of Nordica behind you?”

“And how,” Rev. Whiffle chimed in, “can you get anywhere without the Fundamentalists and the Drys?”
===Chinese===
「诸位忘了，」西米恩·邓普说，「这场竞选的经费，一半是盎格鲁-撒克逊协会掏的。」

「你们也忘了，」摩西·列耶夫斯基声明，「我们上台之后，还要撑你们那个疯点子，把凡带一丝黑人血统的统统剥掉选举权。这在北方要搭上我们几百万张票。你们这伙人可不能好处独占啊。」

「怎么不能？」邓普诘问。「没有钱，你们拿什么赢？」

「那又怎么着，」马修接上一句，「没有北欧骑士团撑腰，你们又走得了几步？」

「还有怎么着，」惠弗尔牧师凑趣道，「没有原教旨派和禁酒派，你们又走得了几步？」

===Original===
At four o’clock they had got no farther than they had been at three. They tried to pick someone not before mentioned, and went over and over the list of eligibles. None was satisfactory. One was too radical, another was too conservative, a third was an atheist, a fourth had once rifled a city treasury, the fifth was of immigrant extraction once removed, a sixth had married a Jewess, a seventh was an intellectual, an eighth had spent too long at Hot Springs trying to cure the syphilis, a ninth was rumored to be part Mexican and a tenth had at one time in his early youth been a Socialist.

At five o’clock they were desperate, drunk and disgusted. The stuffy room was a litter of discarded collars, cigarette and cigar butts, match stems, heaped ash trays and empty bottles. Matthew drank little and kept insisting on the selection of Rev. Givens. To the sodden and nodding men he painted marvelous pictures of the spoils of office and their excellent chance of getting there, and then suddenly declared that the Knights of Nordica would withdraw unless Givens was nominated. The threat aroused them. They cursed and called it a holdup, but Matthew was adamant. As a last stroke, he rose and pretended to be ready to bolt the caucus. They remonstrated with him and finally gave in to him.

Orders went out to the delegates. They assembled in the convention hall. The shepherds of the various state flocks cracked the whip and the delegates voted accordingly. Late that afternoon the news went out to a waiting world that the Democrats had nominated Henry Givens for President and Arthur Snobbcraft for Vice-President. Mr. Snobbcraft didn’t like that at all, but it was better than nothing.

---
===Chinese===
到四点钟，他们还在三点钟的老地方踏步。他们试着物色此前没提过的人，把够格的名单翻来覆去筛了一遍又一遍。一个也不称心。这个太激进，那个太保守；第三个是无神论者，第四个偷偷摸过市里的金库；第五个往上数一代是移民出身，第六个娶过犹太女人；第七个是个知识分子，第八个在温泉疗养地泡得太久，想治好那杨梅疮；第九个风传带着一半墨西哥血统，第十个少不更事的时候竟当过社会党人。

到五点钟，他们绝望了、喝高了、也腻歪透了。闷热的房间里一片狼藉：扔掉的硬领，香烟雪茄的烟屁股，火柴棍，堆成山的烟灰缸，还有空酒瓶。马修没怎么喝，只是一口咬定非吉文斯牧师不可。他给那帮烂醉如泥、点头打盹的家伙描画官家肥缺的美妙前景，说他们大有希望染指；跟着突然摊牌：不提名吉文斯，北欧骑士团就退出。这一吓唬果然把大伙惊醒了。他们骂骂咧咧，说这是明火执仗的敲竹杠，可马修铁了心。最后一招，他站起身来，装出要拂袖搅散核心会议的架势。众人好言相劝，到头来还是依了他。

命令传到各代表团。他们重新集合在会堂里。各州羊群的牧羊人鞭子一甩，代表们便照章投票。那天傍晚，消息发往翘首以待的全世界：民主党提名亨利·吉文斯竞选总统，阿瑟·斯诺布克拉夫特竞选副总统。斯诺布克拉夫特先生心里一百个不情愿，可有总比没有强。

---

===Original===
A few days later the Republican convention opened in Chicago. Better disciplined, as usual, than the Democrats, its business proceeded like clockwork. President Goosie was nominated for reelection on the first ballot and Vice-President Gump was again selected as his running mate. A platform was adopted whose chief characteristic was vagueness. As was customary, it stressed the party’s record in office, except that which was criminal; it denounced fanaticism without being specific, and it emphasized the rights of the individual and the trusts in the same paragraph. As the Democratic slogan was White Supremacy and its platform dwelt largely on the necessity of genealogical investigation, the Republicans adopted the slogan: Personal Liberty and Ancestral Sanctity.

Dr. Crookman and his associates, listening in on the radio in his suite in the Robin Hood Hotel in New York City, laughed softly as they heard the President deliver his speech of acceptance which ended in the following original manner:

“And finally, my friends, I can only say that we shall continue in the path of rugged individualism, free from the influence of sinister interests, upholding the finest ideals of honesty, independence and integrity, so that, to quote Abraham Lincoln, ‘This nation of the people, for the people and by the people shall not perish from the earth.’”

“That,” said Foster, as the President ceased barking, “sounds almost like the speech of acceptance of Brother Givens that we heard the other day.”

Dr. Crookman smiled and brushed the ashes off his cigar. “It may even be the same speech,” he suggested.

---
===Chinese===
几天之后，共和党代表大会在芝加哥开幕。照例比民主党军纪严明，事务办得像钟表一样分毫不乱。古西总统第一轮投票就获得连任提名，副总统冈普（Gump）再次被选中当他的竞选搭档。大会通过了一部政纲，最大的特色是含糊其辞。照老规矩，它大力表彰本党的执政业绩——唯独撇开犯法的那些；它谴责狂热行径，却不点明是谁的；在同一段文字里，既强调个人的权利，又强调托拉斯的权利。民主党的口号既然是「白人至上」，政纲又大书特书查家谱的必要，共和党便针锋相对，提出了口号：「个人自由，祖源神圣」。

克鲁克曼大夫和几位合伙人正在纽约市罗宾汉旅馆他的套房里守着收音机。总统发表接受提名演说，结尾别开生面，竟是这样收的场；他们听着听着，低低地笑出了声。

「最后，朋友们，我只能说，我们将继续走坚韧不拔的个人主义道路，不受邪恶利益集团的摆布，恪守诚实、自立与正直这些最高尚的理想，俾使这个民有、民治、民享的国家不致从地球上消亡——此乃亚伯拉罕·林肯之言。」

总统停止了吠叫。「这话，」福斯特说，「听着简直跟前几天我们听到的吉文斯老兄那篇接受提名演说差不多嘛。」

克鲁克曼大夫微微一笑，掸了掸雪茄上的烟灰。「没准儿根本就是同一篇，」他说。

---

===Original===
Through the hot days of July and August the campaign slowly got under way. Innumerable photographs appeared in the newspapers depicting the rival candidates among the simple folk of some village, helping youngsters to pick cherries, assisting an old woman up a stairway, bathing in the old swimming hole, eating at a barbecue and posing on the rear platforms of special trains.

Long articles appeared in the Sunday newspapers extolling the simple virtues of the two great men. Both, it seemed, had come from poor but honest families; both were hailed as tried and true friends of the great, common people; both were declared to be ready to give their strength and intellect to America for the next four years. One writer suggested that Givens resembled Lincoln, while another declared that President Goosie’s character was not unlike that of Roosevelt, believing he was paying the former a compliment.

Rev. Givens told the reporters: “It is my intention, if elected, to carry out the traditional tariff policy of the Democratic Party” (neither he or anyone else knew what that was).

President Goosie averred again and again, “I intend to make my second term as honest and efficient as my first.” Though a dire threat, this statement was supposed to be a fine promise.

---
===Chinese===
七八月的酷暑天里，竞选慢腾腾地拉开了场子。报纸上登出数不清的照片：两位对垒的候选人置身某个村子的淳朴乡民中间，帮娃娃们摘樱桃，搀老太婆上台阶，在老游泳池里扑腾，在烧烤野宴上大嚼，还站在专列的尾平台上摆姿势。

星期天版面上又登出长文，颂扬两位伟人朴素的美德。两位看来都出身清寒而正派的人家；都被捧成伟大的普通老百姓久经考验的真挚朋友；都宣称准备在今后四年为美利坚献出自己的膂力与才智。有位作者暗示吉文斯酷肖林肯；另一位则宣称古西总统的品格同罗斯福颇为相像，还自以为这话是对前者的恭维。

吉文斯牧师对记者们说：「倘若当选，我打算奉行民主党传统的关税政策」（他和别的什么人都不知道那政策是什么玩意儿）。

古西总统三番五次地保证：「我打算让我的第二个任期，同第一个任期一样诚实，一样有效率。」这话虽说是个凶险的威胁，世人却把它当成一句漂亮的许诺。

---

===Original===
Meanwhile, Dr. Samuel Buggerie and his operatives were making great headway examining birth and marriage records throughout the United States. Around the middle of September the Board of Directors held a conference at which the learned man presented a partial report.

“I am now prepared to prove,” gloated the obese statistician, “that fully one-quarter of the people of one Virginia county possess non-white ancestry, Indian or Negro; and we can further prove that all of the Indians on the Atlantic Coast are part Negro. In several counties in widely separated parts of the country, we have found that the ancestry of a considerable percentage of the people is in doubt. There is reason to believe that there are countless numbers of people who ought not to be classed with whites and should not mix with Anglo-Saxons.”

It was decided that the statistician should get his data in simple form that anyone could read and understand, and have it ready to release just a few days before election. When the people saw how great was the danger from black blood, it was reasoned, they would flock to the Democratic standard and it would be too late for the Republicans to halt the stampede.

---
===Chinese===
与此同时，塞缪尔·比热里博士（Buggerie）带着手下调查员，翻查全美国的出生和婚姻档案，进展神速。九月中旬前后，董事会开了一次会，这位博学之士提交了一份阶段性报告。

「我现在已经拿得出证据了，」肥硕的统计学家得意洋洋，「弗吉尼亚州有一个县，足足四分之一的居民带着非白人血统，不是印第安人就是黑人；我们还能进一步证明，大西洋沿岸所有的印第安人都掺着黑人血。在全国天南地北的好几个县，我们发现相当大比例的居民血统存疑。有理由相信，数不清的人本不该划进白人里头，更不该同盎格鲁-撒克逊人通婚混血。」

于是议定：统计学家须把数据整理成人人能读能懂的浅显样式，赶在选举前几天发布。他们盘算着，老百姓一看清黑血的危害有多大，就会成群结队投奔民主党的旗帜，到时候共和党再想拦住这场人仰马翻的大溃逃，可就来不及了。

---

===Original===
No political campaign in the history of the country had ever been so bitter. On one side were those who were fanatically positive of their pure Caucasian ancestry; on the other side were those who knew themselves to be “impure” white or had reason to suspect it. The former were principally Democratic, the latter Republican. There was another group which was Republican because it felt that a victory for the Democrats might cause another Civil War. The campaign roused acrimonious dispute even within families. Often behind these family rifts lurked the knowledge or suspicion of a dark past.

As the campaign grew more bitter, denunciations of Dr. Crookman and his activities grew more violent. A move was started to close all of his hospitals. Some wanted them to be closed for all time; others advised their closing for the duration of the campaign. The majority of thinking people (which wasn’t so many) strenuously objected to the proposal.

“No good purpose will be served by closing these hospitals,” declared the New York *Morning Earth*. “On the contrary such a step might have tragic results. The Negroes have disappeared into the body of our citizenry, large numbers have intermarried with the whites and the offspring of these marriages are appearing in increasing numbers. Without these hospitals, think how many couples would be estranged; how many homes wrecked! Instead of taking precipitate action, we should be patient and move slowly.”

Other Northern newspapers assumed an even more friendly attitude, but the press generally followed the crowd, or led it, and in slightly veiled language urged the opponents of Black-No-More to take the law into their hands.

Finally, emboldened and inflamed by fiery editorials, radio addresses, pamphlets, posters and platform speeches, a mob seeking to protect white womanhood in Cincinnati attacked a Crookman hospital, drove several women into the streets and set fire to the building. A dozen babies were burned to death and others, hastily removed by their mothers, were recognized as mulattoes. The newspapers published names and addresses. Many of the women were very prominent socially either in their own right or because of their husbands.

The nation was shocked as never before. Republican sentiment began to dwindle. The Republican Executive Committee met and discussed ways and means of combating the trend. Gorman Gay was at his wits’ end. Nothing, he thought, could save them except a miracle.
===Chinese===
这个国有史以来，还从没有哪一场竞选打得这样恶毒。一边，是那些狂热地确信自家血统纯属高加索纯种的人；另一边，是那些自知是「不纯」白人、或者有缘由起疑的人。前者多半投民主党，后者多半投共和党。还有一伙人投共和党，是因为他们觉得民主党一旦得胜，说不定又要打一场南北战争。这场竞选煽起的恶斗，连一家人内部也吵翻了天。而这些家庭裂痕的背后，往往潜伏着对一段黑色往事的知情，或者疑心。

选战越打越毒，对克鲁克曼大夫及其营生的声讨也越来越凶。有人发起运动，要关掉他所有的医院。有的主张一劳永逸地查封；有的建议选举期间暂时关门。多数有头脑的人（这样的人并不很多）激烈反对这项提案。

纽约《大地晨报》（Morning Earth）宣称：「关闭这些医院，一点好处也捞不着。相反，这一步可能酿成悲剧。黑人已经隐没进公民大众的躯体里，大批的人同白人通了婚，这类婚姻的子女正一天多似一天。没有了这些医院，想一想吧，有多少夫妻要成陌路，多少家庭要遭拆散！我们不该贸然行事，而应当耐心，慢慢来。」

北方其他报纸摆出的姿态还要亲善些，可报界总体上是跟着人群跑，或者说领着人群跑，用半遮半掩的字眼，撺掇「黑色终结」的反对者们把法律攥到自己手里。

到头来，在火辣辣的社论、广播演说、小册子、招贴画和讲坛演说的一路鼓动加煽惑之下，辛辛那提一伙暴徒打着保卫白人女性的旗号，袭击了一家克鲁克曼医院，把好几个女人赶到大街上，放火烧了房子。十二个婴儿活活烧死；另外几个叫母亲们抢出来的，一眼就被人认出是混血儿。报纸把姓名住址统统登了出来。许多女人要么自身、要么因为丈夫，在社会上颇有头脸。

全国从来没这么震惊过。共和党的声势开始往下出溜。共和党执行委员会开会，商讨遏止这股潮流的方略。戈曼·盖伊一筹莫展。他想，除非出奇迹，什么也救不了他们了。

===Original===
Two flights below in a spacious office sat two of the Republican campaigners, Walter Williams and Joseph Bonds, busily engaged in leading the other workers (who knew better) to believe that they were earning the ten dollars a day they were receiving. The former had passed for a Negro for years on the strength of a part-Negro grandparent and then gone back to the white race when the National Social Equality League was forced to cease operations at the insistence of both the sheriff and the landlord. Joseph Bonds, former head of the Negro Data League who had once been a Negro but thanks to Dr. Crookman was now Caucasian and proud of it, had but recently returned to the North from Atlanta, accompanied by Santop Licorice. Both Mr. Williams and Mr. Bonds had been unable to stomach the Democratic crowd and so had fallen in with the Republicans, who were as different from them as one billiard ball from another. The two gentlemen were in low tones discussing the dilemma of the Republicans, while rustling papers to appear busy.

“Jo, if we could figure out something to turn the tables on these Democrats, we wouldn’t have to work for the rest of our lives,” Williams observed, blowing a cloud of cigarette smoke out of the other corner of his mouth.

“Yes, that’s right, Walt, but there ain’t a chance in the world. Old Gay is almost crazy, you know. Came in here slamming doors and snapping at everybody this morning,” Bonds remarked.

Williams leaned closer to him, lowered his flame-thatched head and then looking to the right and left whispered, “Listen here, do you know where Beard is?”
===Chinese===
隔下两层楼，一间宽敞的办公室里，坐着共和党的两名竞选干将，沃尔特·威廉斯（Williams）和约瑟夫·邦兹（Bonds）。两位正忙着装模作样，领着别的职员（人家心里明白得很）相信自己真值那十块钱一天的工钱。前者凭着一个带黑人血统的祖辈，冒充黑人混了好些年，直到全国社会平等联盟（National Social Equality League）被警长和房东两头逼着关了张，他才回归白人一族。约瑟夫·邦兹从前是黑人资料同盟（Negro Data League）的头头，原本是黑人，托克鲁克曼大夫的福，如今已是高加索人，并且引以为荣；前不久刚带着桑托普·利科里斯从亚特兰大回到北方。威廉斯先生和邦兹先生都咽不下民主党那路人马，便一头扎进了共和党——这党跟他们之间的差别，就好比一颗台球跟另一颗台球的差别。两位先生正低声议论共和党的困境，手底下则把纸张翻得哗哗响，装出一副忙碌的样子。

「乔，咱们要是能想出个招儿，叫这些民主党人栽个跟头，后半辈子就不用干活啦，」威廉斯说，从嘴角的另一边喷出一口香烟的烟雾。

「说得对，沃尔特，可天底下哪有那样的好事。你知道，老盖伊都快疯了。今儿早上进来，门摔得砰砰响，见谁冲谁嚷嚷。」邦兹说。

威廉斯凑得更近，压下他那头火焰似的乱发脑袋，左右张望了一番，低声说：「听着，你知道比尔德这会儿在哪儿吗？」

===Original===
“No,” answered Bonds, starting and looking around to see if anyone was listening. “Where is he?”

“Well, I got a letter from him the other day. He’s down there in Richmond doing research work for the Anglo-Saxon Association under that Dr. Buggerie.”

“Do they know who he is?”

“Of course they don’t. He’s been white quite a while now, you know, and of course they’d never connect him with the Dr. Shakespeare A. Beard who used to be one of their most outspoken enemies.”

“Well, what about it?” persisted Bonds, eagerly. “Do you think he might know something on the Democrats that might help?”
===Chinese===
「不知道，」邦兹应声一惊，四下看看有没有人偷听。「他在哪儿？」

「嗐，前两天他给我来了封信。他在里士满，给盎格鲁-撒克逊协会做事，在那个比热里博士手底下搞研究。」

「他们知道他是谁吗？」

「他们哪里知道。他变白都好一阵子了，你知道的，他们绝不会把他跟那个莎士比亚·A·比尔德博士联想到一块儿去——那可是他们当年骂得最响的死对头之一。」

「那又怎么样？」邦兹紧追不舍，两眼放光。「你觉得他手里会攥着民主党的什么把柄，能帮上忙的？」

===Original===
“He might. We could try him out anyway. If he knows anything he’ll spill it because he hates that crowd.”

“How will you get in touch with him quickly? Write to him?”

“Certainly not,” growled Williams. “I’ll get expenses from Gay for the trip. He’ll fall for anything now.”

He rose and made for the elevator. Five minutes later he was standing before his boss, the National Chairman, a worried, gray little man with an aldermanic paunch and a convict’s mouth.

“What is it, Williams?” snapped the Chairman.
===Chinese===
「兴许有。反正可以试试他。他真知道点什么，准会倒个干净——他恨死那帮人了。」

「你怎么赶紧联系上他？写信去？」

「那可万万不能，」威廉斯咕哝道。「这笔差旅费我去找盖伊报。这会儿他什么都信。」

他起身朝电梯走去。五分钟后，他已经站在他的顶头上司——全国主席的面前了：一位愁眉苦脸、头发灰白的小个子，长着市议员式的将军肚和一张囚犯嘴。

「什么事，威廉斯？」主席劈头便问。

===Original===
“I’d like to get expenses to Richmond,” said Williams. “I have a friend down there in Snobbcraft’s office and he might have some dope we can use to our advantage.”

“Scandal?” asked Mr. Gay, brightening.

“Well, I don’t know right now, of course, but this fellow is a very shrewd observer and in six months’ time he ought to have grabbed something that’ll help us out of this jam.”

“Is he a Republican or a Democrat?”

“Neither. He’s a highly trained and competent social student. You couldn’t expect him to be either,” Williams observed. “But I happen to know that he hasn’t got any money to speak of, so for a consideration I’m sure he’ll spill everything he knows, if anything.”
===Chinese===
「我想领一笔去里士满的差费，」威廉斯说。「我有个朋友在那边斯诺布克拉夫特的班子里，他手里兴许有些内幕，能派上咱们的用场。」

「丑闻？」盖伊先生眼睛一亮。

「这个眼下自然说不准，不过这位老兄眼光毒得很，这半年下来，手里总该攥着点什么，能帮咱们脱这个困。」

「他是共和党还是民主党？」

「都不是。他是受过严格训练、很有一套的社会研究者，哪一党也指望不上，」威廉斯说。「不过我恰好知道，他穷得叮当响，只要报酬给足，他真知道点什么的话，保管一字不落全倒出来。」

===Original===
“Well, its a gamble,” said Gay, doubtfully, “but any port in a storm.”

Williams left Washington immediately for Richmond. That night he sat in a cramped little room of the former champion of the darker races.

“What are you doing down there, Beard?” asked Williams, referring to the headquarters of the Anglo-Saxon Association.

“Oh, I’m getting, or helping to get, that data of Buggerie’s into shape.”

“What data? You told me you were doing research work. Now you say you’re arranging data. Have they finished collecting it?”
===Chinese===
「唔，这是场赌注，」盖伊将信将疑，「不过风暴之中，不择港口嘛。」

威廉斯当即离开华盛顿去了里士满。当天夜里，他坐在这位昔日暗肤色种族卫士那间逼仄的小屋里。

「比尔德，你在那底下干的什么差事？」威廉斯问，指的是盎格鲁-撒克逊协会的总部。

「哦，我在整理，或者说帮忙整理，比热里那批数据。」

「什么数据？你信里明明说搞研究，如今又说是整理数据。他们收集完了？」

===Original===
“Yes, we finished that job some time ago. Now we’re trying to get the material in shape for easy digestion.”

“What do you mean: easy digestion?” queried Williams. “What are you fellows trying to find out and why must it be so easily digested. You fellows usually try to make your stuff unintelligible to the herd.”

“This is different,” said Beard, lowering his voice to almost a whisper. “We’re under a pledge of secrecy. We have been investigating the family trees of the nation and so far, believe me, we certainly have uncovered astounding facts.
