When I’m finally discharged, which will probably be after election, I’m going to peddle some of that information. Snobbcraft and even Buggerie are not aware of the inflammatory character of the facts we’ve assembled.” He narrowed his foxy eyes greedily.

“Is it because they’ve been planning to release some of it that they want it in easily digestible form, as you say?” pressed Williams.

“That’s it exactly,” declared Beard, stroking his now clean-shaven face. “I overheard Buggerie and Snobbcraft chuckling about it only a day or two ago.”
===Chinese===
「早完事了，前一阵就收齐了。眼下正把材料调理得容易消化。」

「『容易消化』是什么意思？」威廉斯追问。「你们这帮人到底想查个什么，干吗非得整理得这么好消化？你们平素写东西，不是存心叫老百姓看不懂吗？」

「这回不一样，」比尔德说，嗓门压得几乎成了耳语。「我们立过保密的誓。我们在查全国的家族谱系，老实跟你讲，到眼下为止，挖出来的事实已经够惊人了。等最后散伙——多半在选举以后——我打算拿其中一些情报去卖钱。斯诺布克拉夫特，连比热里在内，都没掂出我们攒下的这些东西有多大的爆性。」他贪婪地眯起那双狐狸眼。

「他们打算放出其中一些，所以才要弄成你说的那种好消化的样式，是不是这个缘故？」威廉斯紧逼一句。

「一点儿不差，」比尔德拍着他如今刮得干干净净的脸腮。「就在一两天前，我还亲耳听见比热里跟斯诺布克拉夫特为这个吃吃地笑呢。」

===Original===
“Well, there must be a whole lot of it,” insinuated Williams, “if they’ve had all of you fellows working for six months. Where all did you work?”

“Oh, all over. North as well as South. We’ve got a whole basement vault full of index cards.”

“I guess they’re keeping close watch over it, aren’t they?” asked Williams.

“Sure. It would take an army to get in that vault.”
===Chinese===
「嗐，那量一定小不了，」威廉斯旁敲侧击，「不然犯得上让你们这帮人足足干上半年。你们都上过哪些地方？」

「哦，哪儿都去，南方北方都有。我们现在有一整个地下室金库的索引卡片。」

「我想他们看得死死的吧，啊？」威廉斯问。

「那还用说。想进那间金库，得拉一支队伍来。」

===Original===
“Well, I guess they don’t want anything to happen to the stuff before they spring it,” observed the man from Republican headquarters.

Soon afterward Williams left Dr. Beard, took a stroll around the Anglo-Saxon Association’s stately headquarters building, noted the half-dozen tough looking guards about it and then caught the last train for the capital city. The next morning he had a long talk with Gorman Gay.

“It’s okeh, Jo,” he whispered to Bonds, later, as he passed his desk.
===Chinese===
「我琢磨，东西没甩出来之前，他们可不想出什么岔子，」这位共和党总部来客说道。

没过多久，威廉斯辞别比尔德博士，绕着盎格鲁-撒克逊协会那座气派堂皇的总部大楼转了一圈，看清楼里楼外守着六七个横眉立目的警卫，随后赶上了回首都的末班车。第二天上午，他同戈曼·盖伊长谈了一气。

「成了，乔，」后来他路过邦兹的写字桌时，悄悄说了一句。

## XI / 十一

===Original===
“What’s the matter with you, Matt?” asked Bunny one morning about a month before election. “Ain’t everything going okeh? You look as if we’d lost the election and failed to elect that brilliant intellectual, Henry Givens, President of the United States.”

“Well, we might just as well lose it as far as I’m concerned,” said Matthew, “if I don’t find a way out of this jam I’m in.”

“What jam?”

“Well, Helen got in the family way last winter again. I sent her to Palm Beach and the other resorts, thinking the travel and exercise might bring on another miscarriage.”

“Did it?”

“Not a chance in the world. Then, to make matters worse, she miscalculates. At first she thought she would be confined in December; now she tells me she’s only got about three weeks to go.”

===Chinese===
“马特，你这是怎么啦？”距大选还有约摸一个月的一天早晨，邦尼问道。“事情不都顺顺当当的吗？瞧你这副丧气样儿，倒像咱们已经输了选举，没能让那位才华盖世的知识分子亨利·吉文斯（Henry Givens）当上美利坚合众国总统似的。”

“唉，就我而言，输就输了，没什么两样，”马修（Matthew）说，“要是我想不出法子脱身，这摊烂事可就把我黏住了。”

“什么烂事？”

“唉，海伦（Helen）去年冬天又怀上了。我把她送去棕榈滩（Palm Beach）那些度假胜地，心想旅途劳顿加上多活动，兴许能让她像上回那样把胎颠掉。”

“颠掉了吗？”

“门儿都没有。更糟的是，她还算错了日子。起先她估摸着十二月才临盆；如今却告诉我，只剩三个来星期了。”

===Original===
“Say not so!”

“I’m preaching gospel.”

“Well, hush my mouth! Waddya gonna do? You can’t send her to one o’ Crookman’s hospitals, it would be too dangerous right now.”

“That’s just it. You see, I figured she wouldn’t be ready until about a month after election when everything had calmed down, and I could send her then.”

“Would she have gone?”

“She couldn’t afford not to with her old man the President of the United States.”

===Chinese===
“我的爷，不会吧！”

“句句属实。”

“嚯，快捂住我的嘴！那你打算咋办？总不能把她送进克鲁克曼（Crookman）开的那号医院吧，这当口太危险了。”

“难就难在这儿。你想，我原算着她要到选举过后一个月才会生，那时一切都消停了，我到时再送她走。”

“那她会肯去吗？”

“她老爹是美利坚合众国总统，她不去还行？”

===Original===
“Well, whaddya gonna do, Big Boy? Think fast! Think fast! Them three weeks will get away from here in no time.”

“Don’t I know it?”

“What about an abortion?” suggested Bunny, hopefully.

“Nothing doing. First place, she’s too frail, and second place she’s got some fool idea about that being a sin.”

“About the only thing for you to do, then,” said Bunny, “is to get ready to pull out when that kid is born.”

“Oh, Bunny, I’d hate to leave Helen. She’s really the only woman I ever loved, you know. Course she’s got her prejudices and queer notions like everybody else but she’s really a little queen. She’s been an inspiration to me, too, Bunny. Every time I talk about pulling out of this game when things don’t go just right, she makes me stick it out. I guess I’d have been gone after I cleaned up that first million if it hadn’t been for her.”

===Chinese===
“那可怎么办呐，大个子？快想！快想！这三个星期一眨眼就溜过去了。”

“我能不知道？”

“要不打掉？”邦尼满怀指望地提议。

“没门。一来她身子太弱，二来她脑子里犯了傻，认定那是桩罪过。”

“那照我看，”邦尼说，“你顶好就是先备着，等那孩子一落地就开溜。”

“唉，邦尼，我真舍不得撇下海伦。你要知道，她实在是我这辈子唯一爱过的女人。当然，她也跟旁人一样有成见、有怪癖，可她实在是个小皇后。她也一直是我的一帖强心剂，邦尼。每回事情不顺、我念叨着要抽身退出这行当，她总逼着我撑下去。要不是她，我捞够头一个一百万的时候就早走了。”

===Original===
“You’d have been better off if you had,” Bunny commented.

“Oh, I don’t know. She’s hot for me to become Secretary of State or Ambassador to England or something like that; and the way things are going it looks like I will be. That is, if I can get out of this fix.”

“If you can get out o’ this jam, Matt, I’ll sure take my hat off to you. An’ I know how you feel about scuttling out and leaving her. I had a broad like that once in Harlem. ’Twas through her I got that job in th’ bank. She was crazy about me, Boy, until she caught me two-timin’. Then she tried to shoot me.

“Squaws are funny that way,” Bunny continued, philosophically. “Since I’ve been white I’ve found out they’re all the same, white or black. Kipling was right. They’ll fight to get you, fight to keep you and fight you when they catch you playin’ around. But th’ kinda woman that won’t fight for a man ain’t worth havin’.”

===Chinese===
“那样你如今日子还好过些哩。”邦尼点评道。

“哦，这可说不准。她一心巴望我当上国务卿或是驻英大使什么的；瞧眼下这势头，还真像要成。前提是，我能脱了这身困境。”

“马特，你要真能脱身，我算服了，准给你脱帽致敬。撇下她偷偷溜号是什么滋味，我也懂。当年我在哈莱姆（Harlem）就有过这么一位娘们儿。我在那家银行的差事，还是打她那儿弄来的。她迷我迷得发狂，老兄，直到逮着我脚踏两条船。随后她就朝我开了枪。

“娘们儿就是这路古怪脾气，”邦尼一派哲理地续道。“打从我变成白人才看透，天下的女人全一个样，白的黑的不分。吉卜林（Kipling）说得没错。为了到手你，她们肯拼；为了拴住你，她们肯拼；等你偷腥叫她们逮着，她们照样跟你拼。可那种不肯为男人拼一拼的女人，压根不值一要。”

===Original===
“So you think I ought to pull out, eh Bunny?” asked the worried Matthew, returning to the subject.

“Well, what I’d suggest is this:” his plump friend advised, “about time you think Helen’s gonna be confined, get together as much cash as you can and keep your plane ready. Then, when the baby’s born, go to her, tell her everything an’ offer to take her away with you. If she won’t go, you beat it; if she will, why everything’s hotsy totsy.” Bunny extended his soft pink hands expressively.

“Well, that sounds pretty good, Bunny.”

“It’s your best bet, Big Boy,” said his friend and secretary.

---

===Chinese===
“这么说，你是赞成我开溜喽，邦尼？”发愁的马修把话头拽了回来。

“依我说呀，是这么个章程：”他那胖乎乎的朋友支招道，“眼瞅着海伦快临盆的时候，能凑多少现钱就凑多少，把飞机也备好。等孩子一落地，就去见她，把一切都和盘托出，提出带她一道走。她不肯，你就拍屁股走人；她肯，那就万事大吉喽。”邦尼意味深长地摊开他那双软乎乎的粉红手掌。

“嗯，这听着倒不赖，邦尼。”

“这是你的上上之策，大个子，”他的朋友兼秘书说。

---

===Original===
Two days before election the situation was unchanged. There was joy in the Democratic camp, gloom among the Republicans. For the first time in American history it seemed that money was not going to decide an election. The propagandists and publicity men of the Democrats had so played upon the fears and prejudices of the public that even the bulk of Jews and Catholics were wavering and many had been won over to the support of a candidate who had denounced them but a few months before. In this they were but running true to form, however, as they had usually been on the side of white supremacy in the old days when there was a Negro population observable to the eye. The Republicans sought to dig up some scandal against Givens and Snobbcraft but were dissuaded by their Committee on Strategy which feared to set so dangerous a precedent. There were also politicians in their ranks who were guilty of adulteries, drunkenness and grafting.

The Republicans, Goosie and Gump, and the Democrats, Givens and Snobbcraft, had ended their swings around the country and were resting from their labors. There were parades in every city and country town. Minor orators beat the lectern from the Atlantic to the Pacific extolling the imaginary virtues of the candidates of the party that hired them. Dr. Crookman was burned a hundred times in effigy. Several Lying-In hospitals were attacked. Two hundred citizens who knew nothing about either candidate were arrested for fighting over which was the better man.

The air was electric with expectancy. People stood around in knots. Small boys scattered leaflets on ten million doorsteps. Police were on the alert to suppress disorder, except what they created.

---

===Chinese===
距选举还有两天，局势毫无变化。民主党营里欢天喜地，共和党这边愁云惨雾。美国有史以来头一遭，金钱似乎决定不了一场选举的胜负。民主党的宣传家和造势好手把公众的恐惧与偏见撩拨得如此到家，连犹太人和天主教徒里的大多数都摇摆起来，许多人竟转而拥护那位几个月前还把他们骂得狗血淋头的候选人。不过在这一层上，他们也不过是照老章程办事罢了：想当年黑人还满眼可见的岁月里，他们向来就是站在白人至上那一边的。共和党人本想给吉文斯和斯诺布克拉夫特（Snobbcraft）挖点丑闻，却被本党的战略委员会劝住了——委员会生怕开这样一个危险的先例；何况他们自己队伍里，也不乏犯着通奸、酗酒、贪赃案子的政客。

共和党的古西（Goosie）与甘普（Gump）、民主党的吉文斯与斯诺布克拉夫特，都结束了全国巡回，歇下劳碌的腿脚。大大小小的城市乡镇处处游行。二三流演说家从大西洋岸一路拍着讲台拍到太平洋岸，把雇主政党候选人们想象出来的种种美德吹得天花乱坠。克鲁克曼医生的模拟像叫人烧了一百回。好几家产科医院挨了砸。两百名对两位候选人一无所知的公民，为了争辩谁更够格当总统而大打出手，被逮进了局子。

空气里电一般绷着期待。人们三五成群地扎堆。报童小厮把传单撒上一千万户人家的门阶。警察严阵以待，弹压骚乱——他们自己惹出来的那份除外。

---

===Original===
Arthur Snobbcraft, jovial and confident that he would soon assume a position befitting a member of one of the First Families of Virginia, was holding a brilliant pre-election party in his palatial residence. Strolling in and out amongst his guests, the master of the house accepted their premature congratulations in good humor. It was fine to hear oneself already addressed as Mr. Vice-President.

The tall English butler hastily edged his way through the throng surrounding the President of the Anglo-Saxon Association and whispered, “Dr. Buggerie is in the study upstairs. He says he must see you at once; that it is very, very important.”

Puzzled, Snobbcraft went up to find out what in the world could be the trouble. As he entered, the massive statistician was striding back and forth, mopping his brow, his eyes starting from his head, a sheaf of typewritten sheets trembling in his hand.

“What’s wrong, Buggerie?” asked Snobbcraft, perturbed.

“Everything! Everything!” shrilled the statistician.

===Chinese===
阿瑟·斯诺布克拉夫特满面春风，深信自己不日就要荣任一个配得上弗吉尼亚第一家族（First Families of Virginia，简称 F.F.V.）成员身份的职位，正在他那宫殿般的宅邸里大办选前宴会。主人在宾客中间踱进踱出，笑吟吟地领受他们抢早的道贺。听人开口闭口喊自己“副总统先生”，实在受用。

那个高挑个儿的英国管家急急从围着盎格鲁-撒克逊协会会长的人群里挤出来，低声说：“布热里博士（Dr. Buggerie）在楼上书房。他说必须马上见您，说事情非常、非常要紧。”

斯诺布克拉夫特满腹狐疑地上了楼，要看看到底能出什么岔子。他进门时，那位大块头统计学家正来回踱步，不住擦汗，眼珠子快瞪出眼眶，一沓打字纸在手里抖个不停。

“出什么事了，布热里？”斯诺布克拉夫特不安地问。

“全完了！全完了！”统计学家尖声嚷道。

===Original===
“Be specific, please.”

“Well,” shaking the sheaf of papers in Snobbcraft’s face, “we can’t release any of this stuff! It’s too damaging! It’s too inclusive! We’ll have to suppress it, Snobbcraft. You hear me? We musn’t let anyone get hold of it.” The big man’s flabby jowls worked excitedly.

“What do you mean?” snarled the F.F.V. “Do you mean to tell me that all of that money and work is wasted?”

“That’s exactly what I mean,” squeaked Buggerie. “It would be suicidal to publish it.”

“Why? Get down to brass tacks, man, for God’s sake. You get my goat.”

===Chinese===
“请你讲具体些。”

“嗐，”他把那沓纸直摇到斯诺布克拉夫特鼻子底下，“这一堆东西一份也发不得！杀伤力太大！牵连太广！咱们非把它压下来不可，斯诺布克拉夫特。听见没有？决不能让任何人拿到手。”大胖子松软的双下巴激动得直哆嗦。

“你这是什么意思？”那位弗吉尼亚贵胄呲着牙低吼。“你莫不是说，那么多钱、那么多心血全打了水漂？”

“我就是这个意思，”布热里尖着嗓子说。“发表出去等于集体自杀。”

“为什么？老天爷，有话直说，老兄，你急死我了。”

===Original===
“Now listen here, Snobbcraft,” replied the statistician soberly, dropping heavily into a chair. “Sit down and listen to me. I started this investigation on the theory that the data gathered would prove that around twenty million people, mostly of the lower classes were of Negro ancestry, recent and remote, while about half that number would be of uncertain or unknown ancestry.”

“Well, what have you found?” insisted Snobbcraft, impatiently.

“I have found,” continued Buggerie, “that over half the population has no record of its ancestry beyond five generations!”

“That’s fine!” chortled Snobbcraft. “I’ve always maintained that there were only a few people of good blood in this country.”

“But those figures include all classes,” protested the larger man. “Your class as well as the lower classes.”

===Chinese===
“那您听着，斯诺布克拉夫特，”统计学家正色答道，一屁股重重坐进椅子。“您坐下听我说。我这番调查，本是基于这么一个设想：搜集来的数据会证明，全国约有两千万人——多属下层阶级——具有或近或远的黑人血统，另有约摸一千万人血统不明或无从查考。”

“那你查出了什么？”斯诺布克拉夫特不耐烦地追问。

“我查出的是，”布热里接着说，“过半的人口，家世往上数到五代就无案可稽了！”

“好得很嘛！”斯诺布克拉夫特咯咯直乐。“我一向坚持：这个国家血统高贵的人没几个。”

“可这个数字把各阶级全包了进去，”块头更大的那位抗议道。“您那个阶级也在内，跟下层阶级一样。”

===Original===
“Don’t insult me, Buggerie!” shouted the head of the Anglo-Saxons, half rising from his seat on the sofa.

“Be calm! Be calm!” cried Buggerie excitedly, “You haven’t heard anything yet.”

“What else, in the name of God, could be a worse libel on the aristocracy of this state?” Snobbcraft mopped his dark and haughty countenance.

“Well, these statistics we’ve gathered prove that most of our social leaders, especially of Anglo-Saxon lineage, are descendants of colonial stock that came here in bondage. They associated with slaves, in many cases worked and slept with them. They intermixed with the blacks and the women were sexually exploited by their masters. Then, even more than today, the illegitimate birth rate was very high in America.”

Snobbcraft’s face was working with suppressed rage. He started to rise but reconsidered. “Go on,” he commanded.

“There was so much of this mixing between whites and blacks of the various classes that very early the colonies took steps to put a halt to it. They managed to prevent intermarriage but they couldn’t stop intermixture. You know the old records don’t lie. They’re right there for everybody to see.　﻿…”

===Chinese===
“布热里，你敢羞辱我！”盎格鲁-撒克逊人的头领吼道，从沙发上半个身子都支了起来。

“冷静！冷静！”布热里激动地喊。“您还没听完呢。”

“老天在上，还能有什么比这更糟践本州贵族门面的？”斯诺布克拉夫特擦着他那副黢黑而倨傲的脸。

“呃，咱们搜集的这些统计资料证明，我们的社会名流——尤其是盎格鲁-撒克逊一脉——大都是当年戴着镣铐贩到这儿来的殖民时代那批人的后裔。他们同奴隶厮混，许多时候一道干活、一道睡觉。他们同黑人通婚杂混，他们的女人则遭主子们恣意玩弄。再说，那时候美国的私生率比如今还要高得多。”

斯诺布克拉夫特的脸憋着一腔怒火直抽抽。他起身要发作，转念又坐了回去。“讲下去，”他下令道。

“各色人等的白人跟黑人之间，这种混血闹得实在不像话，殖民地当局很早就着手煞车了。他们总算禁住了通婚，却拦不住杂交。您知道，老档案可不撒谎，全都摆在那儿，谁都能去查。……”

===Original===
“A certain percentage of these Negroes,” continued Buggerie, quite at ease now and seemingly enjoying his dissertation, “in time lightened sufficiently to be able to pass for white. They then merged with the general population. Assuming that there were one thousand such cases fifteen generations ago—and we have proof that there were more—their descendants now number close to fifty million souls. Now I maintain that we dare not risk publishing this information. Too many of our very first families are touched right here in Richmond!”

“Buggerie!” gasped the F.F.V., “Are you mad?”

“Quite sane, sir,” squeaked the ponderous man, somewhat proudly, “and I know what I know.” He winked a watery eye.

“Well, go on. Is there any more?”

“Plenty,” proceeded the statistician, amiably. “Take your own family, for instance. (Now don’t get mad, Snobbcraft). Take your own family. It is true that your people descended from King Alfred, but he has scores, perhaps hundreds of thousands of descendants. Some are, of course, honored and respected citizens, cultured aristocrats who are a credit to the country; but most of them, my dear, dear Snobbcraft, are in what you call the lower orders: that is to say, laboring people, convicts, prostitutes, and that sort. One of your maternal ancestors in the late seventeenth century was the offspring of an English serving maid and a black slave. This woman in turn had a daughter by the plantation owner. This daughter was married to a former indentured slave. Their children were all white and you are one of their direct descendants!” Buggerie beamed.

===Chinese===
“这批黑人当中，”布热里接着说，这会儿神态自若，俨然在享受自己的学术讲演，“有一定比例的人，肤色渐渐浅到可以冒充白人。于是他们便融进了普通人堆里。姑且假定十五代以前有过一千个这样的例子——我们有证据表明远不止此——他们的子孙如今已逼近五千万人。所以依我看，这份情报咱们万万不敢冒险公布。单在里士满，咱们多少个头等名门望族都叫它戳着了！”

“布热里！”那位弗吉尼亚贵胄倒抽一口凉气。“你疯了吗？”

“清醒得很，先生，”那位笨重的胖子略带几分得意地尖声道。“我心里有数。”他眨了眨一只水汪汪的眼睛。

“那讲下去。还有吗？”

“多着呢，”统计学家和颜悦色地继续说。“就拿您自己家打比方吧。（可别恼火，斯诺布克拉夫特。）就拿您自己家打比方。令族出自阿尔弗雷德大王（King Alfred），这不假；可他开枝散叶，子孙成千累万，兴许几十万。其中固然有受人尊崇的体面公民、为国家增光的斯文贵族；可他们里头的大多数，我亲爱的、亲爱的斯诺布克拉夫特，都在您所谓的下等阶层里：也就是说，做工的、蹲大牢的、操皮肉生意的，诸如此类。十七世纪末，您母系的一位先祖，就是个英国婢女跟一个黑奴生下的孩子。这个女人后来又跟种植园主生了个女儿。这个女儿嫁给了一个卖身契满期的前契约奴。他们的孩子全是白人，而您正是他们的嫡系后裔之一！”布热里满面春风。

===Original===
“Stop!” shouted Snobbcraft, the veins standing out on his narrow forehead and his voice trembling with rage. “You can’t sit there and insult my family that way, suh.”

“Now that outburst just goes to prove my earlier assertion,” the large man continued, blandly. “If you get so excited about the truth, what do you think will be the reaction of other people? There’s no use getting angry at me. I’m not responsible for your ancestry! Nor, for that matter, are you. You’re no worse off than I am, Snobbcraft. My great, great grandfather had his ears cropped for nonpayment of debts and was later jailed for thievery. His illegitimate daughter married a free Negro who fought in the Revolutionary War.” Buggerie wagged his head almost gleefully.

“How can you admit it?” asked the scandalized Snobbcraft.

“Why not?” demanded Buggerie. “I have plenty of company. There’s Givens, who is quite a fanatic on the race problem and white supremacy, and yet he’s only four generations removed from a mulatto ancestor.”

“Givens too?”

===Chinese===
“住口！”斯诺布克拉夫特吼道，狭窄的额头上青筋暴起，声音气得直打颤。“你不能坐在那儿这样糟践我的家门，先生。”

“您瞧，这一嗓子恰恰印证了我方才的话，”大块头不紧不慢地续道。“您听见真话都这么激动，换旁人又会怎样？冲我发火没用。您的祖宗又不是我招来的！再说了，也不是您招来的。您不比我惨，斯诺布克拉夫特。我的曾曾祖父因为还不起债给割了耳朵，后来又因偷窃坐了牢。他的私生女嫁了个参加独立战争的黑人自由民。”布热里摇头晃脑，几乎乐不可支。

“这种事你怎么好意思认？”斯诺布克拉夫特大骇，问道。

“有什么不好意思的？”布热里反问。“同病相怜的多了。就说吉文斯吧，种族问题和白人至上他狂热得不行，可他从一位混血先祖数下来才隔了四代。”

“吉文斯也是？”

===Original===
“Yes, and also the proud Senator Kretin. He boasts, you know, of being descended from Pocahontas and Captain John Smith, but so are thousands of Negroes. Incidentally, there hasn’t been an Indian unmixed with Negro on the Atlantic coastal plain for over a century and a half.”

“What about Matthew Fisher?”

“We can find no record whatever of Fisher, which is true of about twenty million others, and so,” he lowered his voice dramatically, “I have reason to suspect that he is one of those Negroes who have been whitened.”

“And to think that I entertained him in my home!” Snobbcraft muttered to himself. And then aloud: “Well, what are we to do about it?”

“We must destroy the whole shooting match,” the big man announced as emphatically as possible for one with a soprano voice, “and we’d better do it at once. The sooner we get through with it the better.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，还有那位自尊得不得了的克雷廷参议员（Senator Kretin）。您知道，他逢人便夸自己是波卡洪塔斯（Pocahontas）和约翰·史密斯船长（Captain John Smith）的后裔；可有几千黑人也同样是。附带说一句，一个半世纪以来，大西洋沿岸平原上就没出过一个没掺黑人血统的印第安人。”

“那马修·费舍尔（Matthew Fisher）呢？”

“费舍尔这个人，咱们连一星半点的档案都翻不着——另外约有两千万人也是这等光景。所以，”他戏剧性地压低嗓音，“我有理由怀疑，他就是那种变白了的黑人。”

“我竟然把这种人请进家里当贵客款待！”斯诺布克拉夫特喃喃自语。随即又扬声问道：“那，这事儿咱们怎么办？”

“咱们得把整摊东西统统毁掉，”大块头宣布道——就凭一副女高音嗓子，这话说得算尽力洪亮了——“而且顶好立刻就办。了结得越早越好。”

===Original===
“But I can’t leave my guests,” protested Snobbcraft. Then turning angrily upon his friend, he growled, “Why in the devil didn’t you find all of this out before?”

“Well,” said Buggerie, meekly, “I found out as soon as I could. We had to arrange and correlate the data, you know.”

“How do you imagine we’re going to get rid of that mountain of paper at this hour?” asked Snobbcraft, as they started downstairs.

“We’ll get the guards to help us,” said Buggerie, hopefully. “And we’ll have the cards burned in the furnace.”

“All right, then,” snapped the F.F.V., “let’s go and get it over with.”

===Chinese===
“可我撇不下满堂宾客呀，”斯诺布克拉夫特抗辩道。跟着他又怒冲冲转向朋友，咕哝着低吼：“你早干什么去了，偏挨到这会儿才查明白？”

“这个嘛，”布热里怯生生地说，“我一能查明就查明了。要知道，那些数据总得归置归置、相互印证啊。”

“你倒说说，这更半夜的，那一山纸张咱们怎么个销法？”两人动身下楼时，斯诺布克拉夫特问。

“叫警卫们来搭把手，”布热里满怀指望地说。“把卡片全塞锅炉里烧掉。”

“那成，”这位弗吉尼亚贵胄干脆地说。“走，赶紧把它办完。”

===Original===
In five minutes they were speeding down the broad avenue to the headquarters of the Anglo-Saxon Association of America. They parked the car in front of the gate and walked up the cinder road to the front door. It was a balmy, moonlight night, almost as bright as day. They looked around but saw no one.

“I don’t see any of the guards around,” Snobbcraft remarked, craning his neck. “I wonder where they are?”

“Probably they’re inside,” Buggerie suggested, “although I remember telling them to patrol the outside of the building.”

“Well, we’ll go in, anyhow,” remarked Snobbcraft. “Maybe they’re downstairs.”

He unlocked the door, swung it open and they entered. The hall was pitch dark. Both men felt along the wall for the button for the light. Suddenly there was a thud and Snobbcraft cursed.

===Chinese===
五分钟后，他们的车子已沿宽阔的大道疾驰，直奔美国盎格鲁-撒克逊协会总部。车停在大门前，两人踏着煤渣路走向正门。夜色和暖，月华如昼。两人四下张望，不见一个人影。

“周围连个警卫的影子都没有，”斯诺布克拉夫特伸长脖子说。“人呢，都跑哪儿去了？”

“兴许在屋里，”布热里猜测。“不过我记得，我是吩咐他们绕着房子外头巡逻的。”

“管他呢，咱们进去就是，”斯诺布克拉夫特说。“兴许在楼下。”

他开开门锁，推门而入。门厅里漆黑一团。两人贴着墙根摸灯的按钮。突然砰的一声响，斯诺布克拉夫特骂出了声。

===Original===
“What’s the matter?” wailed the frightened Buggerie, frantically feeling for a match.

“Turn on that God damned light!” roared Snobbcraft. “I just stumbled over a man.　﻿… Hurry up, will you?”

Dr. Buggerie finally found a match, struck it, located the wall button and pressed it. The hall was flooded with light. There arranged in a row on the floor and neatly trussed up and gagged were the six special guards.

“What the hell does this mean?” yelled Snobbcraft at the mute men prone before them. Buggerie quickly removed the gags.

They had been suddenly set upon, the head watchman explained, about an hour before, just after Dr. Buggerie left, by a crowd of gunmen who had blackjacked them into unconsciousness and carried them into the building. The watchman displayed the lumps on their heads as evidence and looked quite aggrieved. Not one of them could remember what transpired after the sleep-producing buffet.

===Chinese===
“怎么回事呀？”吓坏了的布热里带着哭腔，没命地摸火柴。

“快把那盏该死的灯点上！”斯诺布克拉夫特咆哮道。“我刚绊在一个……人身上。……快点儿行不行？”

布热里博士总算摸着了一根火柴，擦亮，寻着墙上的按钮按了下去。门厅顿时大放光明。只见六名特别警卫在地上排成一溜，捆得结结实实，嘴里还堵着东西。

“这他妈搞的什么名堂？”斯诺布克拉夫特冲着眼前那几个一声不吭、趴在地上的家伙大吼。布热里赶紧去掏堵嘴的布团。

领头的看守解释说，大约一个钟头以前，就在布热里博士前脚离开之后，一伙枪手突然扑上来，用短棍把他们砸得不省人事，抬进了楼里。看守们指着头上的疙瘩作证，一脸的委屈。那顿催人入睡的闷棍之后出了什么事，没有一个人记得起来。

===Original===
“The vault!” shrilled Buggerie. “Let’s have a look at the vault.”

Down the stairs they rushed, Buggerie wheezing in the lead, Snobbcraft following and the six tousled watchmen bringing up the rear. The lights in the basement were still burning brightly. The doors of the vault were open, sagging on their hinges. There was a litter of trash in front of the vault. They all clustered around the opening and peered inside. The vault was absolutely empty.

“My God!” exclaimed Snobbcraft and Buggerie in unison, turning two shades paler.

For a second or two they just gazed at each other. Then suddenly Buggerie smiled.

“That stuff won’t do them any good,” he remarked triumphantly.

===Chinese===
“保险库！”布热里尖叫。“快去看看保险库！”

几个人一齐往楼下冲，布热里呼哧呼哧打头，斯诺布克拉夫特紧随其后，六个头发蓬乱的看守殿后。地下室的灯还亮堂堂地开着。保险库的门大敞着，从合页上耷拉下来。库门前散着一地纸屑杂物。众人围着库口挤作一团，往里探头张望。保险库里空空如也。

“我的天！”斯诺布克拉夫特和布热里不约而同地惊呼，脸色又褪了两分。

有一两秒钟，两人只是面面相觑。跟着，布热里忽然笑了。

“那堆东西到他们手里也顶不了用，”他得意洋洋地说。

===Original===
“Why not?” demanded Snobbcraft, in his tone a mixture of eagerness, hope and doubt.

“Well, it will take them as long to get anything out of that mass of cards as it took our staff, and by that time you and Givens will be elected and no one will dare publish anything like that,” the statistician explained. “I have in my possession the only summary—those papers I showed you at your house. As long as I’ve got that document and they haven’t, we’re all right!” he grinned in obese joy.

“That sounds good,” sighed Snobbcraft, contentedly. “By the way, where is that summary?”

Buggerie jumped as if stuck by a pin and looked first into his empty hands, then into his coat pockets and finally his trousers pockets. He turned and dashed out to the car, followed by the grim-looking Snobbcraft and the six uniformed watchmen with their tousled hair and sore bumps. They searched the car in vain, Snobbcraft loudly cursing Buggerie’s stupidity.

“I—I must have left it in your study,” wept Buggerie, meekly and hopefully. “In fact I think I remember leaving it right there on the table.”

===Chinese===
“为什么顶不了用？”斯诺布克拉夫特追问，语气里急切、指望、疑虑搅作一团。

“嗐，那一大堆卡片，他们要从里头理出个头绪，得花咱们全体职员那么长的工夫；到那时候，您和吉文斯早当选了，谁还敢刊登那种东西，”统计学家解释道。“唯一的摘要在我手里——就是我在府上给您看过的那几页。只要那份文件在我这儿、不在他们那儿，咱们就万无一失！”他咧开大嘴，胖得心花怒放。

“这听着还像句话，”斯诺布克拉夫特宽心地舒了口气。“对了，那份摘要呢？”

布热里像叫针扎了一样蹦起来，先看看自己空空的双手，再摸摸大衣口袋，最后是裤子口袋。他转身冲出去直奔汽车，面色铁青的斯诺布克拉夫特和六个穿制服、顶着乱发肿包的看守紧随其后。车里翻了个底朝天，一无所获，斯诺布克拉夫特高声咒骂布热里的愚蠢。

“我——我准是落在您书房了，”布热里带着哭腔，怯生生又满怀指望地说。“说真的，我记得清清楚楚，就搁在桌上。”

===Original===
The enraged Snobbcraft ordered him into the car and they drove off leaving the six uniformed watchmen gaping at the entrance to the grounds, the moonbeams playing through their tousled hair.

The two men hit the ground almost as soon as the car crunched to a stop, dashed up the steps, into the house, through the crowd of bewildered guests, up the winding colonial stairs, down the hallway and into the study.

Buggerie switched on the light and looked wildly, hopefully around. Simultaneously the two men made a grab for a sheaf of white paper lying on the sofa. The statistician reached it first and gazed hungrily, gratefully at it. Then his eyes started from his head and his hand trembled.

“Look!” he shrieked dolefully, thrusting the sheaf of paper under Snobbcraft’s eyes.

===Chinese===
怒不可遏的斯诺布克拉夫特喝令他上车，一行人一溜烟开了，只留下六个穿制服的看守张着嘴愣在庄园门口，月光在他们乱蓬蓬的头发间闪烁。

车轮嘎吱一声刹住，两个人几乎同时跳下车，冲上台阶，进了宅子，穿过一群莫明其妙的宾客，奔上殖民地式的盘旋楼梯，穿过走廊，一头扎进书房。

布热里啪地打开灯，发狂般满怀指望地四下搜寻。两人同时伸手去抓沙发上那沓白纸。统计学家抢先够着了，如饥似渴、感激涕零地盯住它。跟着，他的眼珠子瞪出了眼眶，手也抖了。

“您瞧！”他悲声尖叫，把那沓纸直塞到斯诺布克拉夫特眼皮底下。

===Original===
All of the sheets were blank except the one on top. On that was scribbled:

> Thanks very much for leaving that report where I could get hold of it. Am leaving this paper so you’ll have something on which to write another summary.
> Happy dreams, Little One.
> G.O.P.

“Great God!” gasped Snobbcraft, sinking into a chair.

===Chinese===
除了最上头一页，其余全是白纸。最上头那页潦草地写着：

> 多谢您把报告搁在我顺手能拿到的地方。这张纸留给您，好歹让您还有张纸，可以另写一份摘要。
> 祝好梦，小乖乖。
> G.O.P.（共和党）

“我的上帝！”斯诺布克拉夫特倒抽一口冷气，瘫进了椅子。

## XII / 十二

===Original===
The afternoon before election Matthew and Bunny sat in the latter’s hotel suite sipping cocktails, smoking and awaiting the inevitable. They had been waiting ever since the day before. Matthew, tall and tense; Bunny, rotund and apprehensive, trying ever so often to cheer up his chief with poor attempts at jocosity. Every time they heard a bell ring both jumped for the telephone, thinking it might be an announcement from Helen’s bedside that an heir, and a dark one, had been born. When they could no longer stay around the office, they had come down to the hotel. In just a few moments they were planning to go back to the office again.

The hard campaign and the worry over the outcome of Helen’s confinement had left traces on Matthew’s face. The satanic lines were accentuated, the eyes seemed sunken farther back in the head, his well-manicured hand trembled a little as he reached for his glass again and again.

He wondered how it would all come out. He hated to leave. He had had such a good time since he’d been white: plenty of money, almost unlimited power, a beautiful wife, good liquor and the pick of damsels within reach. Must he leave all that? Must he cut and run just at the time when he was about to score his greatest victory? Just think: from an underpaid insurance agent to a millionaire commanding millions of people—and then oblivion. He shuddered slightly and reached again for his glass.

===Chinese===
选举前一天下午，马修（Matthew）和邦尼（Bunny）坐在后者的旅馆套房里，一边啜着鸡尾酒、吞云吐雾，一边等待那注定要来的结果。打前一天起，他们就一直这么等着。马修高挑的身形绷得紧紧的；邦尼圆滚滚的，一脸愁相，时不时笨嘴拙舌地插科打诨，想给老板解解闷。每回听见铃响，两个人都一齐扑向电话，只当是海伦（Helen）床边传来的喜讯——生了位继承人，还是个黑皮肤的。办公室里实在待不下去了，他们才挪到旅馆来。这会儿又盘算着，过几分钟还得回办公室去。

艰苦的竞选，加上对海伦分娩吉凶的忧心，在马修脸上留下了痕迹。那几道恶魔似的纹路愈发深了，眼睛好像也往眼窝深处又陷进去一截；他那只修甲修得漂漂亮亮的手，一遍又一遍伸向酒杯时，微微发颤。

他不知道这一切最后会怎么收场。他实在舍不得走。变白之后这日子过得多美：大把的票子，几乎没边儿的权力，一位如花似玉的太太，上等的好酒，还有唾手可得、任他挑拣的姑娘。难道非得撒手不成？偏偏在他即将赢得平生最大胜利的节骨眼上，溜之大吉？想想看：从一个薪水微薄的保险推销员，到号令千百万人的百万富翁——转眼便归于湮没。他微微打了个哆嗦，又把手伸向酒杯。

===Original===
“I got everything fixed,” Bunny remarked, shifting around in the overstuffed chair. “The plane’s all ready with tanks full and I’ve got Ruggles right there in the hangar. The money’s in that little steel box: all in thousand dollar bills.”

“You’re going with me, aren’t you, Bunny?” asked Matthew in almost pleading tones.

“I’m not stayin’ here!” his secretary replied.

“Gee, Bunny, you’re a brick!” said Matthew leaning over and placing his hand on his plump little friend’s knee. “You sure have been a good pal.”

“Aw, cut th’ comedy,” exclaimed Bunny, reddening and turning his head swiftly away.

===Chinese===
“全都安排妥了。”邦尼说，在软垫厚实的扶手椅里挪了挪身子。“飞机全准备好了，油箱加得满满的，拉格尔斯（Ruggles）就在机库里守着。钱都在那只小铁皮箱里：全是千元大钞。”

“你会跟我一块儿走的，对吧，邦尼？”马修问，语气近乎哀求。

“我才不待在这儿呢！”他的秘书答道。

“天哪，邦尼，你可真够朋友！”马修俯过身去，把手搭在胖乎乎的小个子朋友的膝盖上。“你一向是个难得的好伙计。”

“哎呀，快别演苦情戏啦。”邦尼嚷道，脸一红，赶紧把头扭向一边。

===Original===
Suddenly the telephone rang, loud, clear, staccato. Both men sprang for it, eagerly, open-eyed, apprehensive. Matthew was first.

“Hello!” he shouted. “What’s that! Yes, I’ll be right up.”

“Well, it’s happened,” he announced resignedly, hanging up the receiver. And then, brightening a bit, he boasted, “It’s a boy!”

In the midst of her pain Helen was jubilant. What a present to give her Matthew on the eve of his greatest triumph! How good the Lord was to her; to doubly bless her in this way. The nurse wiped the tears of joy away from the young mother’s eyes.

“You must stay quiet, Ma’am,” she warned.

===Chinese===
突然，电话铃响了，响亮、清脆、断断续续。两个人一齐扑上去，急切、瞪圆了眼、心里发虚。马修抢在了前头。

“喂！”他吼道。“什么？……好，我马上上来。”

“嗯，事情来了。”他放下听筒，听天由命地宣布。随即又精神一振，夸口道：“是个男孩！”

疼痛交加之中，海伦却乐得心花怒放。在他马修平生最大胜利的前夜，送上这样一份礼物，多棒！上帝待她多好，竟这样加倍地降福于她。护士替年轻妈妈拭去眼里喜悦的泪水。

“您可得安安静静的，太太。”她提醒道。

===Original===
Outside in the hall, squirming uneasily on the window seat, was Matthew, his fists clenched, his teeth biting into his thin lower lip. At another window stood Bunny looking vacantly out into the street, feeling useless and out of place in such a situation, and yet convinced that it was his duty to stay here by his best friend during this great crisis.

Matthew felt like a young soldier about to leave his trench to face a baptism of machine gun fire or a gambler risking his last dollar on a roll of the dice. It seemed to him that he would go mad if something didn’t happen quickly. He rose and paced the hall, hands in pockets, his tall shadow following him on the opposite wall. Why didn’t the doctor come out and tell him something? What was the cause of the delay? What would Helen say? What would the baby look like? Maybe it might be miraculously light! Stranger things had happened in this world. But no, nothing like that could happen. Well, he’d had his lucky break; now the vacation was over.

A nurse, immaculate in white uniform, came out of Helen’s bedroom, passed them hurriedly, smiling, and entered the bathroom. She returned with a basin of warm water in her hands, smiled again reassuringly and reentered the natal chamber. Bunny and Matthew, in unison, sighed heavily.

===Chinese===
外面走廊里，马修在窗座上局促不安地扭来扭去，双拳紧攥，牙齿咬着薄薄的下嘴唇。邦尼站在另一扇窗前，两眼发直地望着街面，只觉得自己在这样的场面里百无一用、格格不入，却又认定：在这大关大坎上，守在挚友身边是自己的本分。

马修觉得自己像个正要跃出战壕、去领受机枪火力洗礼的年轻士兵，又像个把最后一块钱押在一把骰子上的赌徒。他觉得，要是再不快点出个结果，自己非疯了不可。他起身在走廊里踱来踱去，两手插兜，高高的身影在对面墙上紧跟着他。大夫怎么还不出来给个话？为什么拖这么久？海伦会怎么说？孩子会是什么模样？没准儿奇迹出现，肤色浅得很呢！这世上比这更稀奇的事也不是没有过。可不行，那样的事不会有的。唉，好运他是撞上过一回的；如今假期到头了。

一位护士一身白制服干净利落，从海伦的卧房里出来，微笑着匆匆从他们身边走过，进了浴室。她双手端着一盆温水回来，又安抚似的笑了笑，重新走进产房。邦尼和马修不约而同，重重地叹了口气。

===Original===
“Boy!” exclaimed Bunny, wiping the perspiration from his brow. “If somethin’ don’t happen pretty soon, here, I’m gonna do a Brodie out o’ that window.”

“The both of us,” said Matthew. “I never knew it took these doctors so damn long to get through.”

Helen’s door opened and the physician came out looking quite grave and concerned. Matthew pounced upon him. The man held his finger to his lips and motioned to the room across the hall. Matthew entered.

“Well,” said Matthew, guiltily, “what’s the news?”

“I’m very sorry to have to tell you, Mr. Fisher, that something terrible has happened. Your son is very, very dark. Either you or Mrs. Fisher must possess some Negro blood. It might be called reversion to type if any such thing had ever been proved. Now I want to know what you want done. If you say so I can get rid of this child and it will save everybody concerned a lot of trouble and disgrace. Nobody except the nurse knows anything about this and she’ll keep her mouth shut for a consideration. Of course, it’s all in the day’s work for me, you know. I’ve had plenty of cases like this in Atlanta, even before the disappearance of the Negroes. Come now, what shall I do?” he wailed.

===Chinese===
“好家伙！”邦尼嚷道，抹去额头上的汗。“这地方要再不出点动静，我可要从那扇窗户学布罗迪来个跳楼了（do a Brodie）。”

“要跳咱俩一块儿跳。”马修说。“我从前竟不知道这些大夫办个事要磨蹭这么久，真见鬼。”

海伦的房门开了，大夫走出来，一脸凝重忧色。马修一个箭步扑上去。那人把手指竖在唇边，朝走廊对面的房间示意。马修走了进去。

“怎么样？”马修问，心里发虚。

“费舍尔（Fisher）先生，很遗憾我不得不告诉您，出了一桩糟糕透顶的事。您的儿子黑得非常、非常厉害。不是您就是费舍尔太太，身上必定带有黑人血统。倘若这种事真被证明过，倒可以管它叫返祖现象。现在我要知道的是，您打算怎么办。只要您一句话，我就能把这孩子处理掉，替有关各方省去许许多多麻烦和耻辱。除了护士，没有旁人知道这件事，只要给点好处，她保管守口如瓶。当然了，您知道，这种事对我来说不过是家常便饭。在亚特兰大，这样的案子我经手过不知多少，还是在黑人集体消失之前呢。行了，您倒说说我该怎么办吧？”他哀声催促。

===Original===
“Yes,” thought Matthew to himself, “what should he do?” The doctor had suggested an excellent way out of the dilemma. They could just say that the child had died. But what of the future? Must he go on forever in this way? Helen was young and fecund. Surely one couldn’t go on murdering one’s children, especially when one loved and wanted children. Wouldn’t it be better to settle the matter once and for all? Or should he let the doctor murder the boy and then hope for a better situation the next time? An angel of frankness beckoned him to be done with this life of pretense; to take his wife and son and flee far away from everything, but a devil of ambition whispered seductively about wealth, power and prestige.

In almost as many seconds the pageant of the past three years passed in review on the screen of his tortured memory: the New Year’s Eve at the Honky Tonk Club, the first glimpse of the marvelously beautiful Helen, the ordeal of getting white, the first, sweet days of freedom from the petty insults and cheap discriminations to which as a black man he had always been subjected, then the search for Helen around Atlanta, the organization of the Knights of Nordica, the stream of successes, the coming of Bunny, the campaign planned and executed by him: and now, the end. Must it be the end?

“Well?” came the insistent voice of the physician.

Matthew opened his mouth to reply when the butler burst into the room waving a newspaper.

“Excuse me, sir,” he blurted, excitedly, “but Mister Brown said to bring this right to you.”

===Chinese===
“是啊，”马修心里暗想，“他该怎么办呢？”大夫倒是指点了一条脱身的妙计。对外只说孩子落地就死了便是。可往后呢？难道要永远这样过下去？海伦年轻，又能生养。总不能一个接一个地亲手弄死自己的孩子吧，何况自己疼孩子，还盼着要孩子。何不把这件事一劳永逸地了断掉？还是先让大夫把这男婴弄死，指望下一胎情形好些？一位坦诚的天使朝他招手，劝他结束这种弄虚作假的日子，带上妻子儿子远走高飞，逃离这一切；可一个野心勃勃的魔鬼却在耳畔娇声细语，念叨着财富、权力和名望。

前后不过几秒钟工夫，过去三年的一幕幕盛景，便在他受尽煎熬的记忆银幕上次第闪过：欢乐调俱乐部那个新年除夕，初次瞥见美艳绝伦的海伦，变白手术的那场磨难，头几天摆脱琐碎羞辱和廉价歧视后的甜蜜日子——想当年身为黑人，这些气他哪天不受个遍——随后是亚特兰大四处寻访海伦，北欧骑士团的草创，接二连三的成功，邦尼的到来，由他一手策划、一手执行的竞选：如今，终局到了。非得是终局不可吗？

“怎么样？”大夫的声音追着问。

马修张嘴正要回答，男管家挥着一张报纸闯了进来。

“对不起，先生，”他激动地脱口而出，“布朗先生说这报纸得马上送给您。”

===Original===
The lurid headlines seemed to leap from the paper and strike Matthew between the eyes:

> Givens, Snobbcraft, Buggerie, Kretin and Others of Negro Ancestry, According to Old Records Unearthed by Them.

Matthew and the physician, standing side by side, read the long account in awed silence. Bunny entered the door.

“Can I speak to you a minute, Matt?” he asked casually. Almost reluctant to move, Matthew followed him into the hall.

“Keep your shirt on, Big Boy,” Bunny advised, almost jovially. “They ain’t got nothin’ on you yet. That changing your name threw them off. You’re not even mentioned.”

Matthew braced up, threw back his shoulders and drew a long, deep breath. It seemed as if a mountain had been taken off his shoulders. He actually grinned as his confidence returned. He reached for Bunny’s hand and they shook, silently jubilant.

===Chinese===
那触目惊心的大标题，仿佛从报纸上直蹦出来，劈面打在马修两眼之间：

> 吉文斯、斯诺布克拉夫特、布热里、克雷廷等人皆有黑人血统——铁证正是他们自己掘出的旧档案

马修和大夫并肩站着，屏息噤声地读完那篇长篇报道。邦尼进了门。

“能跟你借一步说话吗，马特（Matt）？”他若无其事地问。马修几乎有些不情愿挪窝，还是跟他到了走廊上。

“沉住气，大个子，”邦尼几乎是乐呵呵地说，“他们还没抓着你什么把柄呢。你一改名，就把他们甩脱了。报上连提都没提你。”

马修顿时来了精神，肩膀一挺，长长地、深深地吸了一口气。就像卸下了压在肩头的千斤大山。信心回来了，他竟咧嘴笑了。他伸手拉过邦尼的手，两人握了握，无声地欢庆。

===Original===
“Well, doctor,” said Matthew, arching his left eyebrow in his familiar Mephistophelian manner, “it sort of looks as if there is something to that reversion to type business. I used to think it was all boloney myself. Well, it’s as I always say: you never can tell.”

“Yes, it seems as if this is a very authentic case,” agreed the physician, glancing sharply at the bland and blond countenance of Matthew. “Well, what now?”

“I’ll have to see Givens,” said Matthew as they turned to leave the room.

“Here he comes now,” Bunny announced.

Sure enough, the little gray-faced, bald-headed man, came leaping up the stairs like a goat, his face haggard, his eyes bulging in mingled rage and terror, his necktie askew. He was waving a newspaper in his hand and opened his mouth without speaking as he shot past them and dashed into Helen’s room. The old fellow was evidently out of his head.

===Chinese===
“唉，大夫，”马修说，左眉往上一挑，还是他那副熟悉的靡菲斯特式（Mephistophelian）神情，“这么看来，那套返祖的把戏还真有几分道理。我从前还一直当它全是胡扯呢。唉，就像我一贯说的：这事儿谁也说不准。”

“是啊，这一例瞧着还挺真章。”大夫表示赞同，目光锐利地瞥了一眼马修那张白净平和的金发面孔。“那么，下一步怎么办？”

“我得去见见吉文斯（Givens）。”马修说，两人转身准备出屋。

“这就来了。”邦尼通报。

果不其然，那个灰白脸、秃脑门的小个子男人，像山羊似的连蹦带跳蹿上楼梯，面如死灰，两眼暴突，又惊又怒，领带也歪到了一边。他手里挥着一张报纸，嘴巴张着却说不出话，一阵风从他们身边刮过，一头扎进海伦的房间。这老头显然是急昏了头。

===Original===
They followed him into the room in time to see him with his face buried in the covers of Helen’s bed and she, horrified, glancing at the six-inch-tall headline. Matthew rushed to her side as she slumped back on the pillow in a dead faint. The physician and nurse dashed to revive her. The old man on his knees sobbed hoarsely. Mrs. Givens looking fifteen years older appeared in the doorway. Bunny glanced at Matthew who slightly lowered his left eyelid and with difficulty suppressed a smile.

“We’ve got to get out o’ this!” shouted the Imperial Grand Wizard. “We’ve got to get out o’ this. Oh, it’s terrible.… I never knew it myself, for sure.… Oh, Matthew, get us out of this, I tell you. They almost mobbed me at the office.… Came in just as I went out the back way.… Almost ten thousand of them.… We can’t lose a minute. Quick, I tell you! They’ll murder us all.”

“I’ll look out for everything,” Matthew soothed condescendingly. “I’ll stick by you.” Then turning swiftly to his partner he commanded, “Bunny order both cars out at once. We’ll beat it for the airport.… Doctor Brocker, will you go with us to look out for Helen and the baby? We’ve got to get out right now. I’ll pay you your price.”

“Sure I’ll go, Mr. Fisher,” said the physician, quietly. “I wouldn’t leave Mrs. Fisher now.”

===Chinese===
两人跟着他进了屋，正瞧见他把脸埋在海伦床上的被子里，而海伦惊骇地瞟着那个六英寸高的大标题。海伦往枕头上一仰，昏死过去，马修抢步冲到她身边。大夫和护士赶忙上前抢救。老头儿跪在地上，嘶哑地抽泣。吉文斯太太出现在门口，看上去老了十五岁。邦尼瞟了马修一眼，马修微微垂下左眼皮，好容易才压住一丝笑意。

“咱们得赶紧脱身！”帝国大巫师（Imperial Grand Wizard）吼道。“咱们必须脱身。哦，太可怕了……这事儿我自己也从来没个准信儿……哦，马修，带我们脱离这步田地，我跟你说。他们差点在办公室把我给围殴了……我前脚从后门溜出去，他们后脚就冲进来了……差不多有一万号人……一分钟也耽搁不得。快，我跟你说！他们会把咱们全都干掉的。”

“一切有我照应，”马修摆出施恩的口吻安抚道。“我不会撇下你们。”随即他迅速转向合伙人，发话道：“邦尼，马上叫两辆车一齐开出来。咱们直奔机场。……布罗克（Brocker）医生，您跟我们一起走，照看海伦和孩子，行不行？咱们现在就得动身。酬金您开价。”

“我当然跟您走，费舍尔先生。”大夫平静地说。“这个时候，我不会丢下费舍尔太太的。”

===Original===
The nurse had succeeded in bringing Helen to consciousness. She was weeping bitterly, denouncing fate and her father. With that logicality that frequently causes people to accept as truth circumstantial evidence that is not necessarily conclusive, she was assuming that the suspiciously brown color of her newborn son was due to some hidden Negro drop of blood in her veins. She looked up at her husband beseechingly.

“Oh, Matthew, darling,” she cried, her long red-gold hair framing her face, “I’m so sorry about all this. If I’d only known, I’d never have let you in for it. I would have spared you this disgrace and humiliation. Oh, Matthew, Honey, please forgive me. I love you, my husband. Please don’t leave me, please don’t leave me!” She reached out and grasped the tail of his coat as if he were going to leave that very minute.

“Now, now, little girl,” said Matthew soothingly, touched by her words, “You haven’t disgraced me; you’ve honored me by presenting me with a beautiful son.”

He looked down worshipfully at the chubby, ball of brownness in the nurse’s arms.

“You needn’t worry about me, Helen. I’ll stick by you as long as you’ll have me and without you life wouldn’t be worth a dime. You’re not responsible for the color of our baby, my dear. I’m the guilty one.”

===Chinese===
护士总算把海伦弄醒了。她哭得死去活来，咒骂命运，也咒骂自己的父亲。人常常凭着那么一种逻辑，把未必算得上铁证的间接证据当作事实来接受——她正是如此，认定新生儿子那可疑的棕色，必定是自家血管里潜藏着黑人血脉的点滴所致。她抬起头，哀哀地望着丈夫。

“哦，马修，亲爱的，”她哭喊道，红金色的长发披散在脸庞四周，“这一切都让我难受极了。早知道的话，我死也不会把你拖累进来。也就不会让你蒙受这份耻辱和难堪。哦，马修，宝贝儿，求你原谅我。我爱你，我的丈夫。求你别离开我，求你别离开我！”她伸出手去，一把攥住他外套的后摆，仿佛他马上就要走掉似的。

“好了，好了，小乖乖，”马修柔声安慰道，被她的话打动了，“你没有辱没我；你给了我一个漂亮的儿子，是给了我体面。”

他低头虔敬地望着护士怀里那个胖乎乎的棕色小肉团。

“你不用替我担心，海伦。只要你要我，我就一直守着你；没有你，这条命一个子儿也不值。咱们孩子的肤色怪不着你，亲爱的。有罪的是我。”

===Original===
Dr. Brocker smiled knowingly, Givens rose up indignantly, Bunny opened his mouth in surprise, Mrs. Givens folded her arms and her mouth changed to a slit and the nurse said “Oh!”

“You?” cried Helen in astonishment.

“Yes, me,” Matthew repeated, a great load lifting from his soul. Then for a few minutes he poured out his secret to the astonished little audience.

Helen felt a wave of relief go over her. There was no feeling of revulsion at the thought that her husband was a Negro. There once would have been but that was seemingly centuries ago when she had been unaware of her remoter Negro ancestry. She felt proud of her Matthew. She loved him more than ever. They had money and a beautiful, brown baby. What more did they need? To hell with the world! To hell with society! Compared to what she possessed, thought Helen, all talk of race and color was damned foolishness. She would probably have been surprised to learn that countless Americans at that moment were thinking the same thing.

===Chinese===
布罗克医生会心一笑，吉文斯愤然立起身来，邦尼吃惊地张大了嘴，吉文斯太太双臂一抱，嘴巴抿成一条细缝，护士则“哦”了一声。

“你？”海伦惊得叫出声来。

“对，是我。”马修又说了一遍，心头仿佛卸下了千斤重担。接着，他花了几分钟，把自己的秘密向那一小圈目瞪口呆的听众和盘托出。

一阵如释重负的暖流传遍海伦全身。想到丈夫是黑人，她心里竟没有一丝反感。搁在从前或许是会有的，可那仿佛已是几个世纪以前的事了，那时她还不晓得自己较远的黑人血统。她为自己的马修感到骄傲。她比以往任何时候都更爱他。他们有钱，又有了一个漂亮的棕色娃娃。还要什么呢？让这个世界见鬼去吧！让上流社会见鬼去吧！海伦心想，比起她所拥有的一切，什么种族不种族、肤色不肤色的空谈，全他妈是蠢话。倘若她知道此时此刻有无数美国人心里转的正是一模一样的念头，她多半会大吃一惊。

===Original===
“Well,” said Bunny, grinning, “it sure is good to be able to admit that you’re a jigwalk once more.”

“Yes, Bunny,” said old man Givens, “I guess we’re all niggers now.”

“Negroes, Mr. Givens, Negroes,” corrected Dr. Brocker, entering the room. “I’m in the same boat with the rest of you, only my dark ancestors are not so far back. I sure hope the Republicans win.”

“Don’t worry, Doc,” said Bunny. “They’ll win all right. And how! Gee whiz! I bet Sherlock Holmes, Nick Carter and all the Pinkertons couldn’t find old Senator Kretin and Arthur Snobbcraft now.”

===Chinese===
“嘿，”邦尼咧嘴笑道，“又能痛痛快快承认自己是个黑鬼了，这滋味可真不赖。”

“是啊，邦尼，”吉文斯老头说，“我看咱们如今全是黑鬼了。”

“是黑人，吉文斯先生，黑人。”布罗克医生走进屋来，纠正道。“我和诸位坐在同一条船上，只不过我那些黑人祖先离现在近得多。我实在盼着共和党赢。”

“您甭操心，大夫，”邦尼说。“他们准赢。赢得那叫一个漂亮！乖乖！我敢打赌，这会儿就算歇洛克·福尔摩斯、尼克·卡特（Nick Carter）带着平克顿侦探社（Pinkertons）全体人马出马，也休想找到克雷廷老参议员和亚瑟·斯诺布克拉夫特的影儿。”

===Original===
“Come on,” shouted the apprehensive Givens, “let’s get out o’ here before that mob comes.”

“Whut mob, Daddy?” asked Mrs. Givens.

“You’ll find out damn quick if you don’t shake it up,” replied her husband.

---

===Chinese===
“快走，”惶惶不安的吉文斯嚷道，“趁那伙暴民还没到，赶紧离开这儿。”

“什么暴民呀，孩子他爸？”吉文斯太太问。

“你再不麻利点儿，立马就知道了。”她丈夫答道。

---

===Original===
Through the crisp, autumn night air sped Fisher’s big tri-motored plane, headed southwest to the safety of Mexico. Reclining in a large, comfortable deck chair was Helen Fisher, calm and at peace with the world. In a hammock near her was her little brown son, Matthew, Junior. Beside her, holding her hand, was Matthew. Up front near the pilot, Bunny and Givens were playing Conquian. Behind them sat the nurse and Dr. Brocker, silently gazing out of the window at the twinkling lights of the Gulf Coast. Old lady Givens snored in the rear of the ship.

“Damn!” muttered Givens, as Bunny threw down his last spread and won the third consecutive game. “I sure wish I’d had time to grab some jack before we pulled out o’ Atlanta. Ain’t got but five dollars and fifty-three cents to my name.”

“Don’t worry about that, Old Timer,” Bunny laughed. “I don’t think we left over a thousand bucks in the treasury. See that steel box over there? Well, that ain’t got nothin’ in it but bucks and more bucks. Not a bill smaller than a grand.”

“Well, I’m a son-of-a-gun,” blurted the Imperial Grand Wizard. “That boy thinks o’ everything.”

But Givens was greatly depressed, much more so than the others. He had really believed all that he had preached about white supremacy, race purity and the menace of the alien, the Catholic, the Modernist and the Jew. He had always been sincere in his prejudices.

===Chinese===
秋夜清冽的空气里，费舍尔那架大型三引擎飞机疾速飞行，朝西南方向，奔往安全的墨西哥。海伦·费舍尔仰倚在一张宽大舒适的躺椅里，心境平和，与世无争。她近旁的吊床上，躺着她的棕色小儿子——小马修。马修坐在她身边，握着她的手。前头飞行员旁边，邦尼和吉文斯正打着康魁安牌（Conquian）。两人身后坐着护士和布罗克医生，默默望着窗外墨西哥湾沿岸闪闪烁烁的灯火。吉文斯老太太在机尾鼾声大作。

“见鬼！”吉文斯咕哝道，邦尼摊下最后一把成形的牌，连胜了第三局。“真恨咱们撤出亚特兰大的时候没来得及捞点票子。我名下统共只剩五块五毛三分钱。”

“这您就不用愁啦，老前辈，”邦尼大笑。“我看咱们留在库里的钱，可不止一千块。瞧见那边那只铁皮箱了吗？嗐，里头除了票子还是票子。最小的面额也是一千。”

“嘿，好家伙！”帝国大巫师脱口而出。“这小子什么都想得到。”

可吉文斯却郁郁寡欢，比谁都消沉。他传道时讲的那些话——白人至上、种族纯正，以及外侨、天主教徒、现代派教徒和犹太人的威胁——他自己原本是真信的。他的偏见，从来都是真心实意的。

===Original===
When they arrived at the Valbuena Air Field outside Mexico City, a messenger brought Bunny a telegram.

“You better thank your stars you got away from there, Matt,” he grinned, handing his friend the telegram. “See what my gal says?”

Matthew glanced over the message and handed it to Givens without comment. It read:

> Hope you arrive safely Senator Kretin lynched in Union Station Stop Snobbcraft and Buggerie reported in flight Stop Goosie and Gump almost unanimously reelected Stop Government has declared martial law until disturbances stop Stop When can I come?
> **Madeline Scranton**.

===Chinese===
飞机降落在墨西哥城郊外的瓦尔布埃纳机场（Valbuena Air Field），一个信使给邦尼送来一封电报。

“马特，你能从那儿脱身，真得谢天谢地。”他咧嘴笑着，把电报递给朋友。“瞧瞧我那姑娘怎么说？”

马修扫了一眼电文，一言不发地递给吉文斯。电报上写道：

> 盼平安抵达 参议员克雷廷（Kretin）在联合车站遭私刑处死 止 据报斯诺布克拉夫特与布热里在逃 止 古西与冈普（Gump）几乎全票连任 止 政府已宣布戒严直至骚乱平息 止 我何时能来？
> **玛德琳·斯克兰顿（Madeline Scranton）**

===Original===
“Who’s this Scranton broad?” queried Matthew in a whisper, cutting a precautionary glance at his wife.

“A sweet Georgia brown,” exclaimed Bunny enthusiastically.

“No!” gasped Matthew, incredulous.

“She ain’t no Caucasian!” Bunny replied.

“She must be the last black gal in the country,” Matthew remarked, glancing enviously at his friend. “How come she didn’t get white, too?”

===Chinese===
“这个斯克兰顿娘们儿是谁？”马修压低嗓子问，一面提防似的瞟了妻子一眼。

“一朵甜滋滋的佐治亚棕妞儿（sweet Georgia brown）！”邦尼眉飞色舞地嚷道。

“不会吧！”马修倒抽一口冷气，难以置信。

“她可不是高加索种（白人）哟！”邦尼答道。

“她八成是全国最后一个黑人姑娘了。”马修说着，艳羡地瞟了朋友一眼。“她怎么没跟着去变白呢？”

===Original===
“Well,” Bunny replied, a slight hint of pride in his voice. “She’s a race patriot. She’s funny that way.”

“Well, for cryin’ out loud!” exclaimed Matthew, scratching his head and sort of half grinning in a bewildered way. “*What* kind o’ *sheba* is that?”

Old man Givens came over to where they were standing, the telegram in his hand and an expression of serenity now on his face.

“Boys,” he announced, “it looks like it’s healthier down here right now than it is back there in Georgia.”

“*Looks* like it’s healthier?” mocked Bunny. “Brother, you know damn *well* it’s healthier!”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，”邦尼答道，话音里透着一丝得意。“她是位种族爱国者。她那人就是那么个怪脾气。”

“嗐，我的老天爷！”马修嚷道，抓了抓脑袋，半信半疑地咧着嘴。“那到底是个*什么样*的*宝贝妞儿*？”

吉文斯老头走到两人站的地方，手里捏着那封电报，脸上如今是一片安详。

“小伙子们，”他宣布道，“眼下瞧着，待在这儿可比回佐治亚那边健康多喽。”

“*瞧着*更健康？”邦尼揶揄道。“老兄，你心里*透亮*得很，这分明就是更健康！”

## XIII / 第十三章
===Original===

Toward eleven o’clock on the evening before election day, a long, low roadster swept up to the door of a stately country home near Richmond, VA, crunched to a stop, the lights were extinguished and two men, one tall and angular, the other huge and stout, catapulted from the car. Without wasting words, they raced around the house and down a small driveway to a rambling shed in a level field about three hundred yards to the rear. Breathless, they halted before the door and beat upon it excitedly.
“Open up there, Frazier!” ordered Snobbcraft, for it was he. “Open that door.” There was no answer. The only reply was the chirping of crickets and the rustle of branches.
“He must not be here,” said Dr. Buggerie, glancing fearfully over his shoulder and wiping a perspiring brow with a damp handkerchief.
“The damned rascal had better be here,” thundered the Democratic candidate for Vice-President, beating again on the door. “I telephoned him two hours ago to be ready.”
As he spoke someone unlocked the door and rolled it aside an inch or two.
“Is that you, Mr. Snobbcraft?” asked a sleepy voice from the darkness within.
“Open that damned door, you fool,” barked Snobbcraft. “Didn’t I tell you to have that plane ready when we got here? Why don’t you do as you’re told?” He and Dr. Buggerie helped slide the great doors back. The man Frazier snapped on the lights, revealing within a big, three-motored plane with an automobile nestling under each of its wings.
“I﻿—I kinda fell asleep waitin’ for you, Mr. Snobbcraft,” Frazier apologized, “but everything’s ready.”
“All right, man,” shouted the president of the Anglo-Saxon Association, “let’s get away from here then. This is a matter of life and death. You ought to have had the plane outside and all warmed up to go.”
“Yes sir,” the man mumbled meekly, busying himself.
“These damned, stupid, poor white trash!” growled Snobbcraft, glaring balefully at the departing aviator.
“D‑D‑Don’t antagonize him,” muttered Buggerie. “He’s our only chance to get away.”
“Shut up, fool! If it hadn’t been for you and your damned fool statistics we wouldn’t be in this fix.”
“You wanted them, didn’t you?” whined the statistician in defense.
“Well, I didn’t tell you to leave that damned summary where anybody could get hold of it.” Snobbcraft replied, reproachfully. “That was the most stupid thing I ever heard of.”

===Chinese===

大选日前夜，将近十一点钟的时候，一辆低矮修长的跑车驶到弗吉尼亚州里士满附近一座气派庄园的门前，嘎吱一声刹住，车灯随即熄灭，两个男人——一个又高又瘦，一个又壮又肥——从车里一跃而出。二人半句废话也没有，绕过宅子，沿一条小车道直奔屋后约三百码外平地上的一座敞棚。两人气喘吁吁地在门前停住，激动地擂起门来。
“开门哪，弗雷泽！”斯诺布克拉夫特喝令道，正是他本人。“把门打开。”没有回应，回答他的只有蟋蟀的唧唧声和枝叶的沙沙声。
“他准是不在。”巴格里博士说着，惊恐地回头望了一眼，用一块湿手帕抹了抹汗涔涔的额头。
“那个该死的流氓最好给我在！”这位民主党副总统候选人雷霆般吼道，又擂起门来。“两个钟头前我就打电话叫他准备好了。”
正说话间，有人开了锁，把门推开了一两英寸。
“是斯诺布克拉夫特先生吗？”门里的黑暗中传来一个睡意惺忪的声音。
“开你那该死的门，蠢货。”斯诺布克拉夫特咆哮道。“我不是吩咐过你，我们一到就得万事俱备吗？你怎么不照吩咐办？”他和巴格里博士帮着把大门推开。弗雷泽啪的一声拉亮电灯，棚里赫然是一架三引擎的大飞机，两边的机翼下各趴着一辆汽车。
“我——我等您等着等着，就有点儿睡着了，斯诺布克拉夫特先生。”弗雷泽赔不是道，“不过样样都齐备了。”
“行啦，伙计，”这位盎格鲁-撒克逊协会主席高声道，“那咱们赶紧离开这儿。这是性命交关的事。你本该把飞机拖到外头，发动机也预热好了待命。”
“是，先生。”那人低眉顺眼地咕哝一声，忙活起来。
“这帮该死的、愚蠢的白人穷鬼！”斯诺布克拉夫特冲着走开的飞行员的背影恶狠狠地嘟哝。
“别——别——别去招惹他，”巴格里低声道，“咱们脱身全指望他了。”
“闭嘴，蠢货！要不是你和你那该死的统计数字，咱们何至于落到这步田地。”
“还不是你自己要的？”统计学家哭丧着辩白。
“我可没叫你把那份该死的总结随手一搁，叫谁都拿得着。”斯诺布克拉夫特反唇相讥。“这种蠢事，我闻所未闻。”

===Original===

Buggerie opened his mouth to reply but said nothing. He just glared at Snobbcraft who glared back at him. The two men presented a disheveled appearance. The Vice-Presidential candidate was haggard, hatless, collarless and still wore his smoking jacket. The eminent statistician and author of *The Incidence of Psittacosis Among the Hiphopa Indians of the Amazon Valley and Its Relation to Life Insurance Rates in the United States*, looked far from dignified with no necktie, canvas breeches, no socks and wearing a shooting jacket he had snatched from a closet on his way out of the house. He had forgotten his thick spectacles and his bulging eyes were red and watery. They paced impatiently back and forth, glancing first at the swiftly working Frazier and then down the long driveway toward the glowing city.
Ten minutes they waited while Frazier went over the plane to see that all was well. Then they helped him roll the huge metal bird out of the hangar and on to the field. Gratefully they climbed inside and fell exhausted on the soft-cushioned seats.
“Well, that sure is a relief,” gasped the ponderous Buggerie, mopping his brow.
“Wait until we get in the air,” growled Snobbcraft. “Anything’s liable to happen after that mob tonight. I was never so humiliated in my life. The idea of that gang of poor white trash crowding up my steps and yelling nigger. It was disgraceful.”
“Yes, it was terrible,” agreed Buggerie. “It’s a good thing they didn’t go in the rear where your car was. We wouldn’t have been able to get away.”
“I thought there would be a demonstration,” said Snobbcraft, some of his old sureness returning, “that’s why I phoned Frazier to get ready.﻿ ﻿… Oh, it’s a damned shame to be run out of your own home in this way!”
He glared balefully at the statistician who averted his gaze.
“All ready, sir,” announced Frazier, “where are we headed?”
“To my ranch in Chihuahua, and hurry up,” snapped Snobbcraft.
“But﻿—But we ain’t got enough gas to go that far,” said Frazier. “I﻿—I﻿—You didn’t say you wanted to go to Mexico, Boss.”
Snobbcraft stared incredulously at the man. His rage was so great that he could not speak for a moment or two. Then he launched into a stream of curses that would have delighted a pirate captain, while the unfortunate aviator gaped indecisively.

===Chinese===

巴格里张口欲辩，却终于什么也没说，只是瞪着斯诺布克拉夫特，斯诺布克拉夫特也回瞪着他。两人一副狼狈相。副总统候选人面容憔悴，没戴帽子，没上领子，身上还穿着那件吸烟服。至于那位声名显赫的统计学家——《亚马逊流域希波帕印第安人鹦鹉热发病率及其与美国寿险费率之关系》（*The Incidence of Psittacosis Among the Hiphopa Indians of the Amazon Valley and Its Relation to Life Insurance Rates in the United States*）的作者——此刻没系领带，下身一条帆布马裤，没穿袜子，外面胡乱套着出门时从壁橱里抓来的一件猎装，离体面二字更是十万八千里。他忘了戴那副厚眼镜，鼓出的眼睛又红又泪水汪汪。两人焦躁地来回踱步，一会儿瞅一眼手脚麻利的弗雷泽，一会儿又顺着长长的车道望向灯火通明的城市。
他们等了十分钟，弗雷泽把飞机里里外外检视一遍，确认一切无恙。随后两人帮他把这只金属巨鸟推出机棚，推上场地，然后感激不尽地钻进机舱，瘫软在软垫座椅上。
“哎呀，这下可算松了口气。”笨重的巴格里喘着粗气，抹了把额头。
“等上了天再松气吧。”斯诺布克拉夫特咕哝道。“今晚那帮暴民闹过之后，什么事都保不齐。我这辈子没受过这等羞辱。一群白人穷鬼涌上我家的台阶，扯着嗓子喊黑鬼。斯文扫地，斯文扫地。”
“是啊，太吓人了。”巴格里附和道。“幸亏他们没绕到后头您停车的地方，不然咱们想跑也跑不成了。”
“我早料到会有示威，”斯诺布克拉夫特说，几分往日的笃定又回到了身上，“所以才打电话叫弗雷泽准备。……唉，让人从自己家里这么撵出去，真他妈的丢人！”
他恶狠狠地瞪着统计学家，统计学家把目光躲开了。
“一切就绪，先生。”弗雷泽禀道，“咱们上哪儿？”
“去我在奇瓦瓦的牧场，麻利点。”斯诺布克拉夫特厉声道。
“可——可是油不够跑那么远啊。”弗雷泽说。“我——我——老板，您可没说要去墨西哥。”
斯诺布克拉夫特难以置信地瞪着这人，怒火烧得他半晌说不出话。随后他破口大骂，那一连串恶言足以叫海盗船长听得心花怒放，倒霉的飞行员目瞪口呆，进退两难。

===Original===

In the midst of this diatribe, the sound of automobile horns and klaxons rent the air, punctuated by shouts and pistol shots. The three men in the plane saw coming down the road from the city a bobbing stream of headlights. Already the cavalcade was almost to the gate of the Snobbcraft country estate.
“Come on, get out of here,” gasped Snobbcraft. “We’ll get some gas farther down the line. Hurry up!”
Dr. Buggerie, speechless and purple with fear, pushed the aviator out of the plane. The fellow gave the propeller a whirl, jumped back into the cabin, took the controls and the great machine rolled out across the field.
They had started none too soon. The automobile cavalcade was already coming up the driveway. The drone of the motor drowned out the sound of the approaching mob but the two fearful men saw several flashes that betokened pistol shots. Several of the automobiles took out across the field in the wake of the plane. They seemed to gain on it. Snobbcraft and Buggerie gazed nervously ahead. They were almost at the end of the field and the plane had not yet taken to the air. The pursuing automobiles drew closer. There were several more flashes from firearms. A bullet tore through the side of the cabin. Simultaneously Snobbcraft and Buggerie fell to the floor.
At last the ship rose, cleared the trees at the end of the field and began to attain altitude. The two men took deep breaths of relief, rose and flung themselves on the richly upholstered seats.
A terrible stench suddenly became noticeable to the two passengers and the aviator. The latter looked inquiringly over his shoulder; Snobbcraft and Buggerie, their noses wrinkled and their foreheads corrugated, glanced suspiciously at each other. Both moved uneasily in their seats and looks of guilt succeeded those of accusation. Snobbcraft retreated precipitously to the rear cabin while the statistician flung open several windows and then followed the Vice-Presidential candidate.
Fifteen minutes later two bundles were tossed out of the window of the rear cabin and the two passengers, looking sheepish but much relieved, resumed their seats. Snobbcraft was wearing a suit of brown dungarees belonging to Frazier while his scientific friend had wedged himself into a pair of white trousers usually worn by Snobbcraft’s valet. Frazier turned, saw them, and grinned.

===Chinese===

正骂得酣畅，汽车喇叭声和电笛声撕破了夜空，其间夹杂着喊叫和枪声。机舱里的三个人望见从城里方向驶来一长溜起伏颠簸的车灯，车队眼看就要到斯诺布克拉夫特庄园的大门口了。
“快，赶紧起飞！”斯诺布克拉夫特喘着气喊。“到前头再想办法弄油。快点！”
巴格里博士吓得脸色发紫，一言不发，把飞行员推出了机舱。那家伙拔转螺旋桨，翻身跳回舱内，握住操纵杆，庞大的机器便碾着田野冲了出去。
他们动身得再晚一步就完了。车队已经开上了车道。马达的嗡鸣盖过了逼近人群的声浪，但两个魂飞魄散的人还是望见了几道火光——那是枪声。几辆汽车离开车道，尾随着飞机压过田野，眼看越追越近。斯诺布克拉夫特和巴格里紧张地盯着前方。都快到田野尽头了，飞机还没离地。追兵逼得更近了。又是几道枪火。一颗子弹穿透机舱侧壁，斯诺布克拉夫特和巴格里双双扑倒在地。
飞机终于升了起来，掠过田野尽头的树梢，开始爬高。两人深深吸了一口宽心的气，起身瘫倒在豪华的软垫座椅上。
突然，一股恶臭钻进了两位乘客和飞行员的鼻子。飞行员回头投来询问的一瞥；斯诺布克拉夫特和巴格里皱起鼻子、拧起眉头，狐疑地彼此对望。两人在座位上都不自在地扭动起来，指责的脸色渐渐换成了心虚。斯诺布克拉夫特一溜烟退进后舱，统计学家推开几扇窗子，也跟着副总统候选人钻了进去。
十五分钟后，两个包袱从后舱窗口被扔了出来，两位乘客面带愧色、却如释重负地回到座位上。斯诺布克拉夫特换上了弗雷泽的一套棕色粗布工装，他的科学家朋友则把自己塞进了一条白裤子——那原是斯诺布克拉夫特的贴身男仆穿的。弗雷泽回过头看见两人，咧嘴一笑。

===Original===

Hour after hour the plane winged its way through the night. Going a hundred miles an hour it passed town after town. About dawn, as they were passing over Meridian, Mississippi, the motor began to miss.
“What’s the matter there?” Snobbcraft inquired nervously into the pilot’s ear.
“The gas is runnin’ low,” Frazier replied grimly. “We’ll have to land pretty soon.”
“No, no, not in Mississippi!” gasped Buggerie, growing purple with apprehension. “They’ll lynch us if they find out who we are.”
“Well, we can’t stay up here much longer,” the pilot warned.
Snobbcraft bit his lip and thought furiously. It was true they would be taking a chance by landing anywhere in the South, let alone in Mississippi, but what could they do? The motor was missing more frequently and Frazier had cut down their speed to save gasoline. They were just idling along. The pilot looked back at Snobbcraft inquiringly.
“By God, we’re in a fix now,” said the president of the Anglo-Saxon Association. Then he brightened with a sudden idea. “We could hide in the rear cabin while Frazier gets gasoline,” he suggested.
“Suppose somebody looks in the rear cabin?” queried Buggerie, dolefully, thrusting his hands into the pockets of his white trousers. “There’s bound to be a lot of curious people about when a big plane like this lands in a farming district.”
As he spoke his left hand encountered something hard in the pocket. It felt like a box of salve. He withdrew it curiously. It was a box of shoe polish which the valet doubtless used on Snobbcraft’s footgear. He looked at it aimlessly and was about to thrust it back into the pocket when he had a brilliant idea.
“Look here, Snobbcraft,” he cried excitedly, his rheumy eyes popping out of his head farther than usual. “This is just the thing.”
“What do you mean?” asked his friend, eyeing the little tin box.
“Well,” explained the scientist, “you know real niggers are scarce now and nobody would think of bothering a couple of them, even in Mississippi. They’d probably be a curiosity.”
“What are you getting at, man?”
“This: we can put this blacking on our head, face, neck and hands, and no one will take us for Snobbcraft and Buggerie. Frazier can tell anybody that inquires that we’re two darkies he’s taking out of the country, or something like that. Then, after we get our gas and start off again, we can wash the stuff off with gasoline.

===Chinese===

飞机一小时接一小时地穿夜飞行，以每小时一百英里的速度掠过一座又一座城镇。拂晓时分，正飞临密西西比州默里迪恩上空，发动机开始断火。
“那儿怎么了？”斯诺布克拉夫特紧张地凑到飞行员耳边问。
“油快见底了。”弗雷泽沉着脸答道。“咱们很快就得降落。”
“不，不，千万别降在密西西比！”巴格里喘着气说，惊恐得脸都紫了。“他们要是查出咱们是谁，会把咱们吊死的。”
“可咱们在天上也撑不了多久了。”飞行员警告道。
斯诺布克拉夫特咬着嘴唇，脑子里飞快盘算。不错，在南方任何地方降落都是冒险，更别提密西西比了，可又有什么法子？发动机断火越来越勤，弗雷泽已经减速省油，他们几乎是在滑翔。飞行员回头询问地望着斯诺布克拉夫特。
“老天爷，这回可真是进退维谷了。”盎格鲁-撒克逊协会主席说。忽然他灵机一动，脸上放光。“等弗雷泽加油的时候，咱们可以躲到后舱去。”他提议道。
“要是有人往后舱里瞅呢？”巴格里愁眉苦脸地问，双手插进白裤子的口袋。“这么大的飞机降在庄稼地里，四下里准围满了看稀奇的人。”
说话间，他的左手在口袋里碰着一个硬东西，摸着像是一盒药膏。他好奇地掏出来——是一盒鞋油，八成是那男仆给斯诺布克拉夫特擦皮靴用的。他百无聊赖地看着，正要塞回口袋，忽然灵光一闪。
“你瞧，斯诺布克拉夫特，”他激动地嚷道，昏花的老眼瞪得比平常更圆。“这东西正合适。”
“什么意思？”他的朋友端详着那只小铁盒问。
“是这样，”科学家解释道，“如今货真价实的黑鬼稀罕了，谁也不会想着去为难两个黑鬼，哪怕是在密西西比。他们没准儿倒成了稀罕物。”
“你葫芦里卖的什么药，伙计？”
“就是这个：咱们把这黑油抹在头上、脸上、脖子上、手上，就没人会把咱们认成斯诺布克拉夫特和巴格里了。弗雷泽可以告诉打听的人，说咱们是他带出国境的两个黑炭，或者随口编点什么。等咱们加足了油重新起飞，再用汽油把这玩意儿洗掉。”

===Original===

It’s our only chance, Arthur. If we go down like we are, they’ll kill us sure.”
Snobbcraft pursed his lips and pondered the proposition for a moment. It was indeed, he saw, their only chance to effectively escape detection.
“All right,” he agreed, “let’s hurry up. This ship Won’t stay up much longer.”
Industriously they daubed each other’s head, neck, face, chest, hands and arms with the shoe polish. In five minutes they closely resembled a brace of mammy singers. Snobbcraft hurriedly instructed Frazier.
The plane slowly circled to the ground. The region was slightly rolling and there was no good landing place. There could be no delay, however, so Frazier did his best. The big ship bumped over logs and through weeds, heading straight for a clump of trees. Quickly the pilot steered it to the left only to send it head first into a ditch. The plane turned completely over, one wing was entirely smashed and Frazier, caught in the wreckage under the engine, cried feebly for help for a few moments and then lay still.
Shaken up and bruised, the two passengers, managed to crawl out of the cabin window to safety. Dolefully they stood in the Mississippi sunlight, surveying the wreckage and looking questioningly at each other.
“Well,” whined Dr. Buggerie, rubbing one large sore buttock, “what now?”
“Shut up,” growled Snobbcraft. “If it hadn’t been for you, we wouldn’t be here.”
---
Happy Hill, Mississippi, was all aflutter. For some days it had been preparing for the great, open-air revival of the True Faith Christ Lovers’ Church. The faithful for miles around were expected to attend the services scheduled for the afternoon of Election Day and which all hoped would last well into the night.
This section of the state had been untouched by the troubles through which the rest of the South had gone as a result of the activities of Black-No-More, Incorporated. The people for miles around were with very few exceptions old residents and thence known to be genuine blue-blooded Caucasians for as far back as any resident could remember which was at least fifty years. The people were proud of this fact. They were more proud, however, of the fact that Happy Hill was the home and birthplace of the True Faith Christ Lovers’ Church, which made the prodigious boast of being the most truly Fundamentalist of all the Christian sects in the United States.

===Chinese===

“这是咱们唯一的活路，阿瑟。要是就这么着下去，他们准把咱们弄死。”
斯诺布克拉夫特咂了咂嘴，把这条计策掂量片刻。他看明白了，这确是瞒过耳目的唯一法子。
“好吧，”他应道，“那就麻利点。这家伙在天上撑不了多久了。”
两人起劲地互相往对方头上、脖子上、脸上、胸口、手上、胳膊上涂抹鞋油。不出五分钟，两人已活脱脱像一对唱灵歌的黑大娘。斯诺布克拉夫特匆匆向弗雷泽交代了一番。
飞机缓缓盘旋着向地面降落。这一带地势微微起伏，找不到像样的降落场。可是事不宜迟，弗雷泽只得尽力而为。大飞机颠过木桩，碾过荒草，直冲一小片树林而去。飞行员急忙向左打舵，结果机头一头扎进沟里。飞机整个翻了个底朝天，一边机翼彻底撞烂，弗雷泽被压在发动机下的残骸中，微弱地呼救了几声，便不动了。
两个乘客震得晕头转向、青一块紫一块，好歹从机舱窗口爬出来，落到了安全地带。他们愁眉苦脸地站在密西西比的太阳底下，打量着残骸，又疑问地彼此对望。
“唉，”巴格里博士揉着一边摔得生疼的大屁股，哭丧着说，“这可怎么办？”
“闭嘴。”斯诺布克拉夫特吼道。“要不是为了你，咱们哪会到这儿来。”
---
密西西比州的快活山（Happy Hill）上下一片沸腾。几天来，全镇一直在筹备真信仰爱基督教会（True Faith Christ Lovers' Church）的盛大露天奋兴布道会。方圆数英里的信众都将参加定于大选日下午举行的礼拜，人人指望这场礼拜热热闹闹地一直开到深夜。
这一带没沾上“不再黑公司”的营生给南方其余地方招来的那些乱子。方圆数英里的居民，除了寥寥几户，都是老住户，据任何一位居民的记忆所及——至少能上溯五十年——他们是如假包换的蓝血高加索人。居民们为此自豪。不过他们更自豪的是，快活山是真信仰爱基督教会的老家和发祥地，而这家教会夸下的海口是：它是合众国所有基督教派里最最地道原教旨派的一家。

===Original===

Other things of which the community might have boasted were its inordinately high illiteracy rate and its lynching record﻿—but these things were seldom mentioned, although no one was ashamed of them. Certain things are taken for granted everywhere.
Long before the United States had rid themselves of their Negroes through the good but unsolicited offices of Dr. Junius Crookman, Happy Hill had not only rid itself of what few Negroes had resided in its vicinity but of all itinerant blackamoors who lucklessly came through the place. Ever since the Civil War when the proud and courageous forefathers of the Caucasian inhabitants had vigorously resisted all efforts to draft them into the Confederate Army, there had been a sign nailed over the general store and post office reading, “**Niger rede & run. If u can’t rede, run eneyhowe.**” The literate denizens of Happy Hill would sometimes stand off and spell out the words with the pride that usually accompanies erudition.
The method by which Happy Hill discouraged blackamoors who sought the hospitality of the place, was simple: the offending Ethiopian was either hung or shot and then broiled. Across from the general store and post office was a large iron post about five feet high. On it all blacks were burned. Down one side of it was a long line of nicks made with hammer and chisel. Each nick stood for a Negro dispatched. This post was one of the landmarks of the community and was pointed out to visitors with pardonable civic pride by local boosters. Sage old fellows frequently remarked between expectorations of tobacco juice that the only Negro problem in Happy Hill was the difficulty of getting hold of a sufficient number of the Sons or Daughters of Ham to lighten the dullness of the place.
Quite naturally the news that all Negroes had disappeared, not only from their state but from the entire country, had been received with sincere regret by the inhabitants of Happy Hill. They envisioned the passing of an old, established custom. Now there was nothing left to stimulate them but the old time religion and the clandestine sex orgies that invariably and immediately followed the great revival meetings.
So the simple country folk had turned to religion with renewed ardor. There were several churches in the county, Methodist, Baptist, Campbellite and, of course, Holy Roller.

===Chinese===

这个村镇还有可以夸口的：高得离谱的文盲率和私刑纪录——不过这些事难得有人提起，尽管谁也不为之害臊。有些事情，在哪儿都是不证自明的。
早在合众国仰承朱尼厄斯·克鲁克曼博士好心然而不请自来的效力、把黑人都打发干净之前，快活山不但早已把住在左近的那几个黑人清理了出去，连碰巧打这儿路过的黑人游方客也一个不留。自打南北战争以来——当年高加索居民们高傲勇武的先人曾奋起抵制邦联军队征他们入伍——杂货铺兼邮局的门楣上就钉着一块牌子，上书：“**黑鬼认字快跑。不认字，也照样跑。**”快活山识字的居民有时会站开几步，把牌上的字一个个拼读出来，脸上带着学问人惯有的那股自豪。
快活山打消黑人游方客借宿念头的法子很简单：那位冒犯人的埃塞俄比亚人，要么吊死，要么枪毙，然后烤熟。杂货铺兼邮局的对面立着一根五英尺来高的粗铁桩，凡是黑人都在那上头烧。桩的一侧有一长溜用锤子凿子敲出来的豁口，每一道豁口记着一个被超度的黑人。这根铁桩是本地一景，本地热心人士常带着情有可原的乡土自豪指给外乡人看。睿智的老头儿们常常一边啐着烟草汁一边说：快活山唯一的黑人问题，就是难得抓着足够多的含的儿孙，好给这地方解解闷。
黑人统统绝迹的消息——不光是从他们这个州，而是从整个国家——传来时，快活山的居民们自然都真心惋惜。他们眼看着一项古老公例的习俗就此消亡。如今能给他们提神的，只剩下老派宗教，还有大型奋兴布道会之后必然立刻跟进的偷偷摸摸的性狂欢了。
于是这些淳朴的乡下人带着新的热忱皈依了宗教。县里有好几座教堂：卫理公会的、浸信会的、坎贝尔派的，当然，还有圣滚教的。

===Original===

The latter, indeed, had the largest membership. But the people, eager for something new, found all of the old churches too tame. They wanted a faith with more punch to it; a faith that would fittingly accompany the fierce corn liquor which all consumed, albeit they were all confirmed Prohibitionists.
Whenever and wherever there is a social need, some agency arises to supply it. The needs of Happy Hill were no exception. One day, several weeks previously, there had come to the community one Rev. Alex McPhule who claimed to be the founder of a new faith, a true faith, that would save all from the machinations of the Evil One. The other churches, he averred, had failed. The other churches had grown soft and were flirting with atheism and Modernism which, according to Rev. McPhule, were the same thing. An angel of God had visited him one summer evening in Meridian, he told them, when he was down sick in bed as the result of his sinning ways, and had told him to reform and go forth into the world and preach the true faith of Christ’s love. He had promised to do so, of course, and then the angel had placed the palm of his right hand on Rev. McPhule’s forehead and all of the sickness and misery had departed.
The residents of Happy Hill and vicinity listened with rapt attention and respect. The man was sincere, eloquent and obviously a Nordic. He was tall, thin, slightly knock-kneed, with a shock of unkempt red hair, wild blue eyes, hollow cheeks, lantern jaw and long apelike arms that looked very impressive when he waved them up and down during a harangue. His story sounded logical to the country people and they flocked in droves to his first revival held in a picturesque natural amphitheater about a mile from town.
No one had any difficulty in understanding the new faith. No music was allowed besides singing and thumping the bottom of a wooden tub. There were no chairs. Everybody sat on the ground in a circle with Rev. McPhule in the center. The holy man would begin an extemporaneous song and would soon have the faithful singing it after him and swinging from side to side in unison. Then he would break off abruptly and launch into an old fashion hellfire-and-damnation sermon in which demons, brimstone, adultery, rum, and other evils prominently figured.

===Chinese===

这最后一种，会众其实最多。可人们渴求新东西，嫌那些老教会都太温吞。他们要一种更有劲的信仰，一种配得上烈性玉米烧酒的信仰——这酒人人都在灌，尽管他们个个都是铁杆的禁酒派。
有社会需求，就会有应运而生的角色，快活山自不例外。几个星期前的一天，镇上来了一位亚历克斯·麦克菲尔牧师（Rev. Alex McPhule），自称是一种新信仰——真信仰——的开山祖师，这信仰能救众人脱离那恶者的诡计。他说，别的教会全砸了；别的教会都软掉了，正跟无神论和现代主义调情——照麦克菲尔牧师的说法，这俩本是同一路货。他告诉他们，某年一个夏天的傍晚，他在默里迪恩因为作孽放荡病倒在床，上帝的一位天使降临，吩咐他改邪归正，到世上去传基督之爱的真信仰。他自然满口应承，于是天使把右掌按在麦克菲尔牧师的额头上，他浑身的病痛苦恼登时一扫而空。
快活山和左近的居民听得如痴如醉、肃然起敬。这人诚恳，能说会道，而且明摆着是个诺迪克人。他又高又瘦，两条腿微微罗圈，一头蓬乱的红头发，一双狂野的蓝眼睛，瘪脸颊，长下巴，两条猿猴似的长胳膊——他讲道时上下抡将起来，煞是威风。他的故事在乡下人听来头头是道，第一场布道会便人如潮涌，开在离镇约一英里一处风景如画的天然圆形剧场里。
新信仰一点也不难懂。除了唱歌和敲木盆底，什么音乐都不许有。没有椅子，人人围成圆圈席地而坐，麦克菲尔牧师居中。这位圣者会即兴起个头唱起来，不一会儿就带着信众跟他唱，一齐左右摇摆。然后他会猛地刹住，来一篇老式的地狱之火加末日诅咒的布道，魔鬼、硫磺、奸淫、朗姆酒，还有各色邪恶，轮番挑大梁。

===Original===

At the height of his remarks, he would roll his eyes heavenward, froth at the mouth, run around on all fours and embrace in turn each member of the congregation, especially the buxom ladies. This would be the signal for others to follow his example. The sisters and brothers osculated, embraced and rolled, shouting meanwhile: “Christ is Love!﻿ ﻿… Love Christ!﻿ ﻿… Oh, be happy in the arms of Jesus!﻿ ﻿… Oh, Jesus, my Sweetheart!﻿ ﻿… Heavenly Father!” Frequently these revivals took place on the darkest nights with the place of worship dimly illuminated by pine torches. As these torches always seemed to conveniently burn out about the time the embracing and rolling started, the new faith rapidly became popular.
In a very short time nothing in Happy Hill was too good for Rev. Alex McPhule. Every latchstring hung out for him. As usual with gentlemen of the cloth, he was especially popular with the ladies. When the men were at work in the fields, the Man of God would visit house after house and comfort the womenfolk with his Christian message. Being a bachelor, he made these professional calls with great frequency.
The Rev. Alex McPhule also held private audiences with the sick, sinful and neurotic in his little cabin. There he had erected an altar covered with the white marble top from an old bureau. Around this altar were painted some grotesque figures, evidently the handiwork of the evangelist, while on the wall in back of the altar hung a large square of white oilcloth upon which was painted a huge eye. The sinner seeking surcease was commanded to gaze upon the eye while making confessions and requests. On the altar reposed a crudely-bound manuscript about three inches thick. This was the “Bible” of the Christ Lovers which the Rev. McPhule declared he had written at the command of Jesus Christ Himself. The majority of his visitors were middle-aged wives and adenoidal and neurotic young girls. None departed unsatisfied.
With all the good fortune that had come to the Rev. McPhule as a result of engaging in the Lord’s work, he was still dissatisfied. He never passed a Baptist, Methodist or Holy Roller church without jealousy and ambition surging up within him. He wanted everybody in the county in his flock. He wanted to do God’s work so effectually that the other churches would be put out of business.

===Chinese===

讲到最来劲的地方，他会眼珠上翻望天，嘴角挂沫，手脚并用地满地爬，挨个拥抱会众，尤其是那些胸脯丰满的大娘大婶。这便是众人效仿的信号。姊妹弟兄们又是亲嘴、又是搂抱、又是打滚，嘴里还喊个不停：“基督就是爱！……爱基督！……啊，在耶稣的怀抱里欢畅吧！……啊，耶稣，我的心肝！……天上的父啊！”这类布道会常挑最黑的夜里举行，礼拜场所只靠几支松明子照着，昏昏惨惨。由于这些火把总在搂抱打滚开始时恰到好处地烧完，新信仰很快便兴旺起来。
没过多久，快活山人就觉得，给亚历克斯·麦克菲尔牧师什么好东西都不为过了。家家都为他敞着门。教士老爷们向来格外讨女士们喜欢，他也不例外。男人们下地干活的时候，这位上帝的人便挨家挨户登门，用基督的讯息宽慰女眷。他是个单身汉，这类教牧出诊自然格外频繁。
亚历克斯·麦克菲尔牧师还在他那间小木屋里单独接见病人、罪人和害神经质的。他在屋里设了一座祭坛，坛面是旧五斗柜上拆下来的白色大理石台面。祭坛四周画着些奇形怪状的人像，一望便知出自这位布道者之手；祭坛后头的墙上挂着一大幅白油布，上面画着一只巨眼。来求解脱的罪人奉命一面认罪许愿，一面死盯着那只眼睛。祭坛上供着一部装订粗陋、约莫三英寸厚的手稿，这便是爱基督会的“圣经”，麦克菲尔牧师声称是奉耶稣基督亲口吩咐写的。登门的多数是中年主妇，还有腺样体肥大、神经兮兮的姑娘。没有一个不满载而归的。
主的事工给麦克菲尔牧师带来这许多鸿运，他仍旧不知足。每回经过一座浸信会、卫理公会或者圣滚教的教堂，嫉妒和野心就在他心里直往上涌。他要全县的人都进他的羊圈。他要把上帝的工干得如此漂亮，叫别的教堂统统歇业。

===Original===

He could only do this, he knew, with the aid of a message straight from Heaven. That alone would impress them.
He began to talk in his meetings about a sign coming down from Heaven to convince all doubters and infidels like Methodists and Baptists. His flock was soon on the nervous edge of expectancy but the Lord failed, for some reason, to answer the prayer of his right-hand man.
Rev. McPhule began to wonder what he had done to offend the Almighty. He prayed long and fervently in the quiet of his bedchamber, except when he didn’t have company, but no sign appeared. Possibly, thought the evangelist, some big demonstration might attract the attention of Jesus; something bigger than the revivals he had been staging. Then one day somebody brought him a copy of *The Warning* and upon reading it he got an idea. If the Lord would only send him a nigger for his congregation to lynch! That would, indeed, be marked evidence of the power of Rev. Alex McPhule.
He prayed with increased fervency but no African put in an appearance. Two nights later as he sat before his altar, his “Bible” clutched in his hands, a bat flew in the window. It rapidly circled the room and flew out again. Rev. McPhule could feel the wind from its wings. He stood erect with a wild look in his watery blue eyes and screamed, “A sign! A sign! Oh, Glory be! The Lord has answered my prayer! Oh, thank you, God! A sign! A sign!” Then he grew dizzy, his eyes dimmed and he fell twitching across the altar, unconscious.
Next day he went around Happy Hill telling of his experience of the night before. An angel of the Lord, he told the gaping villagers, had flown through the window, alighted on his “Bible” and, kissing him on his forehead, had declared that the Lord would answer his prayer and send a sign. As proof of his tale, Rev. McPhule exhibited a red spot on his forehead which he had received when his head struck the marble altar top but which he claimed marked the place where the messenger of the Lord had kissed him.
The simple folk of Happy Hill were, with few exceptions, convinced that the Rev. McPhule stood in well with the celestial authorities. Nervous and expectant they talked of nothing but the Sign. They were on edge for the great revival scheduled for Election Day at which time they fervently hoped the Lord would make good.
At last the great day had arrived.

===Chinese===

他知道，要做到这一步，非得有一道上天的神谕不可。唯有那个才镇得住他们。
他在布道会上开始放风，说将有天兆从天而降，好折服一切心存疑窦的和不信的，比如卫理公会和浸信会的教徒。他的羊群很快盼得神经都绷紧了，可是主不知怎的，迟迟不应允他这位左膀右臂的祷告。
麦克菲尔牧师纳闷起来：自己到底哪里冒犯了全能的上帝？他在卧房的清静里长久而热切地祷告——没客人的时候——可天兆迟迟不至。这位布道者心想，也许得来一场大动静，比他办的这些布道会更大的动静，才能引得耶稣垂目。后来有一天，有人给他捎来一份《警告报》（*The Warning*），他一读，主意来了。主啊，你要是肯赏我一个黑鬼，好叫我的会众吊死他，那该多好！那才真真是麦克菲尔牧师法力无边的铁证。
他祷告得愈发虔诚，可非洲后裔一个也没露面。两天后的夜里，他正坐在祭坛前，双手捧着他的“圣经”，一只蝙蝠从窗口飞了进来，绕着屋子疾飞一圈，又飞了出去。麦克菲尔牧师感觉得到它翅膀扇起的风。他直挺挺站起来，水汪汪的蓝眼睛里透着狂气，尖声喊道：“天兆！天兆！哦，荣耀归主！主应了我的祷告！哦，感谢上帝！天兆！天兆！”喊完他天旋地转，眼前发黑，抽搐着栽倒在祭坛上，人事不省。
第二天，他在快活山四处讲起头天夜里的际遇。他告诉张着嘴发愣的乡亲：主的使者从窗口飞进屋来，落在他的“圣经”上，亲吻他的额头，宣告主必应允他的祷告，降下天兆。为证实此事，麦克菲尔牧师亮出额头上一块红斑——那是他的脑袋磕在大理石坛面上磕出来的，他却说那是主的使者吻过的地方。
快活山的淳朴百姓，几乎没有例外，都认定麦克菲尔牧师在天上的衙门里说得上话。他们又紧张又巴望，开口闭口全是天兆。人人绷着劲儿等定在大选日的那场大布道会，热切指望主到时候兑现。
伟大的日子终于到了。

===Original===

From far and near came the good people of the countryside on horseback, in farm wagons and battered mud-caked flivvers. Many paused to cast their ballots for Givens and Snobbcraft, not having heard of the developments of the past twenty-four hours, but the bulk of the folk repaired immediately to the sacred grove where the preaching would take place.
Rev. Alex McPhule gloated inwardly at the many concentric circles of upturned faces. They were eager, he saw, to drink in his words of wisdom and be elevated. He noted with satisfaction that there were many strange people in the congregation. It showed that his power was growing. He glanced up apprehensively at the blue heavens. Would the Sign come? Would the Lord answer his prayers? He muttered another prayer and then proceeded to business.
He was an impressive figure today. He had draped himself in a long, white robe with a great red cross on the left breast and he looked not unlike one of the Prophets of old. He walked back and forth in the little circle surrounded by close-packed humanity, bending backward and forward, swinging his arms, shaking his head and rolling his eyes while he retold for the fiftieth time the story of the angel’s visit. The man was a natural actor and his voice had that sepulchral tone universally associated with Men of God, court criers and Independence Day orators. In the first row squatted the Happy Hill True Faith Choir of eight young women with grizzled old man Yawbrew, the tub-thumper, among them. They groaned, amened and Yes-Lorded at irregular intervals.
Then, having concluded his story, the evangelist launched into song in a harsh, nasal voice:

> I done come to Happy Hill to save you from Sin,

Salvation’s door is open and you’d better come in,

Oh, Glory Hallelujah! you’d better come in.

Jesus Christ has called me to save this white race,

And with His Help I’ll save you from awful disgrace.

Oh, Glory Hallelujah! We must save this race.

===Chinese===

四乡的善男信女，骑马的骑马，坐农用大车的坐大车，还有开着一辆辆泥壳斑驳的破福特的，从四面八方涌来。不少人没听说过去这二十四小时里的变故，还停下来给吉文斯和斯诺布克拉夫特投上一票，可大多数人径直涌向那片要讲道的圣林。
亚历克斯·麦克菲尔牧师望着一圈套一圈仰起来的脸，心里暗自得意。他看得出，他们正渴着要痛饮他的智慧之言，要被提举超升。他满意地注意到，会众里有不少生面孔，这表明他的法力正在见长。他忐忑地抬眼望了望湛蓝的天。天兆会来吗？主会应他的祷告吗？他又默祷了一遍，随即开始办正事。
他今天好不威风。他裹着一袭白色长袍，左胸口一个大红十字，瞧着竟像古时的一位先知。他在被人挤得密不透风的小圆场里走来走去，前俯后仰，抡着胳膊，摇着脑袋，眼珠乱转，把天使降临的故事第五十遍讲了一遍。此人是天生的戏子，嗓音里自带上帝的仆人、法庭传令官和独立日演说家通用的那种坟头腔。头一排蹲着快活山真信仰唱诗班——八位年轻姑娘，敲木盆的雅布罗老头灰白着脑袋夹在里头。她们隔三差五地呻吟一声、应一声“阿门”、喊一声“是的，主啊”。
故事讲完，布道者扯开又嘶又哑的鼻音唱起来：

> 我来到快活山，救你们脱离罪愆，

> 救恩之门大开，你们最好快进来，

> 哦，荣耀哈利路亚！你们最好快进来。

> 耶稣基督召我来，拯救这白色人种，

> 靠他帮扶，我必救你们脱离奇耻大辱，

> 哦，荣耀哈利路亚！我们必须救这个人种。

===Original===

Old man Yawbrew beat on his tub while the sisters swayed and accompanied their pastor. The congregation joined in.
Suddenly Rev. McPhule stopped, glared at the rows of strained, upturned faces and extending his long arms to the sun, he shouted:
“It’ll come I tell yuh. Yes Lord, the sign will come﻿—ugh. I know that my Lord liveth and the sign will come﻿—ugh. If﻿—ugh﻿—you just have faith﻿—ugh. Oh, Jesus﻿—ugh. Brothers and Sisters﻿—ugh. Just have faith﻿—ugh﻿—and the Lord﻿—ugh﻿—will answer your prayers.﻿ ﻿… Oh, Christ﻿—ugh. Oh, Little Jesus﻿—ugh.﻿ ﻿… Oh, God﻿—ugh﻿—answer our prayers.﻿ ﻿… Save us﻿—ugh. Send us the Sign.﻿ ﻿…”
The congregation shouted after him “Send us the Sign!” Then he again launched into a hymn composed on the spot:

> “He will send the Sign,

Oh, He will send the Sign

Loving Little Jesus Christ

He will send the Sign.”

===Chinese===

雅布罗老头敲着木盆，姊妹们摇来摆去，跟着她们的牧师唱和，会众齐声加入。
突然，麦克菲尔牧师收住话头，瞪着一排排紧绷绷仰起的脸，朝太阳伸出长长的双臂，喊道：
