“它会来的，我告诉你们。是的，主啊，天兆会来——呃。我知道我的主活着，天兆会来——呃。只要——呃——你们有信心——呃。哦，耶稣——呃。弟兄们，姊妹们——呃。只要有信心——呃——主——呃——就会应允你们的祷告。……哦，基督——呃。哦，小耶稣——呃。……哦，上帝——呃——应允我们的祷告吧。……拯救我们——呃。降下天兆给我们。……”
会众跟着他喊“降下天兆给我们！”接着他又即兴编起一首圣歌：

> “他必降下天兆，

> 哦，他必降下天兆，

> 慈爱的小耶稣基督，

> 他必降下天兆。”

===Original===

Over and over he sang the verse. The people joined him until the volume of sound was tremendous. Then with a piercing scream, Rev. McPhule fell on all fours and running among the people hugged one after the other, crying “Christ is Love!﻿ ﻿… He’ll send the Sign!﻿ ﻿… Oh, Jesus! send us the Sign!” The cries of the others mingled with his and there was a general kissing, embracing and rolling there in the green-walled grove under the midday sun.
---
As the sun approached its zenith, Mr. Arthur Snobbcraft and Dr. Samuel Buggerie, grotesque in their nondescript clothing and their blackened skins, trudged along the dusty road in what they hoped was the direction of a town. For three hours, now, they had been on the way, skirting isolated farmhouses and cabins, hoping to get to a place where they could catch a train. They had fiddled aimlessly around the wrecked plane for two or three hours before getting up courage enough to take to the highroad. Suddenly they both thrilled with pleasure somewhat dampened by apprehension as they espied from a rise in the road, a considerable collection of houses.
“There’s a town,” exclaimed Snobbcraft. “Now let’s get this damned stuff off our faces. There’s probably a telegraph office there.”
“Oh, don’t be crazy,” Buggerie pleaded. “If we take off this blacking we’re lost. The whole country has heard the news about us by this time, even in Mississippi. Let’s go right in as we are, pretending we’re niggers, and I’ll bet we’ll be treated all right. We won’t have to stay long. With our pictures all over the country, it would be suicidal to turn up here in one of these hotbeds of bigotry and ignorance.”
“Well, maybe you’re right,” Snobbcraft grudgingly admitted. He was eager to get the shoe polish off his skin. Both men had perspired freely during their hike and the sweat had mixed with the blacking much to their discomfort.
As they started toward the little settlement, they heard shouts and singing on their left.
“What’s that?” cried Dr. Buggerie, stopping to listen.
“Sounds like a camp meeting,” Snobbcraft replied. “Hope it is. We can be sure those folks will treat us right. One thing about these people down here they are real, sincere Christians.”
“I don’t think it will be wise to go where there’s any crowds,” warned the statistician. “You never can tell what a crowd will do.”
“Oh, shut up, and come on!” Snobbcraft snapped.

===Chinese===

这节词他唱了一遍又一遍，众人跟着唱，声浪惊天动地。随后麦克菲尔牧师一声刺耳的尖叫，手脚着地，在人堆里窜来窜去，挨个搂过去，嘴里喊着：“基督就是爱！……他要降下天兆！……哦，耶稣！降天兆给我们！”众人的喊声同他的搅作一团，正午的太阳底下，绿墙般的树林里，一场亲嘴搂抱打滚的群戏就此铺开。
---
日头快到头顶的时候，阿瑟·斯诺布克拉夫特先生和塞缪尔·巴格里博士，一身不伦不类的衣裳、一脸一身黑，正沿着尘土飞扬的大路跋涉，指望走的方向有个镇子。他们已经走了三个钟头，一路绕着孤零零的农户和小木屋，指望走到个能搭上火车的地方。在摔烂的飞机旁边干转悠了两三个钟头之后，他们才攒够勇气上了大路。忽然，两人从一道路基高处望见好大一片房舍，心头一喜，喜里又掺着惶恐。
“那儿有个镇子，”斯诺布克拉夫特喊道，“来，把这身该死的玩意儿从脸上洗掉。那儿八成有电报局。”
“哦，你疯了吧，”巴格里哀求道，“这层黑油一洗，咱们就完了。到这会儿，咱们的事全国都传遍了，密西西比也不例外。就这么进去，装成黑鬼，我敢打赌人家准好好待咱们。咱们又不用久待。咱们的照片贴遍了全国，跑到这种偏执和无知的窝子里顶着白脸露面，那是自己找死。”
“嗯，兴许你说得对。”斯诺布克拉夫特勉强认了。他巴不得把这层鞋油从皮肤上洗掉。两人一路走来汗如雨下，汗水混着黑油，说不出的难受。
他们刚朝那片小小的聚落走去，忽听得左边传来喊声和歌声。
“那是什么？”巴格里博士叫道，停下脚步细听。
“像是在开野营布道会，”斯诺布克拉夫特答道，“但愿是。这帮人保管会好好待咱们。这儿的乡下人有一样好处：个个是货真价实、真心实意的基督徒。”
“我看往人堆里凑不妥，”统计学家警告道，“人一多，什么事都保不齐。”
“哦，闭嘴，走吧！”斯诺布克拉夫特喝道。

===Original===

“I’ve listened to you long enough. If it hadn’t been for you we would never have had all of this trouble. Statistics! Bah!”
They struck off over the fields toward the sound of the singing. Soon they reached the edge of the ravine and looked down on the assemblage. At about the same time, some of the people facing in that direction saw them and started yelling “The Sign! Look! Niggers! Praise God! The Sign! Lynch ’em!” Others joined in the cry. Rev. McPhule turned loose a buxom sister and stood wide-eyed and erect. His prayers had come true! “Lynch ’em!” he roared.
“We’d better get out of here,” said Buggerie, quaking.
“Yes,” agreed Snobbcraft, as the assemblage started to move toward them.
Over fences, through bushes, across ditches sped the two men, puffing and wheezing at the unaccustomed exertion, while in hot pursuit came Rev. McPhule followed by his enthusiastic flock.
Slowly the mob gained on the two Virginia aristocrats. Dr. Buggerie stumbled and sprawled on the ground. A dozen men and women fell upon him while he yelled to the speeding Snobbcraft for help. The angular Snobbcraft kept on but Rev. McPhule and several others soon overtook him.
The two men were marched protesting to Happy Hill. The enthused villagers pinched them, pulled them, playfully punched and kicked them during their triumphant march. No one paid the slightest attention to their pleas. Too long had Happy Hill waited for a Negro to lynch. Could the good people hesitate now that the Lord had answered their prayers?
Buggerie wept and Snobbcraft offered large sums of money for their freedom. The money was taken and distributed but the two men were not liberated. They insisted that they were not Negroes but they were only cudgeled for their pains.
At last the gay procession arrived at the long-unused iron post in front of the general store and post office in Happy Hill. As soon as Mr. Snobbcraft saw the post he guessed its significance. Something must be done quickly.
“We’re not niggers,” he yelled to the mob. “Take off our clothes and look at us. See for yourself. My God! don’t lynch white men. We’re white the same as you are.”
“Yes, gentlemen,” bleated Dr. Buggerie, “we’re really white men. We just came from a masquerade ball over at Meridian and our plane wrecked. You can’t do a thing like this. We’re white men, I tell you.”
The crowd paused. Even Rev. McPhule seemed convinced.

===Chinese===

“你的话我听得够多了。要不是你，咱们哪来这许多麻烦。统计！呸！”
两人斜插过田野，朝歌声处走去。不一会儿便到了沟壑边，朝下望见那群会众。几乎同时，面朝这边的几个人瞧见了他们，嚷起来：“天兆！瞧！黑鬼！赞美上帝！天兆！吊死他们！”旁人跟着喊成一片。麦克菲尔牧师撒开一位丰腴的姊妹，瞪圆眼睛，直挺挺站起来。他的祷告应验了！“吊死他们！”他怒吼。
“咱们最好快离开这儿。”巴格里抖着说。
“嗯。”斯诺布克拉夫特应道，那群人已经朝他们移动过来。
翻篱笆、钻树丛、跨沟坎，两人一路狂奔，这份不习惯的营生累得他们直喘粗气；麦克菲尔牧师带着他热情高涨的羊群穷追不舍。
那群人慢慢追近这两位弗吉尼亚贵族。巴格里博士绊了一跤，摊开四肢摔在地上，十几个男女扑到他身上，他朝着撒腿飞奔的斯诺布克拉夫特高声呼救。瘦长的斯诺布克拉夫特兀自奔逃，可麦克菲尔牧师和几个很快就赶上了他。
两人一路抗议着被押往快活山。兴高采烈的村民们在凯旋途中又是掐又是拽，还嬉皮笑脸地捶他们踢他们。他们的苦苦哀求，谁也不睬。快活山等一个可以私刑处置的黑人，等得实在太久了。如今主既然应了祷告，善男信女们还能犹豫吗？
巴格里哭了，斯诺布克拉夫特许下大笔钱财换他们的自由。钱收下了，也分掉了，人却没放。两人一口咬定自己不是黑人，换来的只是一顿棍棒。
这支欢腾的队伍终于来到快活山杂货铺兼邮局前那根久违了的铁桩跟前。斯诺布克拉夫特先生一望见那根桩子，就猜到了它的用处。必须马上想辙。
“我们不是黑鬼，”他朝人群喊道，“扒了我们的衣裳瞧瞧。你们亲眼看看。我的上帝！别私刑弄死白人。我们跟你们一样，是白人。”
“是啊，诸位先生，”巴格里博士哭喊道，“我们真是白人。我们刚在默里迪恩参加化装舞会回来，飞机摔了。你们不能这么干。我跟你们说，我们是白人。”
人群迟疑了。连麦克菲尔牧师似乎也被说动了。

===Original===

Eager hands tore off the men’s garments and revealed their pale white skins underneath. Immediately apology took the place of hatred. The two men were taken over to the general store and permitted to wash off the shoe polish while the crowd, a little disappointed, stood around wondering what to do. They felt cheated. Somebody must be to blame for depriving them of their fun. They began to eye Rev. McPhule. He glanced around nervously.
Suddenly, in the midst of this growing tenseness, an ancient Ford drove up to the outskirts of the crowd and a young man jumped out waving a newspaper.
“Looky here!” he yelled. “They’ve found out th’ damned Demmycratic candidates is niggers. See here: Givens and Snobbcraft. Them’s their pictures. They pulled out in airplanes last night or th’ mobs wouldda lynched ’em.” Men, women and children crowded around the newcomer while he read the account of the flight of the Democratic standard bearers. They gazed at each other bewildered and hurled imprecations upon the heads of the vanished candidates.
Washed and refreshed, Mr. Arthur Snobbcraft and Dr. Samuel Buggerie, each puffing a five-cent cigar (the most expensive sold in the store) appeared again on the porch of the general store. They felt greatly relieved after their narrow escape.
“I told you they wouldn’t know who we were,” said Snobbcraft disdainfully but softly.
“Who are you folks, anyway?” asked Rev. McPhule, suddenly at their elbow. He was holding the newspaper in his hand. The crowd was watching breathlessly.
“Why﻿—why‑y I’m﻿—a﻿—er﻿—a that is﻿ ﻿…” spluttered Snobbcraft.
“Ain’t that your pichure?” thundered the evangelist, pointing to the likeness on the front page of the newspaper.
“Why no,” Snobbcraft lied, “but﻿—but it looks like me, doesn’t it?”
“You’re mighty right it does!” said Rev. McPhule, sternly, “and it *is* you, too!”
“No, no, no, that’s not me,” cried the president of the Anglo-Saxon Association.
“Yes it is,” roared McPhule, as the crowd closed in on the two hapless men. “It’s you and you’re a nigger, accordin’ to this here paper, an’ a newspaper wouldn’t lie.” Turning to his followers he commanded, “Take ’em. They’re niggers just as I thought. The Lord’s will be done. Idea of niggers runnin’ on th’ Demmycratic ticket!”
The crowd came closer. Buggerie protested that he was really white but it was of no avail.

===Chinese===

几双急切的手扒掉两人的衣裳，露出底下惨白的皮肤。仇恨登时换成了赔罪。两人被领到杂货铺，获准洗掉鞋油，人群略带失望地围在四周，不知如何是好。他们觉得被坑了。害他们空欢喜一场，总得有人顶罪。他们开始拿眼睛剜麦克菲尔牧师。牧师心虚地四下张望。
就在这越来越紧的当口，一辆老掉牙的福特突突突开到人群边上，一个小伙子跳下车，挥着一张报纸。
“瞧这儿！”他嚷道。“查明啦，那帮该死的民主党候选人是黑鬼。瞧：吉文斯和斯诺布克拉夫特。这就是他俩的相片。昨儿夜里他们要不坐飞机跑喽，早叫人吊死了。”男女老少把他围了个水泄不通，听他念民主党正副旗手出逃的新闻。众人面面相觑，对着那两位销声匿迹的候选人一通咒骂。
洗涮一新、神清气爽的阿瑟·斯诺布克拉夫特先生和塞缪尔·巴格里博士，一人叼着一支五分钱的雪茄（铺子里卖的顶贵的一种），重新在杂货铺廊子上露面。死里逃生，两人大大松了口气。
“我说什么来着，没人认得出咱们。”斯诺布克拉夫特低声说，口气里带着轻蔑。
“你们两位到底是什么人？”麦克菲尔牧师冷不丁出现在他们肘边，手里捏着那张报纸。人群屏息看着。
“呃——呃，我——是——呃——那个，就是说……”斯诺布克拉夫特结巴起来。
“这不就是你的相片吗？”布道者指着报纸头版的画像，声若洪钟。
“呃，不是，”斯诺布克拉夫特扯谎，“不过——不过长得是有点像我，是不是？”
“可不，像得很！”麦克菲尔牧师疾言厉色道，“而且这*就*是你！”
“不，不，不，那不是我，”盎格鲁-撒克逊协会主席喊道。
“就是你，”麦克菲尔吼道，人群朝这两个倒霉蛋围拢过来。“照这张报上说的，你就是个黑鬼，报纸可不撒谎。”他转身吩咐信徒：“拿下。跟我料的一样，他们就是黑鬼。主的旨意必得成全。黑鬼还想挂着民主党的名头竞选，成什么话！”
人群越逼越近。巴格里辩白自己真是白人，全然无用。

===Original===

The crowd had sufficient excuse for doing what they had wanted to do at first. They shook their fists in the two men’s faces, kicked them, tore off their nondescript garments, searched their pockets and found cards and papers proving their identity, and but for the calmness and presence of mind of the Rev. McPhule, the True Faith Christ Lovers would have torn the unfortunate men limb from limb. The evangelist restrained the more hotheaded individuals and insisted that the ceremonies proceed according to time-honored custom.
So the impetuous yielded to wiser counsel. The two men, vociferously protesting, were stripped naked, held down by husky and willing farm hands and their ears and genitals cut off with jack knives amid the fiendish cries of men and women. When this crude surgery was completed, some wag sewed their ears to their backs and they were released and told to run. Eagerly, in spite of their pain, both men tried to avail themselves of the opportunity. Anything was better than this. Staggering forward through an opening made in the crowd, they attempted to run down the dusty road, blood streaming down their bodies. They had only gone a few feet when, at a signal from the militant evangelist, a half-dozen revolvers cracked and the two Virginians pitched forward into the dust amid the uproarious laughter of the congregation.
The preliminaries ended, the two victims, not yet dead, were picked up, dragged to the stake and bound to it, back to back. Little boys and girls gaily gathered excelsior, scrap paper, twigs and small branches while their proud parents fetched logs, boxes, kerosene and the staves from a cider barrel. The fuel was piled up around the groaning men until only their heads were visible.
When all was in readiness, the people fell back and the Rev. McPhule, as master of ceremonies, ignited the pyre. As the flames shot upward, the dazed men, roused by the flames, strained vainly at the chains that held them. Buggerie found his voice and let out yelp after yelp as the flames licked at his fat flesh. The crowd whooped with glee and Rev. McPhule beamed with satisfaction. The flames rose higher and completely hid the victims from view. The fire crackled merrily and the intense heat drove the spectators back. The odor of cooking meat permeated the clear, country air and many a nostril was guiltily distended.

===Chinese===

人群这下有了充分的由头，正好去干他们起先就想干的事。他们冲两人挥拳头、踢打，撕掉他们那身不伦不类的衣裳，搜他们的口袋，搜出了证明身份的卡片和文书；若不是麦克菲尔牧师沉得住气、临事不乱，真信仰爱基督会的信徒们早把这两个倒霉鬼大卸八块了。布道者拦住性子更躁的几个，坚持仪式要按照祖传的老规矩按部就班。
于是莽撞的让位给了高明的。两人声嘶力竭地抗议着，被剥得精光，被几个健壮热心的雇农死死按住，在男女们魔鬼似的嚎叫声里，被折刀割掉了耳朵和阳物。这套粗刀手术做完，有个促狭鬼把两人的耳朵缝到了他们背上，然后放他们起身，叫他们跑。两人顾不得剧痛，都急着想抓住这个机会——什么都比这强。他们跌跌撞撞从人群闪开的口子里挤出去，想顺着尘土飞扬的大路逃命，鲜血顺着身子往下淌。才跑出几步，随着这位战斗牧师一声号令，六支左轮手枪一齐炸响，两个弗吉尼亚人在会众的哄堂大笑里一头栽进尘土。
序幕演罢，两个还没断气的受难者被拾起来，拖到铁桩前，背贴背捆住。小男孩小女孩欢天喜地地拾细刨花、废纸、细枝和小树枝，他们得意的爹娘则搬来木头、木箱、煤油，还有苹果酒桶上拆下来的桶板。柴火在那两个哼哼不已的人四周越堆越高，直到只露出脑袋。
一切齐备，人们退开，麦克菲尔牧师以司仪的身份点燃了柴堆。烈焰腾起，两个被火燎醒的懵懂人徒劳地挣拽着锁链。巴格里找回了嗓门，火焰舔上他肥厚的肉，他一声惨嚎接一声惨嚎。人群欢喜得又喊又跳，麦克菲尔牧师满面红光。火越烧越旺，把两个受难者整个吞没。柴火噼啪炸响，炙热逼得看客们连连后退。烤肉的香味在清朗的乡间空气里弥漫开来，多少鼻孔心虚地张大。

===Original===

The flames subsided to reveal a red-hot stake supporting two charred hulks.
There were in the assemblage two or three whitened Negroes, who, remembering what their race had suffered in the past, would fain have gone to the assistance of the two men but fear for their own lives restrained them. Even so they were looked at rather sharply by some of the Christ Lovers because they did not appear to be enjoying the spectacle as thoroughly as the rest. Noticing these questioning glances, the whitened Negroes began to yell and prod the burning bodies with sticks and cast stones at them. This exhibition restored them to favor and banished any suspicion that they might not be one-hundred-percent Americans.
When the roasting was over and the embers had cooled, the more adventurous members of Rev. McPhule’s flock rushed to the stake and groped in the two bodies for skeletal souvenirs such as forefingers, toes and teeth. Proudly their pastor looked on. This was the crowning of a life’s ambition. Tomorrow his name would be in every newspaper in the United States. God had indeed answered his prayers. He breathed again his thanks as he thrust his hand into his pocket and felt the soothing touch of the hundred-dollar bill he had extracted from Snobbcraft’s pocket. He was supremely happy.
And So On and So On
In the last days of the Goosie administration, the Surgeon-General of the United States, Dr. Junius Crookman, published a monograph on the differences in skin pigmentation of the real whites and those he had made white by the Black-No-More process. In it he declared, to the consternation of many Americans, that in practically every instance the new Caucasians were from two to three shades lighter than the old Caucasians, and that approximately one-sixth of the population were in the first group. The old Caucasians had never been really white but rather were a pale pink shading down to a sand color and a red. Even when an old Caucasian contracted vitiligo, he pointed out, the skin became much lighter.
To a society that had been taught to venerate whiteness for over three hundred years, this announcement was rather staggering. What was the world coming to, if the blacks were whiter than the whites? Many people in the upper class began to look askance at their very pale complexions.

===Chinese===

火焰渐渐熄下去，露出一根烧红的铁桩，撑着两具焦黑的躯壳。
人群里有两三个漂白了皮肤的黑人，想起本族往昔吃过的苦头，本想上去帮那两人一把，可顾惜自家性命，忍住了。饶是这样，有些爱基督的信徒还是狠狠地打量他们，因为比起旁人来，他们瞧这场热闹的神情不够投入。这几个漂白黑人觉出这些疑问的眼光，便放声嚎叫起来，拿棍子戳烧着的尸身，朝尸身扔石头。这一番表演使他们重新讨得了欢心，把“他们不是百分之百美国人”的嫌疑洗刷得干干净净。
烧烤完毕，余烬凉透，麦克菲尔牧师羊群里胆大的几个冲到铁桩前，在两具尸身上摸索骨骼纪念品，比如食指、脚趾和牙齿。他们的牧师骄傲地看着。这是他毕生宏愿的加冕。明天，全美国每一家报纸都会登他的名字。上帝当真应了他的祷告。他把手插进口袋，摩挲着那张从斯诺布克拉夫特口袋里摸出来的一百美元钞票，再度感谢上苍，幸福到了顶点。

如此这般，循环往复

古西政府任期的最后日子里，美国医务总监朱尼厄斯·克鲁克曼博士发表了一部专著，论真正的白人与经“不再黑”法子变白的白人在皮肤色素上的差异。他在书里宣布——这话让多少美国人目瞪口呆——新造的高加索人几乎无一例外，都比老牌高加索人白上两到三个色度，而全国人口约有六分之一属于前一拨。老牌高加索人从来就算不上真正的白，只是一种浅淡的粉红，往下渐渐淡成沙色和红色。他指出，就连老牌高加索人害了白癜风，皮肤也会白得多。
对一个三百多年来被教着敬奉白色的社会来说，这道宣告委实炸耳。要是黑人比白人还白，这世界还要成什么世界？上流社会里许多人开始对自己过分苍白的面色侧目而视起来。

===Original===

If it were true that extreme whiteness was evidence of the possession of Negro blood, of having once been a member of a pariah class, then surely it were well not to be so white!
Dr. Crookman’s amazing brochure started the entire country to examining shades of skin color again. Sunday magazine supplements carried long articles on the subject from the pens of hack writers who knew nothing whatever of pigmentation. Pale people who did not have blue eyes began to be whispered about. The comic weeklies devoted special numbers to the question that was on everyone’s lips. Senator Bosh of Mississippi, about to run again for office, referred several times to it in the Congressional Record, his remarks interspersed with “Applauses.” A popular song, “Whiter Than White” was being whistled by the entire nation. Among the working classes, in the next few months, there grew up a certain prejudice against all fellow workers who were exceedingly pale.
The new Caucasians began to grow self-conscious and resent the curious gazes bestowed upon their lily-white countenances in all public places. They wrote indignant letters to the newspapers about the insults and discriminations to which they were increasingly becoming subjected. They protested vehemently against the effort on the part of employers to pay them less and on the part of the management of public institutions to segregate them. A delegation that waited upon President Goosie firmly denounced the social trend and called upon the government to do something about it. The Down-With-White-Prejudice-League was founded by one Karl von Beerde, whom some accused of being the same Doctor Beard who had, as a Negro, once headed the National Social Equality League. Offices were established in the Times Square district of New York and the mails were soon laden with releases attempting to prove that those of exceedingly pale skin were just as good as anybody else and should not, therefore, be oppressed. A Dr. Cutten Prodd wrote a book proving that all enduring gifts to society came from those races whose skin color was not exceedingly pale, pointing out that the Norwegians and other Nordic peoples had been in savagery when Egypt and Crete were at the height of their development. Prof.

===Chinese===

倘若果真白到了家就是黑人血统的凭据、就是曾经身为贱民一员的印记，那可就得留神了，千万别白得过了头！
克鲁克曼博士这本惊世骇俗的小册子，惹得全国重新检视起肤色的深浅来。星期天画报增刊上登起连篇累牍的文章，出自一帮对色素一窍不通的蹩脚写手的手笔。面色苍白而又没有蓝眼睛的人，开始被人窃窃私语。幽默周刊纷纷为这个全国人嘴边的话题出了专号。密西西比州的博什参议员眼看又要出来竞选，在《国会议事录》里好几处提起这事，发言当中点缀着阵阵“鼓掌”。一支流行歌曲《比白还白》被全国吹着口哨哼唱。此后几个月，劳工阶级里渐渐生出一种偏见，专拣肤色过分苍白的工友另眼相看。
新造的高加索人开始心里发毛，恼恨公共场所里投向他们雪白面庞的好奇目光。他们给报纸写去愤怒的信，控诉他们越来越常吃的侮辱和歧视。他们强烈抗议雇主力图少付他们工钱、公共机构的管理人力图把他们隔离起来的做法。一个进见古西总统的代表团严词痛斥了这股社会风气，呼吁政府出面管一管。“打倒白肤色偏见联盟”（Down-With-White-Prejudice-League）由一个叫卡尔·冯·比尔徳的人创立——有人说，此人就是当年以黑人身份执掌全国社会平等联盟的那位比尔德博士。联盟在纽约时报广场一带设了办事处，邮件很快塞满了通稿，力证肤色过分苍白的人同谁都一样好，因此不该受压迫。一位卡滕·普罗德博士著书证明，社会一切传世的贡献都出自那些肤色并非过分苍白的种族，并指出，埃及和克里特文明登峰造极之时，挪威人和别的诺迪克民族还茹毛饮血呢。

===Original===

Handen Moutthe, the eminent anthropologist (who was well known for his popular work on *The Sex Life of Left-Handed Morons Among the Ainus*) announced that as a result of his long research among the palest citizens, he was convinced they were mentally inferior and that their children should be segregated from the others in school. Professor Moutthe’s findings were considered authoritative because he had spent three entire weeks of hard work assembling his data. Four state legislatures immediately began to consider bills calling for separate schools for pale children.
Those of the upper class began to look around for ways to get darker. It became the fashion for them to spend hours at the seashore basking naked in the sunshine and then to dash back, heavily bronzed, to their homes, and, preening themselves in their dusky skins, lord it over their paler, and thus less fortunate, associates. Beauty shops began to sell face powders named *Poudre Nègre*, *Poudre le Egyptienne* and *L’Afrique*.
Mrs. Sari Blandine (formerly Mme. Sisseretta Blandish of Harlem), who had been working on a steam table in a Broadway Automat, saw her opportunity and began to study skin stains. She stayed away from work one week to read up on the subject at the Public Library and came back to find a recent arrival from Czechoslovakia holding down her job.
Mrs. Blandine, however, was not downhearted. She had the information and in three or four weeks time she had a skin stain that would impart a long-wearing light-brown tinge to the pigment. It worked successfully on her young daughter; so successfully, in fact, that the damsel received a proposal of marriage from a young millionaire within a month after applying it.
Free applications were given to all of the young women of the neighborhood. Mrs. Blandine’s stain became most popular and her fame grew in her locality. She opened a shop in her front room and soon had it crowded from morning till night. The concoction was patented as Blandine’s Egyptienne Stain.
By the time President-Elect Hornbill was inaugurated, her Egyptienne Stain Shoppes dotted the country and she had won three suits for infringement of patent. Everybody that was anybody had a stained skin. A girl without one was avoided by the young men; a young man without one was at a decided disadvantage, economically and socially. A white face became startlingly rare.

===Chinese===

汉登·穆特教授，那位著名的人类学家（他那部论述《阿伊努人中左撇子低能儿性生活》的通俗著作家喻户晓）宣布说，根据他对最苍白公民的长期研究，他确信这些人智力低人一等，他们的孩子在学校里应当同别的孩子隔开。穆特教授的研究结论被奉为权威，因为他足足苦干了三个星期来汇集数据。四个州的议会当即着手审议为苍白儿童另设学校的法案。
上流社会开始四处寻摸变黑的路数。时新的玩法是到海滨花上几个钟头赤身晒太阳，再带着一身晒得浓浓的棕褐色赶回家来，对着自己黝黑的皮肤顾影自怜，在那些较苍白、因而也较不幸的同类跟前摆谱。美容铺子开始卖起名叫“黑粉”（*Poudre Nègre*）、“埃及粉”（*Poudre le Egyptienne*）和“非洲粉”（*L'Afrique*）的香粉。
萨里·布兰丁太太（原哈莱姆的西瑟雷塔·布兰迪什夫人）先前在百老汇一家自助食堂的餐台帮工，瞧出机会来了，便着手钻研皮肤染色剂。她告了一星期假，泡在公共图书馆里啃这门学问，回来一看，一个刚打捷克斯洛伐克来的新人占住了她的位子。
不过布兰丁太太并不气馁。学问已在她手里，不出三四个星期，她就调出了一种能让皮肤染上经久不褪的浅棕色的染剂。这染剂在她年幼的女儿身上试验成功；成功到什么地步呢——小丫头涂上不满一个月，就有一位年轻的百万富翁向她求婚。
左邻右舍的年轻姑娘人人获赠免费试用。布兰丁太太的染剂大行其道，名声在她那一方越叫越响。她在自家前屋开了间铺子，很快从清早到深夜宾客盈门。这配方注册了专利，取名“布兰丁埃及染肤剂”。
到当选总统霍恩比尔就职的时候，她的“埃及染肤连锁店”已星罗棋布遍布全国，她还赢了三场专利侵权官司。但凡有头有脸的，人人皮肤都染过。姑娘家要是没染，小伙子躲着走；小伙子要是没染，经济上、社交上都矮人一头。一张白脸变得稀罕得吓人。

===Original===

America was definitely, enthusiastically mulatto-minded.
Imitations of Mrs. Blandine’s invention sprang up like weeds in a cemetery. In two years there were fifteen companies manufacturing different kinds of stains and artificial tans. At last, even the Zulu Tan became the vogue among the smart set and it was a common thing to see a sweet young miss stop before a show window and dab her face with charcoal. Enterprising resort keepers in Florida and California, intent on attracting the haute monde, hired naturally black bathing girls from Africa until the white women protested against the practice on the ground that it was a menace to family life.
One Sunday morning Surgeon-General Crookman, in looking over the rotogravure section of his favorite newspaper, saw a photograph of a happy crowd of Americans arrayed in the latest abbreviated bathing suits on the sands at Cannes. In the group he recognized Hank Johnson, Chuck Foster, Bunny Brown and his real Negro wife, former Imperial Grand Wizard and Mrs. Givens and Matthew and Helen Fisher. All of them, he noticed, were quite as dusky as little Matthew Crookman Fisher who played in a sandpile at their feet.
Dr. Crookman smiled wearily and passed the section to his wife.

===Chinese===

整个美国千真万确、满腔热忱地装了一脑袋黑白混血。
仿冒布兰丁太太发明的东西，像坟地里的野草一样疯长。两年之间，就有十五家公司在制造五花八门的染色剂和人造晒黑膏。到后来，连“祖鲁棕”（Zulu Tan）也在时髦圈里成了风尚，常见一位娇滴滴的小姐在橱窗跟前站定，拿炭往脸上扑。佛罗里达和加利福尼亚那些一心招徕上流社会的高明度假庄主，特地从非洲雇来天生黑肤的泳装女郎，直到白人妇女群起抗议，说这营生危及家庭生活。
一个星期天的早晨，医务总监克鲁克曼翻着他心爱报纸的画页增刊，看见一张照片：一群快活的美国人，穿着最新式的暴露泳装，陈列在戛纳的沙滩上。他在人群里认出了汉克·约翰逊、查克·福斯特、邦尼·布朗和他那真黑人的妻子、前帝国大巫师吉文斯和吉文斯太太，还有马修和海伦·费希尔。他注意到，他们个个都跟在他们脚边沙坑里玩土的小马修·克鲁克曼·费希尔一样黑。
克鲁克曼博士疲惫地笑了笑，把画页递给了妻子。
